# Switchboard architecture

Requirements live in [prd.md](prd.md). This document describes components, state and protocols. Implemented: the hub lifecycle, the core's store, settings, playbooks and [flow decisions](#core-decisions), the GitHub source, the Claude Code runner and classifier, the hub runtime, the report command, the Zoho Desk and Forum sources, the pool with picks, shown with unstarted work items in the Unstarted group, the [dock](#dock) with its [Settings window](#settings-window), and the [VS Code extension](#vs-code-extension) that serves each item window, the experimental [Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions) in the [terminal holder](#terminal-holder) (PRD R93–R95), which start only with `sessions.codeMode` on, and the [conversation](#conversation) of a Code Mode session in its item window's Conversation view, read, typed, with its status and artifacts on their own tabs (PRD R96–R100), with its replies read aloud by the [voice](#voice) (PRD R105), each session in its [persona](#personas)'s voice (PRD R106).

Session and triage mechanics come from Ground Control (`D:\git\ground-control`), where they are verified. M IDs below refer to its [mechanics.md](../../ground-control/docs/mechanics.md). Re-verify them in Switchboard's own mechanics.md before relying on a version-fragile one.

## Packages

The application icon is a patch panel with four thick socket rings and a bold bright cyan connection, on a rose-to-plum gradient tile that stays visible against a dark taskbar, with transparency outside its rounded tile. `extensions/switchboard/assets/icon.png` supplies the VS Code extension listing at 256 pixels. The dock executable, main window and Settings window use `apps/dock/src/Switchboard.Dock/Assets/dock.ico`, containing 16, 20, 24, 32, 40, 48, 64, 128 and 256 pixel versions of that artwork. Chrome's extension icon uses 16, 32, 48 and 128 pixel PNGs in `extensions/chrome/assets`, copied into its unpacked build.

History tabs use light and dark monochrome SVGs of the patch panel; the status bar uses the `switchboard-logo` product icon from `assets/switchboard.woff`. Their vector source is `extensions/switchboard/assets/mark.svg`. Run `python tools/build-icon-font.py` with `fonttools` installed to regenerate the font, the History SVGs and a simplified colour vector variant `apps/dock/src/Switchboard.Dock/Assets/dock.svg`. For future raster exports, use `extensions/switchboard/assets/icon.png`: resize it with bicubic interpolation and preserve alpha for each listed Chrome PNG size and each PNG frame in the dock ICO. The vector generator does not rewrite these raster files.

npm workspaces, TypeScript strict, Node ≥ 22.13, Vitest, esbuild and vsce, following Ground Control's layout. Only `extensions/switchboard` imports `vscode`. Implemented so far: `packages/core`, `packages/conversation`, `packages/voice`, `packages/github`, `packages/zoho`, `packages/forum`, `packages/claude`, `packages/codex`, `packages/web`, `packages/hub`, `apps/hub`, `apps/dock`, `extensions/switchboard`, `extensions/chrome`, `extensions/firefox` and `extensions/gnome-shell`. `apps/dock` is C# on .NET 10 with Avalonia 12; its workspace scripts run `dotnet build` and `dotnet test`, so `npm run verify` covers it.

| Package | Contents | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/core` | Domain types, source and runner interfaces, store, playbook loader and renderer, flow rules | — |
| `packages/claude` | Claude Code runner, classifier, debrief fork and fork detection, and `CodeModeRunner` ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)) | core, the holder client |
| `packages/codex` | App-server client and runner, legacy exec runner, triage classifier, run records, hooks and hook trust ([Codex runner](#codex-runner)) | core |
| `packages/holder` | The terminal holder's host, its client, the attach client and the protocol ([Terminal holder](#terminal-holder)). The client entry imports no native module. The host's artifact is `holder.js` with the pinned node-pty's `lib` helpers and prebuilt binaries, installed per build in `<home>/holder/<build>/` | nothing in Switchboard; node-pty, `@xterm/headless`, `@xterm/addon-serialize` |
| `packages/conversation` | Shared conversation log, tags, rules and artifacts; the lettered-question parser, which its `questions` module, importing nothing, gives the view and the page; Claude's transcript, screen and guarded input mechanics ([Conversation](#conversation)) | core, `@xterm/headless` |
| `packages/voice` | The voice process ([Voice](#voice)): the voice target, speech through OpenAI's realtime API and playback through decibri, and the voice protocol and its client, from claude-collab's `tts.js` and `speech-order.js`. The main entry imports no native module. Its artifact is `voice.js` with decibri and its platform package, installed per build in `<home>/voice/<build>/` | core's `settings` module; decibri |
| `packages/github` | GitHub source: assigned state, triage context | core |
| `packages/zoho` | Zoho Desk source | core |
| `packages/forum` | Forum source over the forum database | core |
| `packages/web` | The web page's own front end and its Dark Modern theme copy, carried as strings for the hub to serve ([Web page](#web-page)) | core's `group` and `snooze` modules; conversation's `questions`, `providerIcons` and `voiceControls` modules |
| `packages/hub` | Polling scheduler, flow execution, loopback HTTP/SSE server, report endpoint, debrief scheduler, `HolderLink`, which finds or starts the terminal holder, the snapshot worker, window scopes, the conversation service, `VoiceLink` and `VoiceControl`, which supervise the voice process and route speech, and the web page's routes | core, sources, claude, conversation, web, the holder and voice clients |
| `apps/hub` | Hub entry point, the `switchboard report` CLI, `hub.js attach`, `hub.js holder` and `hub.js web`, bundled as one file | hub |
| `apps/dock` | The dock: a Windows and Linux app that lists the work beside VS Code, switches to each work item's window, and edits the settings ([Dock](#dock), [Settings window](#settings-window)) | the hub's dock stream and config routes |
| `extensions/switchboard` | Hub process lifecycle, claimed sessions and the dock's commands in each item window, worktree changes, ticket history, Open in Switchboard, the status bar item, the Conversation view ([VS Code extension](#vs-code-extension)) | core, hub client, conversation's `questions` module |
| `extensions/chrome` | Chrome extension that hides snoozed cards, shows each card's playbook and snoozes a card on GitHub project boards, links issues, pull requests and cards to their rows in VS Code, and keeps the dock's links in a Switchboard tab group, one tab per kind ([Browser extension](#browser-extension)) | core's `group` and `snooze` modules, hub browser endpoint |
| `extensions/firefox` | The Firefox build of `extensions/chrome`, from the same source ([Browser extension](#browser-extension)) | `extensions/chrome` |
| `extensions/gnome-shell` | GNOME Shell extension that lists and brings forward VS Code's windows and Ptyxis's, reports which Ptyxis window gains the focus, opens the dock's links so the browser comes forward, and reports full-screen windows to the dock on the session bus ([Dock](#dock)) | — |

The ad-hoc source is local and lives in `packages/hub`. State, the hub bundle, its record and its log live in the Switchboard home, `~/.switchboard`. Settings and playbooks live in the config folder, so the operator can keep them in a git repository. The config folder is the VS Code setting `switchboard.configDir` (machine scope): an absolute path, or one starting with `~` for the user profile. When the setting is empty, it is the Switchboard home. For a relative path, the extension shows an error and uses the Switchboard home. Each window reads the setting on every hub start, so all windows agree on one hub. When the setting changes, a window whose hub reads another folder stops it; every window then reconnects to one hub started on the new folder, and the keeper does not count that stop as a failure.

## Components

| Component | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| Hub | Separate Node process that runs the core: polls sources, creates work items, runs triage, starts setup and task sessions, enforces one session in a turn per ticket (PRD R17), serves the windows, the dock and the report command. |
| Source plugins | Supply tickets, decide whether each is in the assigned state, and build each ticket's triage context: GitHub, Zoho Desk, Forum, ad-hoc. |
| Playbook loader | Reads the playbooks in settings.json and renders their templates. |
| Classifier | Runs one-shot triage and returns structured output. |
| Runner | Starts, stops and tracks agent sessions for one provider, behind the `SessionRunner` interface in `packages/core/src/runner.ts`, which the hub runtime calls: Claude Code and Codex, and Claude Code's [Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions). |
| Worktree linker | Links worktrees to tickets from session reports, falling back to branch-name convention. |
| Store | Local SQLite database holding tickets, work items, triage results, sessions and worktrees. |
| VS Code extension | Starts the hub, opens claimed sessions in the item window's Claude view or Codex sidebar, runs the dock's commands, opens a worktree's changes, shows a ticket's history and the window's conversation, and shows the dock from its status bar item. |
| Conversation layer | Runs in the hub: follows each live Code Mode session's transcript and screen, keeps its conversation, and serves each window its own work item's ([Conversation](#conversation)). |
| Dock | Lists the work items beside VS Code, switches to each item window, runs the controls, and edits the settings. |
| Voice process | PRD R105: a Node.js process per Switchboard home, started by the hub while a window wants it, that decides which session the voice speaks for and speaks the replies the hub offers ([Voice](#voice)). |
| Terminal holder | PRD R94: a Node.js process per Switchboard home, started by the hub, that holds Code Mode sessions in pseudo-terminals, replays their screens to attached terminals, and lists and kills them ([Terminal holder](#terminal-holder)). |

## Data model

```
Ticket     id, source, sourceKey, ref, sourceId, title, url, assigned, pool, snoozed, snoozedUntil, snoozeComment, sortAt, scopeAt, lastPolledAt, repoRoot, repo, blockers, hostWindow
WorkItem   id, ticketId, playbook, status, stage, stageAt, taskPrompt, guidance, reportedPrompt, warning, error, unassignedAt, queuedAt, createdAt, statusAt, runStartedAt, readyAt, orderAt, scopeAt, doneAt, doneReason, approvalPrompt, approvalSummary, approvalReport, approvalHeld, parentId
Triage     id, ticketId, workItemId, context, playbook, immediacy, severity, model, startedAt, endedAt, error
Worktree   id, ticketId, path, branch, linkedBy (report | convention)
Link       id, ticketId, label, target, reportedAt, mentioned (found in the ticket's text by triage, PRD R61; cleared for good once a session reports it)
Paste      id, workItemId, label, file, reportedAt
Comment    id, workItemId, at, text
Message    id, sessionId, workItemId, at, origin, text, transcriptUuid
Reply      id, sessionId, workItemId, at, kind, answers, opening, source, replaces, rewrittenBy, withdrawn, transcriptUuid
Checkpoint sessionId, transcript, offset, lastUuid, lastAt
WindowBinding file, workItemId, since, scopeGen, selectedWorkItemId, selectedSessionId, ticketId, title
Session    id, workItemId, kind (setup | task), provider, nativeSessionId, shortId, cwd, state, stateAt, stateDetail, outcome, outcomeDetail, startedAt, endedAt, released, releasing, question, retiredIds, pendingFork, external, move, movedAt, promptless, personaId, personaVersion, personaPinned
```

- `Ticket.assigned` is the assigned state the last complete poll saw, and `Ticket.pool` whether it saw the ticket in the pool. `Ticket.ref` is the reference people use, set by the source when it builds the ticket: `#<n>` for GitHub and ad-hoc, `#<ticket number>` for Zoho, `T<topic id>` for Forum; core holds no per-source rule. `Ticket.sortAt` is the source's sort time, which orders the Unstarted group: Zoho's `customerResponseTime`, Forum's last public post, and for GitHub the later of the operator's latest assignment and the Status field's `updatedAt`. `Ticket.scopeAt` is when the ticket last entered the source's scope (PRD R53). Each polled ticket carries the source's `enteredAt`, or null, and core stores the source's time whenever it has one, because a source's scope can be wider than the assigned state and the pool: a GitHub issue on another status, or a Zoho ticket another engineer holds. Without one, the ticket keeps the time it has, or takes the poll's time when it has none and is assigned or in the pool. Ad-hoc tickets enter scope when created. A pool triage has a `ticketId` and no `workItemId` until a pick creates the work item and attaches it. `Ticket.repoRoot` is the repo an ad-hoc prompt chose; it is empty for other sources, which use their `repoRoot` setting.
- `Ticket.repo` is the ticket's repository, `owner/name` in lowercase, which a poll, a ticket report (R83) and a handled assignment (R85) set from the source's `PolledTicket.repo` or lookup; empty means the source's home repository, and a source without repositories leaves it empty ([Repo roots](#repo-roots)).
- `Ticket.sourceKey` is the key people use, such as a GitHub issue number or a Zoho ticket number. `Ticket.sourceId` is the source's internal ID when API calls need a different one, such as Zoho's internal ticket ID. A schedule's ticket holds its `scheduleText` there, and a task report's ad-hoc ticket `task` ([Flow](#flow)). `Ticket.adhocKey` is the ad-hoc key of a ticket a ticket report moved to a source (PRD R83), null otherwise: new ad-hoc keys count it (`Store.adhocKeys`), so it is not used again, `windowOf` names the item window by it, and rows and History label the moved work item by how it was made while it has no playbook. The moved work item is the ticket's first, because an ad-hoc ticket has one; the snapshot marks it `moved`, so a later work item of the ticket is not labelled.
- `Ticket.hostWindow` is the workspace file of the item window an ad hoc conversation was started from, its host, compared by `windowFileKey` (lower case, `/` separators); null for every other ticket ([Hosted conversations](#hosted-conversations)). `Session.promptless` marks a Code Mode session started with no prompt. `Session.personaId`, `personaVersion` and `personaPinned` are a Code Mode session's persona, the version of the tone section its prompt last got, and whether the operator pinned it ([Personas](#personas)). `WindowBinding` keeps, by `windowFileKey` of the window's file, its primary work item, the ticket and title it showed, its selected conversation and that conversation's current session, and `scopeGen` ([Window scope](#window-scope)). There is no `Session.rulesVersion`: every session is read as if it carries the current conversation rules.
- `Ticket.blockers` (PRD R90) is a JSON list of `{url, ref, title}`, one per open blocker, from the last complete poll that listed the ticket with `PolledTicket.blockers`. A poll entry without it keeps the stored list: Zoho Desk and Forum never set it, so theirs stays `[]`, and the GitHub source's own read of an issue missing from the search does not read blockers. `ref` is `#<n>` in the source's repository and `owner/repo#<n>` in another. Migration 37 adds it with `[]`. `queueWaits` gives a `blockers` wait while it is not empty, so the scheduler holds the queued work item until a poll empties it, and a setup session that ends while it is not empty queues the task as a snooze does; changes are logged, and History words them as `Blocker: #12`, `Blockers: …` or `No open blockers`.
- `Triage.context` is the clipped text the classifier received, with the current time. Keeping it shows why a playbook was chosen.
- `Triage.focus` is the URL of the page or comment the classifier chose (PRD R79), null on a failed or running triage and on triages from before migration 25, which adds it. The snapshot's `WorkItemView.focus` and `PoolView.focus` are core's `effectiveFocus`: the `focus` of the latest triage with a playbook, else the ticket's URL, so a running or failed triage keeps the earlier focus. A pool ticket's Triage again deletes its earlier triages, so it shows the ticket's URL until the new triage succeeds.
- `WorkItem.status` takes the values in PRD R34. `WorkItem.error` holds the cause shown for `failed` or `blocked`.
- `WorkItem.readyAt` is when the work item first became `awaiting-approval` or `finished`, set by the flow's `setStatus`; nothing else changes it, Retry included. A new work item, a returned ticket's included, starts with both null. `WorkItem.orderAt` is the operator's position in Ready for review (PRD R73), or null. Migration 20 adds both, turns `blocked` work items whose error is "The session finished its turn." or "The session ended." into `finished` with no error, and sets `readyAt` to `statusAt` on `awaiting-approval` and `finished` work items.
- `WorkItem.unassignedAt` records that the ticket left the assigned state while a session or the triage of a sessionless work item was running (PRD R10). `WorkItem.queuedAt` orders queued starts.
- `WorkItem.taskPrompt` is the rendered prompt the task session ran, or for a work item without a playbook, an ad-hoc or task one, its own prompt (`ownPrompt` in `group.ts`), which a `start` action's `prompt` replaces with its trimmed text when the two differ after trimming (PRD R77); a `start` with `prompt` on a work item with a playbook is refused.
- A `start` or `pick` action's `playbook` (PRD R35) replaces triage's choice when it is in `playbookChoices` in `flow.ts`: the task playbooks of that choice's namespace, none for a work item without a playbook or a scheduled run; any other is refused, and the event detail holds it. The snapshot sends the list as `WorkItemView.playbookChoices` for an `unstarted` work item and `PoolView.playbookChoices`, and Start with guidance…'s `input.choice` offers it when it has more than one.
- `WorkItem.guidance` is the trimmed text of Start with guidance… (PRD R77), set by a `start` or `pick` action's `guidance` and cleared on `session-started` of a task session, whose prompt has it appended, so a failed start keeps it for Retry; a plain Start sets it to null. It is not in the activity log's changes, because the action's event detail holds it. Migration 24 adds it.
- `WorkItem.doneReason` is `session`, `operator` or `unassigned`.
- `WorkItem.reportedPrompt` is the prompt of the `task` report that created the work item (PRD R68), or null. A repeated report matches an open child by it, because Start with prompt… can replace `taskPrompt`. It is not in the activity log's changes or History. Migration 35 adds it and copies `taskPrompt` into it for work items with a `parentId`.
- `WorkItem.parentId` is the work item whose session sent the `task` report that created this one (PRD R68), or null. Migration 15 adds it.
- Migration 16 rebuilds `workItems` with `AUTOINCREMENT` IDs and deletes the work items done as `unassigned` that never had a session and have no child work item (PRD R10). Migrations run with foreign keys off, because a table rebuild drops a parent table, and each fails unless `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` is empty before it commits.
- `WorkItem.warning` is set when the work item ends because its ticket left the assigned state with no done report (PRD R10, R11).
- `Session.state` is the observed state, `Session.stateDetail` the error kind of a `failed` turn, or for a `waiting` one `start-prompt` (`START_PROMPT`, PRD R41) or `permission-prompt` (`PERMISSION_PROMPT`, PRD R70), or for an `idle` one `question-card` (`QUESTION_CARD`, PRD R103), a turn that ended on a question card no message has answered, and `Session.outcome` the reported or decided outcome (PRD R36). `Session.released` records that the release stop was sent, so it is sent once (PRD R39), and `Session.releasing` that it has not finished: the session is not opened, and Stop and Delete still stop it. `Session.question` is a Codex session's reported question, `{id, text}` as JSON, which it waits on once its turn ends (PRD R70); the ID tells two reports of the same text apart for the roster read. Migrations 22 and 23 add them; neither is in the activity log, whose report event names the question.
- `Session.retiredIds` lists the native IDs of the copies a fork retired, stored comma-separated like `reports` and left out of the activity log, and `Session.pendingFork` the short ID of a fork waiting for the background process's release (PRD R78). Migration 26 adds both.
- `Session.external` marks a session the operator registered from a window Switchboard did not open (PRD R81); the roster read that ends the session clears it. Migration 27 adds it, left out of the activity log.
- `Session.move` is a Continue in Background under way (PRD R89), stored as JSON like `question`: the attempt's `id`, the tab process's `pid`, `phase` (`waiting` for that process to exit, or `launching` after `claude --bg --resume` ran), its start `at` and the launch's `deadline`. `Session.movedAt` is when the last move ended. The hub drops a roster read's result for a session whose `move` or `movedAt` changed while the read ran, as for a question reported during it. Migration 36 adds both, left out of the activity log, whose events name the move.
- `Session.rateLimit` (PRD R111), JSON that migration 59 adds, is the usage or rate limit the session's last turn stopped at ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)), null otherwise; it is in the snapshot's session view and left out of the activity log's changes, because the marker and limit events name it.
- `Session.codeMode` (PRD R93), a boolean migration 40 adds as false for every existing session, is in the snapshot's session view and left out of the activity log; the session-start event will name it. `Session.lastTree`, which migration 41 adds, is the process tree, `[{pid, created}]` as JSON, that the holder last listed for a Code Mode session, null for other sessions and before the first tree; a roster read stores a changed, non-empty tree, and a resume, or a Stop that ended the processes it lists, clears it. It is left out of the activity log and the snapshot. `Session.launchId` is the UUID of a Code Mode session's current process launch, start or resume, passed to the process as `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`; null for other sessions, and also left out of the log. The hook writer records `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH` in each marker as `launch`, only when set, and with it `startSource`: `clear`, `fork` or `resume` from the `SessionStart` that began the marker's session ID, kept in each later marker of that ID because the writer replaces the marker on every event, else null ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). Nothing sets `codeMode` yet. Planned: a Code Mode session's `nativeSessionId` is the UUID the hub passes with `--session-id`, and `shortId` its first 8 characters, because the roster gives an interactive session no short ID ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M67). Its `released` is set once the hub has ended its process, by Stop, Delete or the idle limit, and cleared when an attach resumes it. The start, stop and resume actions carry `codeMode`, as they carry `provider`.
- A session's `cwd` follows its playbook's `workdir`: the ticket's worktree when one exists, otherwise the source's repo root.
- `Paste` is a drafted text a session named for the operator to copy (PRD R60). It belongs to the work item, not the ticket, because a returned ticket's new work item drafts new texts; `(workItemId, label)` is unique, so a later paste with the same label replaces the file and keeps its place. Migration 14 adds the table.
- Migration 60 indexes `workItems.doneAt`, `workItems (status, doneAt)` and `tickets` by `snoozed`, `pool` and `(source, repo)`, and `Store.workItems` reads the snapshot's work items as a union of indexed reads, so a snapshot's cost does not grow with the done history: on a copy of the operator's store at 100 times its 545 work items, that read fell from 30 ms to 1 ms and `reposBySource` from 54 ms to 2 ms.

## Hub lifecycle

Implemented. Follows Ground Control's hub (GC R35, M25).

- **Start:** extension activation copies `dist/hub.js` to `~/.switchboard/hub.js` when it differs. It accepts a running hub only when the hub's `build`, a hash of its bundle, matches the bundle installed at that moment, and its `configDir` matches the window's config folder. It asks any other hub to stop and starts a new one only after the old one has gone; if the old hub refuses, the window reports it. Otherwise it starts one detached with VS Code's executable and `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, with `ELECTRON_*`, `VSCODE_*` and `NODE_OPTIONS` removed from its environment. Output goes to `~/.switchboard/hub.log`.
- **Record:** the hub listens on `127.0.0.1` on an ephemeral port and writes `~/.switchboard/hub.json` with `protocol`, `version`, `build`, `configDir`, `execPath` (its `process.execPath`: VS Code's executable when a window started it, Node.js when started by hand or by tests; `''` read from an older hub), `port`, `token`, `pid` and `startedAt`. A record without `configDir`, from an older hub, reads as an empty one, so a window replaces that hub. The hub holds a lock for its lifetime: a named pipe `\\.\pipe\switchboard-<hash of home>` on Windows, `~/.switchboard/hub.sock` elsewhere. Only the lock holder writes or removes the record, so concurrent starts leave one hub, and the OS releases the pipe when the hub dies. A hub that cannot take the lock waits up to 5 seconds for the holder's record and otherwise exits. A forced exit on Windows leaves the record behind, so clients accept it only when `GET /status` with the token returns the recorded pid.
- **Endpoints:** `GET /hub` returns identity without a token. `GET /status`, `GET /events`, `GET /dock/events`, `POST /window`, `POST /window/withdraw`, `POST /window/focus`, `POST /window/theme`, `POST /dock/show`, `POST /dock/click`, `POST /poll`, `POST /config/read`, `POST /config/check`, `POST /config/save`, `POST /config/delete`, `POST /shutdown`, `POST /report`, `POST /contract`, `POST /register`, `POST /background`, `POST /background/check`, `POST /ticket`, `POST /action`, `POST /open`, `POST /claim` `POST /volume`, `POST /history`, `POST /attach`, `POST /holder/list`, `POST /holder/stop`, `POST /open-url`, `POST /dock/select`, `POST /dock/search`, `POST /dock/new-conversation`, `POST /dock/focus`, `POST /open/withdraw`, `GET /v1/conversation/events`, `POST /v1/conversation/history`, `POST /v1/conversation/send`, `POST /v1/conversation/seen`, `POST /v1/conversation/statuses`, `POST /v1/conversation/select` and `POST /v1/conversation/new-conversation` require `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. `/events` sends `hello` with the window's key ([Window scope](#window-scope)), then a `state` event with the window's scoped snapshot when it opens and after every change, and `claim` when something is left for a window ([VS Code extension](#vs-code-extension)), and `/dock/events` a `dock` event with the [dock view](#dock) the same way, and `needs` when work items start needing the operator, plus `show` when `POST /dock/show` asks for the dock's list (409 when no dock stream is open) and `config` after the hub reloads the settings or playbooks. Each window opens `/events?workspace=<file>&focused=<0|1>`, with an empty `workspace` for a window that is not an item window, which adds `folder=<path>` when it is a folder window, opened on one plain folder ([Window scope](#window-scope)), and posts `POST /window/focus {window, workspace, focused, at}`, with `window` the key of its stream and `at` the time of the change, when it gains or loses focus. The hub tracks focus by window key, so two folder windows, which have no workspace file, are told apart; a report without a key, from an extension older than keys, names the newest stream of its workspace file. The hub adds `windows` to each dock message, `{open, front, count}`: the workspace files of connected item windows, the workspace file of the window that last gained focus and still has it, null while a folder window or none has it, how many windows are connected, and `folders`, the canonical folders of connected folder windows; it sends the dock view again when a window connects, disconnects or changes focus. A blur from a window that is not in front changes nothing; a report older than that window's latest report or than the last change applied is ignored; a focus report naming a key or workspace file no stream holds is ignored; and a reloaded window, whose new stream opens before the old one closes, is listed once and keeps its focus, which passes to a newer stream of the same workspace file or folder only while that stream's latest report is focused. The `/config/*` routes serve the [Settings window](#settings-window). `/action` takes an operator event (`adhoc`, `start` with `{workItemId, guidance?}`, `approve`, `mark-done`, `retry`, `stop`, `open-question` with `{workItemId, nativeSessionId}`, `move` with `{workItemId, after}`, where `after` is a work item ID or null, `reset-position` with `{workItemId}`) and answers 409 with the core's refusal; for `open-question` and `move` it keeps only those fields, and it refuses a `start` or `pick` whose `guidance` is neither a string nor null (PRD R77). `/history` takes `{window, ticketId, revision?}` (PRD R59) and answers 403 "Not in this window." for a ticket outside the window's scope. `/contract` takes `{nativeSessionId}` and answers `{contract}` for the latest session with that Claude session ID, or 404, for the contract hook ([Claude Code runner](#claude-code-runner)). `/register` takes `{nativeSessionId, title}` for the register command and answers `{key, contract}`, or 409 with the refusal ([Session reports](#session-reports)). `/attach` takes `{sessionId}`, the Switchboard session ID, for the [attach client](#attach-client) and answers `{port, token, session}` of the terminal holder, resuming an ended Code Mode session first, or 404 for an unknown session and 409 with any other refusal ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). `/holder/list` answers `{sessions}`, the held sessions read again, and `/holder/stop` takes `{id}` and answers `{stopped}` with the Switchboard session ID, or 404 for an ID that names no held session and 409 with any other refusal ([Visibility](#visibility-of-held-sessions)).
- **Snapshot worker.** The snapshot and the dock view are built once per burst of changes in a worker thread (`SnapshotBuilder` in `packages/hub/src/snapshotBuilder.ts`), because a build reads the store for hundreds of milliseconds, which on the main thread would hold back conversation events. The hub bundle runs itself as the worker (`isSnapshotWorker` in `apps/hub/src/main.ts`), which opens the store read-only (`Store.openReadOnly`) and builds in one read transaction, so a commit between two queries cannot mix two states, and the main thread posts it the runtime's inputs (`Runtime.snapshotInputs`: configuration, errors, held sessions, source states and the clock) as one message per build and publishes the snapshot and dock view it returns whole, so no view mixes two builds. One build runs at a time; changes during it make one more. Views read on the main thread between builds use the latest; a worker that fails or exits is logged once and replaced by builds on the main thread, which also settle the builds it had taken, as are builds of a hub with no bundle path, such as tests'. `buildSnapshot` in `packages/hub/src/snapshot.ts` is the projection both run, and `Runtime.snapshot()` still builds one synchronously for the hub's own reads.
- **Dock start** (PRD R71): `HubStarter` in the dock. Once the dock's `/dock/events` stream answers a record's token, it keeps that record's `execPath` and `configDir` in `~/.switchboard/dock-hub.json`, a file of its own so the UI's `dock.json` writes cannot race it; a record without them changes nothing. When `HubClient` finds no hub, by a missing record or a stream that fails or ends, it calls `HubStarter.Start`, which starts a hub only once none has answered for 5 seconds (`Grace`, from the first failed attempt, reset when a hub answers), so a VS Code window replacing the hub, as for a new config folder, wins the hub lock, and at most once per 30 seconds: with nothing kept it starts nothing; with a missing runtime or `hub.js` it names the path; otherwise it runs `<execPath> ~/.switchboard/hub.js --home=<home> --config=<configDir>` without `ELECTRON_*`, `VSCODE_*` and `NODE_OPTIONS` and with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, as `startHub` does. Elsewhere than Windows it runs through `/bin/sh`, which appends the output to `hub.log` and `exec`s the hub, so the hub keeps its log after the dock exits; on Windows it starts the runtime directly, unlogged, because `cmd.exe` would expand a `%NAME%` in a path. A started hub that has exited by the next attempt is reported with its exit code and the log's path, rather than as starting. The hub's lock keeps concurrent starts by the dock and a window to one hub. `HubClient.NotRunningText` holds what the dock says meanwhile.
- **Clients:** each window holds one `/events` stream, and the dock one `/dock/events` stream; either counts as a client. A dock that runs forwarded clicks opens its stream with `?click=1`; `POST /dock/click {rowKey}` sends a `click` event to the newest such stream only, so one dock runs it and an older dock never gets it, and answers 202 once sent (accepted, not completed) or 409 "The dock is not running." with none (PRD R65, R69, R71). The hub exits 30 minutes after its last stream closes, but not while any session is active or a debrief runs (PRD R43, R55). A stream that sends no response headers within 5 seconds counts as refused. When a stream ends, the window finds or starts a hub and reconnects. It gives up and shows an error after three consecutive failures; a connection that lasts under 10 seconds counts as a failure.
- **Browser endpoint:** for the browser extension, which cannot read `hub.json`, the hub also listens on the fixed port `127.0.0.1:47615` (`BROWSER_PORT` in core). It serves, without a token, `GET /snoozed`: `{"urls": [...]}`, the URLs of tickets snoozed now, from every source; and `GET /cards` (PRD R67): `{"cards": [{url, playbook, group, snoozed, links}]}`, one per ticket URL, built from the snapshot by `cardsOf` in `packages/hub/src/cards.ts`. A card covers each work item and pool ticket of a polled source with a URL that the dock lists, so an unsnoozed done work item only while Done lists it (from `doneSince`) and a snoozed ticket while Snoozed lists it, because a card for a row the dock does not show could not open it; `url` is the ticket's URL as stored, `playbook` the name the dock shows (`<namespace>:triage` during triage, null before triage chooses one), `group` the dock's group (`blocked`, `review`, `progress`, `unstarted` or `done`, from `groupOf`; a pool ticket is `unstarted`), `snoozed` the ticket's flag, and `links` the targets of the ticket's links (PRD R52, R69), from `WorkItemView.links` or `PoolView.links`. A ticket with several work items gives the pool entry, otherwise the open work item, otherwise the latest one. `/snoozed` stays, because the browser extension is loaded separately and can be older than the hub. It also takes `POST /snooze {url, until}`, where `until` is epoch milliseconds or null, finds the assigned or pool ticket whose URL matches, ignoring case, and sends the core's `snooze` event (PRD R66): 200 `{done: true}`, 404 when no ticket in scope has the URL, 409 with the core's refusal for a past time, 400 for another body or one over 16 KB. `POST /unsnooze {url}` finds the ticket the same way and sends the core's `restore` event, with the same answers. Both POST routes answer 403 to an `Origin` that does not start with `chrome-extension://` or `moz-extension://` and 415 to a content type other than `application/json`, before reading the body. A web page's JSON request needs a CORS preflight, which fails because the endpoint sends no CORS headers, and a `no-cors` request goes out as `text/plain`, so only an extension with host permission for the port can snooze or unsnooze; any such extension can, because an unpacked extension's ID depends on its folder ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M23). The routes send no CORS headers, so web pages cannot read them, and answers 403 to a `Host` other than `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, against DNS rebinding. A hub that cannot take the port, such as a second hub for another home, logs it and runs without the endpoint.
- **Entry point:** `hub.js [--home=<dir>] [--config=<dir>] [--idle-ms=<ms>] [--code-mode-idle-ms=<ms>] [--browser-port=<port>] [--page-port=<port>]` serves, reading settings and playbooks from `--config`, or from the Switchboard home without it; `--browser-port=0` serves no browser endpoint, and `--page-port=0` the [web page](#web-page) only on the hub's port, as in the spawn tests; `--code-mode-idle-ms`, a whole number above 0, replaces the 1-hour Code Mode idle limit for tests and live acceptance, and the hub logs it; `hub.js --stop` stops the running hub; `hub.js report …` sends a session report; `hub.js attach <session ID>` runs the [attach client](#attach-client); `hub.js web` prints a [web page](#web-page) link and `hub.js web revoke` signs every browser out.
- **Terminal holder** (PRD R94, R95, [Terminal holder](#terminal-holder)): `HolderLink` in `packages/hub/src/holder.ts` finds or starts the holder. The flow event `holder-lost` ends the sessions of a holder that is gone, and `held-idle` names a held session past the idle limit. The entry point's `hub.js holder list` and `hub.js holder stop <id>` list and stop held sessions ([Visibility](#visibility-of-held-sessions)).
- **Runtime:** the hub opens the store, loads settings and playbooks and reloads them on change, polls each enabled source every `pollIntervalSeconds`, then reads `git worktree list` in its repo root for the branch convention, sending `worktree-found` only for a ticket that has no worktree yet. Entries that git marks `prunable`, such as one whose directory was deleted, are left out of both the convention and report validation. While any session has started, it reads the roster every 30 seconds and hook markers every 5 seconds. Before a roster read or a release stop can end a setup session, it reads the convention again, so an unreported worktree still counts.
- **Startup:** the hub runs `<sessions.nodePath> --version` and shows a failure with the hook errors, because the hooks and session reports run that Node.js and a hook that cannot start fails silently. It checks once for each agent the hub starts, with the PATH that agent gets: the hub's, with that agent's absolute `sessions.claudePath` or `sessions.codexPath` directory first, and names the agent that lacks Node.js. A Claude background session started by an already running Claude service has that service's environment, which the check does not cover. A change to any of the three paths runs the check again. It installs each agent's hooks when `sessions.installHooks` is on, runs again any triage it abandoned, asks each session's runner about any session still `starting` (`reconcile`): Claude's fails it with "The hub stopped while the session was starting.", and Codex's completes, waits for or fails it from its run record, or answers `pending` when the process query fails, which the hub asks about again at each roster read; then it reads the roster (PRD R43).
- **Platforms:** Windows, and Linux under GNOME on Wayland, run the hub, the extension, the browser extension and the dock (PRD § Scope); the dock reserves a screen edge on both, and on Linux brings item windows forward through the GNOME Shell extension ([Dock](#dock)). On both, the hub runs under VS Code's own executable in Node mode, source commands run through Git Bash on Windows and `bash` elsewhere, and Claude's and Codex's hook scripts run with `node`. Paths compare without case or separator differences on every platform, as on Windows, so on Linux two paths that differ only in case count as one.
- **Home override:** when `SWITCHBOARD_HOME` is set in VS Code's environment, the extension passes it to the hub as `--home`. Both the variable and `--home` name the directory that holds `.switchboard`, in place of the user home, so the Switchboard home is `<override>/.switchboard`. The extension then ignores `switchboard.configDir`, so the config folder is `<override>/.switchboard` too. It does not change the user profile that Claude and `gh` see. Live acceptance depends on it.

## Rows

- The row rules, the groups and their order, each row's controls, click, time, priority and texts, live in `packages/hub/src/rows.ts`, which imports only types and browser-safe core modules, so the History webview bundles it as `@switchboard/hub/rows`; `dockView` in `dock.ts` turns them into the texts the [dock](#dock) draws.
- Groups follow PRD R27, in the order Ready for review (`review`), Blocked on you (`blocked`, only when it has rows), In progress (`progress`), Unstarted, Snoozed, Done (`dockView` orders them; `listGroups` builds them). `groupOf` in `packages/core/src/group.ts` checks Done, then Unstarted (`unstarted`, `triaging`, and `failed` at triage, `failedAtTriage`), then Ready for review (`readyAt` set, `queued`, questions, blocks and failures included), then Blocked on you (`blocksOnYou`: `blocked`, `failed`, or a question: a `waiting` active session, or a `setup`, `running` or `awaiting-approval` work item whose latest session ended with outcome `opened`, `waitsOnQuestion`), then Unstarted for `queued`, else In progress. Blocked on you, Ready for review, In progress and Unstarted start open; Done and Snoozed start closed. An empty Ready for review says "Nothing to review. Finished work appears here for you to check.", an empty In progress "Nothing in progress. Work appears here while its session runs.", an empty Unstarted "Nothing to start. New work appears here until its session starts." (`GROUP_EMPTY`); the dock shows this text in italics in the muted colour, aligned with a row's text, wrapped to the dock's width.
- `listGroups` orders Blocked on you by `clockOf`'s start, oldest wait first; Ready for review by `reviewOrder` in `group.ts`, `orderAt ?? readyAt` then ID, which the flow's `move` also uses; In progress by `clockOf`'s start, `runStartedAt`; each then by ID.
- `turnOf` in `group.ts` gives `operator` in Blocked on you and for a `blocksOnYou`, `awaiting-approval` or `finished` work item in Ready for review or In progress, `agent` for the other work items there, and null in Unstarted, Done and for a running hosted conversation whose promptless session is idle or ended. `needsYou` is an unsnoozed work item on the operator's turn; the status bar counts it and the notifications follow it. `stripeOf` gives an unsnoozed row on the operator's turn `error` (failed), `warning` (blocked or a question) or `turn` (any other Ready for review row), and null on the agent's turn and elsewhere.
- The main control (`mainControl`) is: Open in terminal on a row whose background session waits on a prompt before it starts or on a permission prompt (`waitsToStart`, `waitsOnPermission`), or whose Code Mode session waits on anything, which offers no Open in Claude (PRD R41, R70, R95); Open in Claude on a row whose active session waits in its background process on anything else (PRD R70), even when it is `awaiting-approval`; Start on an Unstarted row (`start` for a work item, `pick` for a pool ticket), or Start with prompt… on an ad-hoc or task one (PRD R77), Approve on an `awaiting-approval` row, Retry on a `failed` row with no active session, or Triage again on a pool row whose triage failed; a `blocked` or `finished` row has none, because a click opens its session, and neither does a row waiting on a question card (PRD R103), and neither does an In progress or Done row. A pool row has no main control while its triage runs. `controlsOf` lists every control that applies: Start, Start with guidance… (the same `start` action with an `input` that names the `guidance` field, PRD R77), Approve, Copy, Open in Claude, Stop on a session in its background process, Retry (`retry`) on a `failed` work item with no active session, Mark done, Snooze… and Unsnooze, Add comment… with its `input` (PRD R87), Open changes on a row with a worktree, Delete with its `confirm` question unless a session starts (PRD R76), and History; `poolControls` a pool row's, with Start with guidance… after a pick's Start. `dockView` adds `moveControls` to a Ready for review row (PRD R73): Move to top (`move` after null) and Move up (after the row two above, or null) except on the first row, Move down (after the next row) except on the last, and Reset position (`reset-position`) when `orderAt` is set. Hub controls carry the action the dock posts to `POST /action`; Copy, Open changes and History carry the message their handler reads.
- The row mark shows what differs within the group, with its meaning in the tooltip (PRD R27). Blocked on you: answer a question (`question`, for a waiting session, one whose turn ended on an unanswered question card on a `running` work item (`waitsOnCard`, PRD R103), or one Open in Claude stopped), approve a permission (`permission`, a shield, for an active session waiting with `PERMISSION_PROMPT`), blocked, failed. Ready for review, on the operator's turn: the Blocked on you marks first, then an approval report, as `send` (Approve to send) when the playbook has `approvalSends: true` and `review` (Review, then approve) otherwise, including a work item whose playbook is gone or an ad-hoc prompt, or `finished` (Review the finished turn, an eye); on the agent's turn: setting up, running (spins), queued. In progress: setting up, running (spins), Rate limited (`limited`, a watch) for a `setup` or `running` work item whose latest session waits on its usage limit ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)), or Idle (a circle outline) for a hosted conversation between turns; a rate-limited row stays on the agent's turn with no stripe. Done: finished by the session, marked done by the operator, left the assigned state. Unstarted: no mark, except Triaging (spins) on a work item or pool ticket being triaged, Failed on a work item that failed at triage, and Queued on a `queued` work item. `markOf` in `group.ts` decides it, `MARK_GLYPHS` gives each mark's codicon glyph, which the dock, the Switchboard pane and the browser extension's pills draw, and `MARK_ICONS` the same codicon's name, for the web page and the Conversation view, which draw codicons by class; a web test checks each name's glyph in `codicon.css` against `MARK_GLYPHS`. `SPINNING_MARKS` are the marks that spin. A queued row's line 3 follows the mark with one text per entry of `WorkItemView.waits`, which the hub fills from `queueWaits` in `packages/core/src/queue.ts`, the check the scheduler uses before it starts a queued work item: `slot` (held slots, from `heldSlots`, at `sessions.concurrency`), `session` (a live session of the ticket, this work item's own or another's), `snoozed`, and `limit` (`Starts after the usage limit resets, Fri 1:20 PM`) while a session of the provider the work item starts in waits on a usage limit with a reset time. A rate-limited row's line 3 starts with `limitTexts` in `rows.ts`: `Rate limited · resumes 1:20 PM`, the reset Claude Code waits for, or the hub's next attempt, or `retries 2:05 PM` after an attempt or without a reset time, with the limit's text and the hub's attempt in the title; a background session's `Rate limited · resets 1:20 PM`. A snoozed row keeps the mark of the group it returns to, and its line 3 shows the wake time (`Wakes Mon, Oct 12, 6:00 AM`) unless the snooze is indefinite, then `Ticket.snoozeComment` with its line breaks as spaces; with a comment the wake time is `Wakes Oct 12`, the whole time its title. The comment is cut at `CAPS.note`, with the whole text as its title (`wake` in `dock.ts`).
- The priority dot is shown only on a priority ticket, in every group, with the immediacy and severity in its tooltip (PRD R50).
- Line 2 holds the playbook, the key and a return icon and the count when the ticket has earlier work items that started a session with the same playbook or one its `returnOf` lists (PRD R51). Line 3 holds the mark, the wait reasons or wake time, the blockers (`blockerTexts`, PRD R90: in the warning tone on an open row that is not `queued`, whose `blockers` wait says `Starts when #12 closes` instead), any warning or error (clipped, in full in the tooltip), `From <key>` on a work item a `task` report created (PRD R68), and the stage and its age; with none of these, the mark's title, such as `Setting up` (`orMarkTitle`). The dock draws a muted `·` between details, like the ` · ` within one. While the active session waits on a question (the Answer a question mark), line 3 leaves out the stage (PRD R70). Otherwise, on an `awaiting-approval` row with a summary, the summary, its first letter capitalised and cut at its cap, and ` · ` with the age since `statusAt` replace the stage; the tooltip holds the whole summary and `Last stage: <stage>` when there is a stage. The triage context, task prompt and session history stay in the store; History shows the session history.
- Time in queue is a whole number of minutes, hours, days or weeks (`40m`, `5h`, `3d`, `2w`), from the start the group gives (PRD R27, R53), with the start in the tooltip. Blocked on you: `WorkItem.statusAt` for a block or failure, otherwise the waiting session's `stateAt`. Ready for review: `WorkItem.readyAt` (`Ready since`); line 3 keeps the approval summary's age since `statusAt` or the stage's age. In progress: `WorkItem.runStartedAt`, else `statusAt` (`Working since`). Unstarted and Snoozed: `Ticket.scopeAt`. Done: `WorkItem.scopeAt` to `doneAt`, never the ticket's, which moves when the ticket returns; the row shows the done time (`doneStamp`). Without a scope entry, the work item's creation; a pool ticket without `scopeAt` shows no time. Core sets `statusAt` on every status change and `runStartedAt` when a work item enters setup or running from another status, so a waiting session or a setup → running sequence stays one run and time spent queued is not counted; `done` copies the ticket's `scopeAt`, or the work item's creation, to the work item. Migration 6 estimates the times of existing work items by status: `doneAt` and `createdAt` for done ones, `queuedAt` for queued ones, the latest session's end for blocked, failed and approval ones, and its start for running ones.
- Stage staleness (PRD R58): `staleSince` marks an unsnoozed row whose work item holds a slot (`holdsSlot`: `setup` or `running`, not waiting on a question), in Ready for review or In progress, and whose active session is `running`, when `STALE_STAGE_MS` (30 minutes) or more has passed since the latest of `WorkItem.stageAt`, `WorkItem.runStartedAt` and the session's `stateAt`, which is when it last began running, since a `running` marker carries no detail. The stage age then takes the warning tone with the title `No stage report for <age since that time>`; a row with no stage shows `No stage report · <age>` instead. Core keeps a stage across a new session or a wait, so the session's `stateAt` keeps an older stage from marking a resumed run. The age updates on each view, which the hub sends at least every minute because of the `tick` timer.
- The Unstarted group holds `unstarted` and `triaging` work items, work items that failed at triage (`failedAtTriage` in `packages/core/src/group.ts`: `failed` with a latest triage whose `error` is set), which have the `failed` mark, no stripe and no operator's turn, `queued` work items without `readyAt` whose live session does not wait on the operator, and pool tickets with no open work item, of enabled sources only, sorted together by importance rank, counting an unrated one as rank 2, then oldest first by the ticket's sort time or the work item's creation; the snapshot's pool is in sort-time order. Migration 33 gives a failed work item of a polled source whose latest triage did not fail a failed triage with its error when its logged status last changed from `triaging`, or when it has no playbook or task prompt, so a triage that could not start before triage rows recorded it counts as failed at triage. A pool ticket shows Start only once its triage has a result, and the core refuses a pick without one. A failed pool triage shows its error with Triage again (`retriage-pool`). A triaging work item or pool ticket shows `<namespace>:triage` in the playbook slot, from the snapshot's `namespaces` (each source's `namespace` setting), or `triage` when the source has none; a triaging or queued work item has no main control.
- **Terminal until review** (PRD R71): the snapshot carries `openVSCode`, `dock.openVSCode` from settings.json. With `review`, `dockView` passes the mode to `controlsOf` and `mainControl`, which put `ANSWER_IN_TERMINAL` (`{type: 'click'}`, Answer in terminal) where Open in Claude or Codex, or a prompt before start's Open in terminal, would be, and the row's `open` comes from `reviewOpenOf` in `dock.ts` instead of `openOf`, following the PRD's rules in order. Two open types are added: `terminal`, a `DockTerminal` `{provider, sessionId, cwd, args, name, title, handoff, active}`, and `select`, which only selects the row; `null` keeps its `always` meaning. `args` are the CLI's: `attach <short ID>`, `--resume <session ID>` or Codex's `resume <thread>`, or `hub.js`'s `attach <Switchboard session ID>` for a Code Mode session ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). `name` is the item window's name, else the ticket key, then `-` and the session ID's first eight characters, which are also its short ID, so an attach and a later resume share a terminal and another session never takes it; `title` is `<ticket title> · sb:<name>`. `handoff` names the `open-question` stop a Codex question needs first. The row's `window` is unchanged, so the workspace file, History, Open changes and closing still use it. `row.terminal` holds the same payload for the menu's Open in terminal, which leaves `commands`. The snapshot also carries `openTerminal`, `dock.openTerminal`; with `always`, `app` sets `row.terminal` and drops the window's Open in terminal from `commands` as `review` does, and `auto` sets `row.terminal` and keeps that command, so the dock picks one when it runs (PRD R28). The hub's `dock` view adds `executable`, `sessions.claudePath` or `sessions.codexPath`, to every terminal, as `/claim` does for a VS Code terminal; for a Code Mode terminal, `sessions.nodePath`, with `args` starting with `<home>/hub.js`.
- Endpoint additions for the pool: `/action` also takes `pick` with `{ticketId, guidance?}` and `retriage-pool` with a `ticketId`.
- Snooze (PRD R49): the Snooze… control carries a `snooze` action with no wake time; the dock's `SnoozeDialog` offers the view's `snooze` choices, built by `snoozeChoices` in `packages/core/src/snooze.ts`, which the browser extension also uses (days at 6am local, Next week on the first Monday at 6am, In 2 weeks and In 4 weeks 14 and 28 days on, a choice with the same wake time as an earlier one left out, Indefinitely as null), and a date from the day of the earliest timed choice, which wakes at that choice's local time of day, so the dock does not repeat the 6am rule. It posts `{type: 'snooze', ticketId, until, comment}` to `/action`, where `until` is epoch milliseconds or null and `comment` is the trimmed comment, left out when blank. The hub refuses a non-numeric `until` or a non-string `comment` with 400 and the core refuses an `until` that has passed with 409. The core stores the trimmed comment, or null, in `Ticket.snoozeComment`, which every end of a snooze clears with `snoozed` and `snoozedUntil` (`UNSNOOZED` in `flow.ts`); the field is not logged, because the snooze event's detail holds `comment`. The browser extension's snooze sends no comment, so it clears one. The core ends every due snooze (`snoozedUntil <= now`) at the start of each event, before applying it, and the runtime sends a `tick` every `WAKE_MS` (60 s) so a wake needs no other event. A snoozed row offers Snooze… and Unsnooze, so a new wake time replaces the old one without waking the ticket first; Unsnooze ends a snooze early. A poll that finds a ticket neither assigned nor in the pool clears `snoozed` and `snoozedUntil`; migration 11 clears them on tickets already out of scope. Migration 7 renames `iceboxed` to `snoozed` and adds `snoozedUntil`; an iceboxed ticket becomes an indefinite snooze.
- Source toggles (PRD R3): `POST /sources {name, enabled}` writes the source's row in the store's `sourceStates` table (`Runtime.setSourceEnabled`) and rebuilds the sources and their polls; it does not touch settings.json. A source configured in settings.json with no row is off. Each load of a valid settings.json deletes the rows of sources it no longer lists (`Store.keepSourceStates`), so a source removed and added again starts off. A successful toggle sends `config` on the dock stream, so an open Settings window reads the source states again. A source not configured, or `adhoc`, is refused with 409. A disabled source is not polled and its pool is hidden; its work items stay.
- Refresh: `POST /poll` (`Runtime.pollNow`) polls each source that is on now and restarts its poll interval, so the next timed poll is a full interval later; a source with a read in flight is not read again. With no source built it answers 409 with each enabled source's build error, or `No source is polled. Turn one on in the header.` when none is on.

- **Pull requests** (PRD R92): a source with `pullRequests(urls)` (the GitHub source, one GraphQL query of up to 50 aliased `repository { pullRequest(number) { state isDraft reviewDecision commits(last: 1) { … statusCheckRollup { state } } } }` fields, `pullRequestStatus`) reads, after each of its successful polls, the pull request links of every ticket that has a work item not done, whatever the ticket's source, such as a Zoho ticket that links one, skipping one it last read as merged or closed (`Runtime.readPullRequests`). The hub keeps the results in memory by URL, `{state: open | draft | merged | closed, review: approved | changes-requested | pending | null, checks: passing | failing | running | null}`, passes them to the snapshot as `pullRequests`, and `linksOf` puts each on its pull request link as `pr`; a window's scoped snapshot carries only its own work items' (`WindowScopes.scoped`). A window coming to the front (`focusChanged` with a key) reads them again through `refreshPullRequests`, at most every 30 seconds (`PULL_REQUESTS_FOCUS_MS`), queued behind any read of the same source; a failed read is logged and keeps what was read. No webhooks.
## VS Code extension

The extension makes each VS Code window an item window's helper; its only view is the Switchboard pane, which shows that window's work items (PRD R71, R92).

- **Claims.** On `hello`, whenever the window gains focus, and on a `claim` event naming its workspace file (`claimIsFor` in `src/claims.ts`), or any `claim` event in a folder window, which has no workspace file and hears only those the hub sends its own streams, it posts `POST /claim` ([Open in VS Code](#claude-code-runner)) and runs the dock's commands for the window with `runCommand`: `openTabs` first, then it opens the claimed session, so a session in an editor tab is in front, then `changes` (Open changes), `history` (History), `log` (Show hub log) and `openUrl` (a Markdown report or file in the Markdown preview, another file in an editor, a URL in the browser). `openLink` checks that a Markdown file exists, then opens it with `vscode.openWith(uri, 'vscode.markdown.preview.editor', {preview: false})`, the Markdown extension's preview editor: one tab per file, which Toggle Preview (`markdown.togglePreview`) turns into the text editor in place, so editing the draft adds no second tab (mechanics M27). The hub logs each window command left, withdrawn and claimed. When `POST /window` or `POST /open` names a destination, the hub sends `claim {workspace}` on the `/events` streams of the window keys bound to it only (architecture.md § Window scope), an item window's file or a folder window's folder, because VS Code reports no focus change to a window the dock brings forward when it is already open (observed 2026-10-09: Open report's commands waited unclaimed until `code <workspace>` focused the window); a command for any window is not announced.
- **Item window tabs** (PRD R72): `ItemTabs` in `src/tabs.ts` holds this window's workspace file, null in a folder window, which opens nothing. `windowTabs(snapshot, file, windows)` in `packages/hub/src/dock.ts` lists the tabs of every work item whose `windowOf` file is that file: `focuses`, each work item's ID and `focus` (PRD R79), and `reports`, the `approvalReport` of each `awaiting-approval` work item, each once. Links are not tabs. `ItemTabs` keeps each work item's last focus and every report it has seen: the first `state` after activation only records them, and each later one opens a focus that differs from its work item's last one, each URL once, and a report it has not seen, so a later triage's new focus opens, a report whose work item leaves `awaiting-approval` and returns does not open again, and a work item that leaves the snapshot and returns with the same focus does not; `openTabs` waits up to 15 seconds for a state and opens each focus once, then the reports. `openTab` in `src/open.ts` opens a URL with `workbench.action.browser.open {url, reuseUrlFilter}`, VS Code's integrated browser, which reuses a browser tab already on that URL and opens it pinned, and fails with the URL when the command fails. The filter, from `reuseFilter` in `src/tabs.ts`, is the URL itself, except for a Zoho Desk ticket link, `/support/<portal>/ShowHomePage.do#Cases/dv/<id>`, which Zoho redirects to `/agent/<portal>/<department>/tickets/details/<id>`, so its filter is `/agent/*/*/tickets/details/<id>` on the same host. The Simple Browser is not a fallback, because it shows one page at a time. It opens a Markdown file as `openLink` does, in the Markdown preview editor, pinned, and another file with `showTextDocument` and `preview: false`. The command IDs come from VS Code 1.141.0's workbench bundle and are not a public API.
- **Closing done item windows** (PRD R76): `DoneWindow` in `src/doneWindow.ts` holds this window's workspace file, null in a folder window, which never closes. Each `state` passes it the work items `windowItems` (`packages/hub/src/dock.ts`, which `windowTabs` also uses) gives for that file, and the conversations it hosts (`hostedItems`, [Hosted conversations](#hosted-conversations)), so it closes only when its primary and every hosted conversation are done, also with `dock.openVSCode: review`. It records the IDs of the work items it saw not done, and holds done while every one is `done` with no session `starting`, `running` or `waiting` and one of them is a recorded ID, so a work item that moves to another window, by a session in another folder, does not close this one. With the snapshot's `openVSCode` `review` it also holds done once its work has left review: it records the IDs it saw `awaiting-approval` or `finished`, and holds while one of them is a recorded ID, none is `awaiting-approval` or `finished`, and no session is `starting` or released, no longer releasing and not ended, which is one resumed in a tab or a terminal (PRD R71, R76). It records when the work became done and when the window lost focus, and `closeAt` is the later of the two plus `switchboard.closeDoneWindowsAfterMinutes` (default 30, also for a value that is not a nonnegative number); focus or work that is no longer done clears it. The focus at activation and after `itemWindow` comes from `vscode.window.state.focused`, later focus only from focus changes. `closeIfDone` in `src/closeWindow.ts` runs after each `state`, on each focus change (`onDidChangeWindowState`), which it passes to `DoneWindow.focus`, and on a timer the extension sets for `closeAt` (at most 2^31 - 1 ms ahead, then set again), or each minute while `closeAt` has passed: when `switchboard.closeDoneWindows` (default true) is on, `closeAt` has passed, and no tab (text, notebook or custom editor) and no text document `isDirty`, `DoneWindow.close` returns true once and the extension runs `workbench.action.closeWindow`; a failed command shows `Switchboard: the window did not close: <error>`. For the integration suite, whose window is not an item window, the extension API's `itemWindow` gives the window a workspace file, and `receive` and `windowStateChanged` run the same handlers as a hub `state` and a focus change.
- **Open changes** (PRD R57): `src/git.ts` runs `git.openRepository` with the path, checks that the Git API's repository root is that path, because VS Code can answer with another repository (mechanics M21), awaits `repository.status()`, finds the merge base with `getMergeBase` (never `getBranchBase`, which writes to the git config), reads `diffBetween(base, 'HEAD')`, the index group, and the merge, working-tree and untracked groups, and passes the three lists to `changesPlan` in `src/changes.ts`. `changesPlan` follows each file through the stages to one row, sorts by path and caps at 400 rows, saying so in the title. A rename onto a deleted file's path takes that file's base when the renamed file has none, and otherwise the deletion keeps its own row. `_workbench.openMultiDiffEditor` opens the rows with `git:` URIs at the base and `file:` URIs on disk. VS Code reuses an open editor with the same source URI without updating its rows, so the extension first closes the tabs whose label starts with the ticket key, which outlasts a title change, and whose input has `textDiffs`, even when there are no changes now, then opens with the source URI `switchboard-changes:/<key>/<time>`. A conflict counts as a modification, including a modify/delete conflict, which leaves the modified file on disk.
- **History** (PRD R59): `src/historyPanel.ts` opens a `switchboard.history` webview tab per ticket, titled `<key> History`, or reveals the open one. The page (`src/webview/historyMain.ts`, `history.css`) posts `ready` when its script runs and `{type: 'shown', revision}` after rendering a history, and the extension then posts `{window, ticketId}` to the hub's `POST /history`, naming the window key of its `/events` hello, so the hub answers only for the window's own work item's ticket or one whose History command the window claimed ([Window scope](#window-scope)), which answers with `Store.ticketHistory`: the ticket, its work items newest first with their sessions, triages and pastes (no task or approval prompt, except `prompt`, the `ownPrompt` of a work item without a playbook, which the section shows under its heading), its pool triages, worktree and links, its activity events oldest first with their changes to this ticket's rows, `recordedSince` and `revision`; or 409 `Unknown ticket <id>.`, shown as a warning, which closes a tab that showed nothing. Later reads send the `revision` the tab shows, and the hub answers `{unchanged: true}` when it is current, without building the history. Every stream `state` makes a visible tab read again at most once a second and marks a hidden one stale, which reads when shown again. A read that fails shows its error on a page with nothing yet, in page wording, "Switchboard is not running. The history reloads when it starts." when no hub answered, and is read again from the hub whose stream sends `hello`, because the keeper names that hub as current only after the hello. The page stores the ticket, key and the operator's filters and open sections with `setState`, so `registerWebviewPanelSerializer` restores the tab after a reload (activation event `onWebviewPanel:switchboard.history`). The pure `historyView` (`src/webview/historyModel.ts`) turns the reply into a header and sections of entries: each event becomes one entry per work item whose rows it changed, worded from the event type and detail, with one line per other change; a work item's changes caused by another work item's event, such as a session the queue started, are worded from those changes alone. Waiting time is the union of logged needs-you statuses and logged `waiting` session states, and `renderHistory` (`historyRender.ts`) draws the chips, which are filters with no counts since a category's entries spread over several sections, filter, day headings, gaps and collapsed runs. Below 560 pixels the time column keeps its 62 pixels and only the gaps go. The page draws on each history or error, chip press, filter change and every 30 seconds with `morph` (`src/webview/morph.ts`), keyed by section, run, day and entry, so a redraw changes only what differs and the focused chip, header or link, the filter's caret and the scroll position stay put. `morph` removes gone keyed nodes before placing the rest, so a removal moves nothing, and puts back the focus and a text selection, in its direction, that a reorder's move dropped. A link posts `{type: 'open', action}`: `open` opens a session with [Open in VS Code](#claude-code-runner), offered only while the session is not running in its background process, or a Code Mode session's attach terminal in every state (**Code Mode sessions** below); `openFolder` opens a worktree with `vscode.openFolder`; `openUrl` opens a URL or file.
- **Switchboard pane** (PRD R92): `Pane` in `src/pane.ts` provides the webview views `switchboard.pane` (contributed to `explorer`) and `switchboard.paneScm` (to `scm`), with `retainContextWhenHidden`. Each view loads `pane.js` (`src/webview/paneMain.ts`) and `pane.css`, posts `ready`, and then renders every `{type: 'model', model}` the extension posts after each `state`, with `renderPane` in `src/webview/paneRender.ts`, patched in by `morph` with each work item's block keyed by its row key and its pills (by target), Report and main control keyed within it, so a focused button keeps its focus and stays the same control when a Report button appears or a block is added. `paneModel` in `src/paneModel.ts` builds the model from `dockView`: the rows whose `window.file` is this window's workspace file, each as its key, its ticket key (not a link when the ticket has no URL) and `links` as pills, the `Open report` command's file, and `main`, `mainGlyph` and `mainTitle`, with the row's `sourceKind` (the ticket pill's colour: GitHub's the foreground, Zoho `#089949`, Forum `#57AE57`), `playbook`, `mark` and `stripe` (the block's left edge), its line 3 split into `progress` (details with no tone: waits and the stage with its age) and `alerts` (warnings and errors, such as a failed setup, and the rate-limit wait with its reset time, `Rate limited · resumes …` or `Starts after the usage limit resets …`, which the dock draws untoned, in the warning colour), a pull request link's `pr` (`pullRequestTitle` names its state, review and checks in the pill's tooltip; review and checks show as `pass`, `request-changes` or `eye` and `check`, `error` or a spinning `loading` codicon), `status`, the first line of the latest status of this window's Conversation view, only on the work item it belongs to, and `unread`, the row's unread count, drawn after the playbook with the unread mark ([Unread](#unread)); and `voice`, the voice status bar item's icon, text and tooltip (`voiceStatus`), with Talk here (`{type: 'talk-here'}`, which runs the view's `talkHere`) while the voice is elsewhere. It shows nothing global: with no such row, "No work item in this window. Open one from the dock to see it here." and the voice only. The pane is redrawn after each `state` and each change of the Conversation view's state. A click posts `{type: 'open', target}`, run with `openTab`; `{type: 'main', key}`, which finds the row again in the latest snapshot and runs its main control: a hub control after its `confirm` (a modal warning) or `input` (`showInputBox` with the control's text, which refuses a blank one, setting the action's `field`) with `act`, which shows a refusal; Open in terminal (`mainTerminal`: the row's `terminal` window command, or the same command built from its `terminal` when `dock.openTerminal` gives the row none) posted to `POST /window` for this window's workspace file and claimed at once, so the hub adds the agent's CLI from `sessions.claudePath` or `sessions.codexPath`; or else the row's click with `openRow`, as for Open in Claude and Answer in terminal; A row no longer in the window runs nothing. The extension API's `pane` returns the model each resolved view last rendered, and `paneClick` runs a click, for the integration suite.
- **Side bar widths** (PRD R91): `SideBars` in `src/sideBars.ts` runs every `POLL_MS`, 250 ms, while `switchboard.rememberSideBarWidths` is on and `workbench.editor.centeredLayoutFixedWidth` is off. The pane's page posts `{type: 'size', inner, outer, dpr}` (`innerWidth`, which counts a scrollbar, `outerWidth` and `devicePixelRatio`) on a `ResizeObserver` change and every 250 ms; `Pane.size` returns the latest report of a view that is visible, received since it last became visible and within the last second, because a hidden view keeps reporting stale sizes (mechanics M61). While `outer * scale` is under `switchboard.sideBarWidthsMinWindow` (default 1200), a poll reads nothing, logs `Paused` once, and marks a resize with no base, so the side bars are set once the window is wider. `vscode.getEditorLayout` gives the editor area's width; a single group, which reports its height, is first laid out with `vscode.setEditorLayout {orientation: 0, groups: [{}]}`, which leaves the focus alone. The room for the side bars and the editor area is `outer * scale` less a fixed offset: 14 pixels of frame times `scale`, 16 of sashes, and 44 for the activity bar unless `workbench.activityBar.location` is `top`, `bottom` or `hidden` (mechanics M61); the secondary is the room less the pane's width and the editor's, and hidden under 100 pixels. `scale`, CSS pixels per `outer` pixel, is `1.2 ** -window.zoomLevel` until a resize the editor took whole, between two held readings above its minimum with the primary unchanged, measures it within 1% of a zoom level's `1.2 ** (n / 2)`, which it then takes, and an editor that narrowed or held as the window widened, as when a panel beside it closed, measures nothing; the window keeps the latest measured one in memory, for its `dpr` only, because a ratio seen again may come from another zoom and display scale. A pane found outside the primary side bar stops reading until the pane is hidden and shown again, also one wider than the room the editor leaves above its minimum once a resize has measured the scale. A change of `outer` marks a resize; once the readings hold two polls, the primary is stepped with `decreaseViewWidth` and `increaseViewWidth`, then, after the focus is noted, the secondary with the primary hidden (`closeSidebar`, `toggleSidebarVisibility`), each visible side bar more than half a step from `wantedWidth` of its points at the room, at least 170 pixels: the line between the points either side of the room, or outside them the nearest point's width times `sqrt(room / point.room)`; narrowing steps always run and widening ones only with the editor above its minimum, and one editor group is required; a primary not widened for the editor's minimum while the secondary narrowed is set again after the wait, from the room the secondary freed. A secondary whose reading grew 150 pixels or more between two polls while visible, as a panel beside the editor makes it, gets no steps until it reads that much narrower than after the jump, or hidden. The side bars are read again after four polls, and a resize meanwhile is set after them; after a restore that stepped only the primary, in a window that kept its width through the wait, a pane whose width did not follow while the editor's did is outside the primary side bar, and nothing is read until it is hidden and shown again. A scale the resize measured is used for that restore. With the window unchanged, a side bar that moved more than 8 pixels from the last widths and held four polls was dragged: it is kept as a point `{room, width, at}` with `withPoint`, which drops points within 200 pixels of room and points that would make the width fall as the room grows, and keeps the six newest, by room; a narrowing with the editor at its minimum is not kept, nor any change after a jump of 150 pixels between two polls, which only keeps a side bar shown without points. A hidden pane marks a resize and drops the base widths, so the side bars are set, in either direction, when it is visible again. Before a side bar is hidden, `increaseViewSize` shows what has the focus when one editor group is over a step above its minimum and the primary over a step above 170, and `decreaseViewSize` undoes it, also when no width moved, because a focused panel below the editor grows in height: the editor widens with the focus in the editor, narrows with it in a side bar's view, where a pane that grew within 300 ms names the primary, and holds with it in a webview; the test waits up to 300 ms for the pane to report the primary's steps first; `focusSideBar` or `focusAuxiliaryBar` puts it back, and nothing moves it when it was in the editor or was not tested. `SideBarIo.log` writes each kept width, restore (the widths before and after the steps and the wanted ones, or nothing to set), measured scale and stop to the `Switchboard Side Bars` log output channel, and all but a restore with nothing to set to `setStatusBarMessage` for five seconds. Points are `globalState` keys `sideBars.points.<side>`, shared by every window of the profile; Three seconds after activation, while `switchboard.rememberSideBarWidths` is on, a workspace without the `workspaceState` key `pane.expanded` gets it, and when neither pane view has resolved, `switchboard.pane.focus {preserveFocus: true}` expands the Explorer's, which opens the Explorer; a view that resolved, even if later collapsed, leaves the key set. `switchboard.forgetSideBarWidths` removes every `sideBars.` key and `pane.expanded`. The extension API's `paneSize`, `sideBarWidths`, `pollSideBars` and `expandPaneOnce` serve the integration suite.
- **A row's click from VS Code** (PRD R65, R69): `openRow` in `src/open.ts` builds the dock view from the window's latest snapshot with `dockView` and runs the row's click as the dock's `Switcher.Click` does: a row without a window opens the row's `open` URL, its focus (PRD R79); otherwise it writes the workspace file with the hub's `writeWorkspaceFile`, except for a hosted conversation's row, whose window is its host's, for which it posts `POST /dock/select {rowKey}` instead; for `question` it posts `open-question` and waits up to 20 seconds for a snapshot in which that session is `ended`; for `session` it posts `POST /open {sessionId, cwd, workspace}`. A `terminal` or `select` row is the dock's to run: it posts `POST /dock/click {rowKey}` and returns `dock` on 202, or shows the hub's error, such as "The dock is not running.", on 409. With `openVSCode: review` a `url` row opens the URL even when the row has a window.
