It then runs `vscode.openFolder` on the workspace file with `forceNewWindow`, which VS Code answers by bringing the window that has the file forward, or opening one (switcher spike Q4); VS Code holds the foreground after the operator's click on its notification or link, so Windows lets it, where it would refuse the dock (M26). The item window claims the session when it gains focus; a click whose item window is this window claims at once, because it does not gain focus again. The window is not maximized or moved to the dock's monitor. A second click on the row being opened is ignored, and a click on another row replaces it, which then makes no further request or switch, as in the dock. The extension API's `openRow` takes a stand-in hub record for the integration suite.
- **Notifications of work on the operator's turn** (PRD R65) are the dock's, because a window sees only its own work item ([Dock](#dock)).
- **Open in Switchboard** (PRD R69): the extension registers a `UriHandler` and activates `onUri`, so `vscode://switchboard.switchboard/open?url=<ticket URL>` reaches it. VS Code decodes the URI's query once; `openUrlOf` in `src/search.ts` takes everything after `url=` as the ticket URL, so a URL with its own `&` survives. The window cannot see other work items, so it posts `POST /open-url {url}`, and the hub answers the row: `targetForUrl` in `packages/hub/src/needs.ts` walks the rows `listGroups` lists, in list order, for the first whose ticket URL matches, then the first whose ticket has a matching link (`WorkItemView.links`, or `PoolView.links` on a pool row). URLs compare by `urlKey` in `packages/core/src/group.ts`: a GitHub issue or pull request URL, any tab such as `/files` included, as `github.com/<owner>/<repo>/<issues|pull>/<number>` in lower case, any other URL as parsed without a trailing slash. The answer is that one row of the dock view, with its terminal commands, the one exception to the [window scope rule](#window-scope), because the operator asked for it and the dock today only calls `SetForegroundWindow` on an open item window, which Windows refuses a process without the latest input (mechanics M26), so the click runs in VS Code; `openRow` runs its click. A row whose click stops a session for its question waits for a snapshot the window cannot see, so its click runs in the dock (`POST /dock/click`). With no row the hub answers 404 and the window shows `Switchboard: no work item has <url>.`, and a URI with another path or no `url=` gets `Switchboard: cannot open <uri>.` The extension API's `openUri` runs the same handler for the integration suite, whose portable VS Code does not register for `vscode://` links.
- **Status bar item** (PRD R64, R71): a left-aligned item, `$(switchboard-logo)` with the tooltip `Switchboard: click to show the dock.`; how many work items need the operator is the dock's to show, since a window sees only its own work item. Its command, `switchboard.showDock`, posts `POST /dock/show`, which activates the running dock; when the hub answers 409, because no dock stream is connected, or no hub answers, it starts `<Switchboard home>/dock/Switchboard.Dock.exe --home <home>` detached, without the `ELECTRON_*` and `VSCODE_*` variables, or warns that the dock is not installed there.
- **New Claude Session in Editor Tab** (PRD R75): the `switchboard.newClaudeTab` command (`newClaudeTab` in `src/open.ts`) calls `claude-vscode.editor.open` with no session and `{programmatic: 'pin-to-panel'}`, which opens a tab and writes no preference (mechanics M29); executing Claude's contributed command activates Claude's extension. Its `$(add)` button is in `editor/title`, when `!config.claudeCode.useTerminal`, and in `view/title` for `claudeVSCodeSidebar` and `claudeVSCodeSidebarSecondary`.
- **Continue in Background** (PRD R89): the `switchboard.continueInBackground` command (`continueInBackground` in `src/background.ts`), an `editor/title` button with `$(debug-step-out)` and a command palette entry, both when `activeWebviewPanelId == 'claudeVSCodePanel'`, and `switchboard.continueSidebarInBackground`, the same logic for the Claude sidebar (`continueInBackground(deps, 'sidebar')`), a `view/title` button for `claudeVSCodeSidebar` and `claudeVSCodeSidebarSecondary`, kept out of the command palette. The sidebar has no tab, so it posts a null label, refuses while any Claude tab is open, and on 200 runs only `workbench.action.closeWindow`, because hiding the sidebar leaves its session's process running (mechanics M55). It refuses, asking the hub nothing, when the active tab is not a Claude tab (a webview whose view type ends in `claudeVSCodePanel`), when the window has another Claude tab or a terminal named `Switchboard · …` (Open in terminal's), or when a tab or text document is dirty, the check `closeWindow.ts` makes. It posts `/background` with the tab's label, its extension host's `process.pid` and the IDs of `windowItems` for the window's workspace file, with a 35-second timeout. While the request runs it watches `tabGroups.onDidChangeTabs`, and when a Claude tab with the label closed meanwhile it closes nothing more, so a new tab with the same label stays. On 200 it asks `/background/check` whether the moving process is still the window's only Claude session, and closes nothing when another started meanwhile, such as in the sidebar, or the check fails; with the watch still on, it then checks the window again. Then, from a tab, closes that tab with `tabGroups.close`, which ends its process (mechanics M55), then runs `workbench.action.closeWindow`; a refusal, a changed window, or a tab close that throws or answers false shows a warning and closes nothing more, and the hub's 30-second wait then leaves the session in its tab.
- **Hub log.** Each window creates the `Switchboard Hub` output channel with the `log` language, so it is listed in the Output view, and follows `hub.log` with `LogTail` in `src/hubLog.ts`: at activation it writes the file's last 64 KB from the first whole line, then `watchFile` checks the file each second and writes the whole lines added since; a line still being written waits for its newline, and a file that shrank is read again from its start after a marker line. A plain channel keeps the hub's own timestamps, where a `LogOutputChannel` would add the time each line was read. `switchboard.showHubLog` (Switchboard: Show Hub Log) and the dock's `log` window command read the file and show the channel without taking focus.
- **Code Mode sessions** (PRD R95): the hub sends a Code Mode session's Open in terminal as a `terminal` command with `codeMode: true` whose `sessionId` is the Switchboard session ID, and turns a claimed open of a Code Mode session into such a command, never a Claude view open; `/claim` adds `executable`, `sessions.nodePath`, and `args`, `["<home>/hub.js", "attach", <Switchboard session ID>]`. `openTerminal` (`terminalCommandOf` reads the command) runs `executable` with `args` as `shellPath` and `shellArgs`, with no shell, in a terminal named `Switchboard · <key>` as for a background session, whose reuse key is `code:<Switchboard session ID>`, so it survives a Claude session ID change; it ignores `active` and never opens the Claude view, and refuses a command without `executable` or `args` with "The hub sent no attach command for Code Mode session <id>.". The attach client keeps running after its session ends, so `openTerminal` shows that terminal again while it has not exited. A window reload forgets which terminal is the session's, because VS Code restores a terminal without its `shellPath` and `shellArgs`, so the next Open in terminal opens a second attach client beside it. A claimed Code Mode open arrives as that `terminal` command, run after `openTabs` and before the other commands. History's start entry for a Code Mode session reads "Session <id> started in Code Mode", and its links, on the start and on each wait, say Open in terminal in every state: `sessionOpen` gives `{type: 'open', sessionId: <Switchboard session ID>, cwd, provider: 'claude', codeMode: true}`, which `openAttach` posts as `POST /open` with the window key; the hub answers the destination window, and the window claims the open at once when it is that window, else opens the destination's workspace file with `vscode.openFolder`, which brings that window forward to claim it; a refusal or no answer shows "Switchboard: <the hub's error>" or "Switchboard: the hub did not answer.". Every notification that a request to the hub failed goes through `warnHub` (`src/hubNotice.ts`): a warning with the hub's refusal, or "Switchboard: the hub did not answer." with a **Show Hub Log** button that runs Switchboard: Show Hub Log. Open in VS Code from History does the same for a background session. The pane's main control is Open in terminal where it would be Open in Claude, and `mainTerminal` builds a Code Mode command from the row's `terminal` when the row has none.
- **Conversation view** ([Conversation view](#conversation-view)): `ConversationView` in `src/conversation.ts`.
- The extension contributes the `switchboard-logo` icon, the Conversation view's containers and views, the `switchboard.showDock`, `switchboard.showHubLog`, `switchboard.newClaudeTab` and `switchboard.continueInBackground` commands, the `switchboard.forgetSideBarWidths` command (Reset Side Bar Widths), the title-bar buttons of the last two, the Switchboard pane's two views, and its settings (`switchboard.configDir`, `switchboard.closeDoneWindows`, `switchboard.closeDoneWindowsAfterMinutes`, `switchboard.rememberSideBarWidths`); it has no activity bar view or row commands. The command palette offers only what no visible control does (`menus.commandPalette`): Show Hub Log, Open Web Page and Reset Side Bar Widths, and, while the `switchboard.voiceEnabled` context key is on, Voice: Replay Last Reply and Voice: Show Log. It hides Show Dock (the status bar item runs it), New Claude Session in Editor Tab and Continue in Background (title-bar buttons), Open Artifact, New Conversation… (the view's plus), and Voice: Talk Here, Voice: Stop Speaking, Voice: Toggle Speak Replies and Voice: Toggle Microphone (the composer's buttons and the voice status bar item); every command stays registered. The voice commands' titles start with "Voice:", and the keys of Voice: Stop Speaking (Ctrl+Alt+.) and Voice: Toggle Microphone (Ctrl+Alt+M) work only while `switchboard.voiceEnabled` is on, which the Conversation view sets from its `VoiceView.enabled`.

## Dock

The dock compares group content before it updates its list, including nested action data. A focus report carries the whole view on both platforms; when the groups are unchanged, their controls stay attached. A changed view updates the list in place (`Lists.Sync`): a group, date band or row keeps its view model and its controls while its key stays, `RowViewModel.Update` takes the row's new texts, and only rows added, removed or moved get new controls, so keyboard focus, the selection and the scroll position survive the view. The held sessions window likewise keeps each held session's row and updates its texts, and the Settings window updates its navigation buttons and page buttons in place while the entries stay the same. While the pointer is pressed, the dock holds the latest view until after release, loss of capture or deactivation, so even a changed elapsed-time label cannot replace the pressed row before its click runs. Starting a row drag releases the held view, since a drag suppresses the click and needs current rows for its drop target. Linux's window tracking still receives each view.

PRD R71. `apps/dock` (`Switchboard.Dock.exe` on Windows, `Switchboard.Dock` on Linux) is a C# .NET 10 app with Avalonia 12.1.3. Window management sits behind `IWindows` and `IVsCode` (`Switching/Platform.cs`). On Windows they are `Win32Windows`, `VsCodeInstall` and `AppBar` in `Platform/`, described below. `npm run publish --workspace @switchboard/dock` publishes it to `~/.switchboard/dock/`.

- **Linux.** Under GNOME on Wayland an app cannot list, raise or watch another app's windows (mechanics M38). The dock runs under XWayland, so `App` docks it with `MainWindow.DockOnX11` when its window is an X11 window, else floats it (`Float`), and uses `ShellWindows` when the GNOME Shell extension's service is on the session bus at start, else `HubWindows`. Either way `HubWindows.Update` takes the view's `windows` ([Hub lifecycle](#hub-lifecycle)): a change of `front` to an item window selects its row (`MainWindow.ShowItemWindow`), and an item window that leaves `open` clears the selection, as on Windows.
  - **Docking.** `DockOnX11`, before the window shows, sets `_NET_WM_WINDOW_TYPE_DOCK` (`X11Dock`, libX11 and libXext on its own display connection). Mutter keeps a dock window above other windows, on every workspace and out of the taskbar, and leaves it inside a strut, from which it moves a normal window (M39). On open, after a move or resize and on a screen change, `PlaceOnEdge` places the window in the strip at the kept edge of the kept monitor, matched by Avalonia's `Screen.DisplayName` (the RandR output name, such as `HDMI-2`), or of the primary monitor when that is gone, between the monitor's left or right edge and the kept width in pixels at its scale, from the top to the bottom of that monitor's work area. The work area is Mutter's for that monitor, from the root window's `_GTK_WORKAREAS_D<current desktop>` (`X11Dock.WorkAreas`, `DockPlacement.WorkAreaOn`), because Avalonia's comes from `_NET_WORKAREA`, one rectangle for all monitors, which leaves out a panel at the bottom of one monitor or the top bar of a lower one (M39); without it Avalonia's is used. It then sets `_NET_WM_STRUT_PARTIAL` and `_NET_WM_STRUT`, measured from the X screen's edges (`DockPlacement.Strut`; the screen's width from `XGetGeometry` on the root window, because `XDisplayWidth` keeps the width the connection opened with), so maximized windows fill the rest. A strut at an edge another monitor adjoins would also reserve that monitor's rows, so there (`DockPlacement.CanReserve`) it sets both to zeros and reserves nothing. A drag of the title docks at the nearer edge of the monitor under the pointer, read with `XQueryPointer` because the window moves under it, and keeps the monitor's name, or no name for the primary monitor; in a gap between monitors nothing changes. The grip sets the width as when floating. With auto-hide on, `PlaceOnEdge` sets both struts to zeros (see Auto-hide). Mutter does not focus a dock window on a click, so the dock takes the focus with `XSetInputFocus` after a release whose press was not on a row, when a row's click (`Open`) or a command run in VS Code (`Run`) returns a reason it did not finish, and on a `show` event. A press on a row, its buttons included, leaves the focus where the click or control puts it, such as an item window, and takes the focus off the search box, because Avalonia is not told when another app's window takes the focus and would keep showing its caret. Taking the focus on the press lost the click on the operator's desktop (M39). While a full-screen window covers the dock's monitor, `MainWindow.ShowFullScreen` asks for `_NET_WM_STATE_BELOW`, because Mutter keeps a dock window above a full-screen one, and removes it afterwards.
  - **Extension.** `extensions/gnome-shell` (`switchboard@switchboard`, GNOME Shell 50) exports `switchboard.Shell` at `/switchboard/Shell` on the session bus. `Windows() → a(ts)` lists VS Code's normal windows (`isListed`: class `com.microsoft.VSCode`, or `Code` under XWayland, with a size, which leaves out the 0 × 0 window VS Code never shows and keeps minimized windows and those on other workspaces), front first, by Mutter window ID and title. `Activate(ts) → b` runs `Main.activateWindow` on one of those windows, which switches to its workspace and unminimizes it, moves it to the monitor with the given connector (`get_monitor_for_connector`; an empty or unknown one leaves it where it is), then maximizes it, and returns false for any other ID. `Terminals() → a(ts)` lists Ptyxis's normal windows the same way (`isListedTerminal`: class `org.gnome.Ptyxis`, mechanics M47), and `Activate` takes their IDs too. The `TerminalFocused(s)` signal carries a Ptyxis window's title each time Mutter's `focus-window` becomes one, and `TerminalUnfocused` is sent when it moves from a Ptyxis window to any other window or none (`terminalFocusSignal`); VS Code windows report their focus through the hub instead. `OpenUrl(s) → b` opens an http or https URL (`isWebUrl`) with the default handler from a shell launch context, whose activation token lets a running browser bring its window forward, and returns false for any other URL and an error when the handler does not start; any client on the session bus can open such a URL with the browser in front. `ShellWindows.OpenUrl` opens links through it and falls back to `xdg-open` when it refuses, fails or is missing from an older extension, but not when it does not answer within the 2-second timeout, since it may still open the link; the status line then says the browser did not open it (mechanics M54). `FullScreen() → as` and the `FullScreenChanged(as)` signal list the monitors a full-screen window covers, by Mutter connector (`get_logical_monitors`, `get_monitors`, `get_connector`), which XWayland uses as the RandR output name; the signal is sent only when the list changes. Any client on the session bus can call it. `npm run publish --workspace @switchboard/gnome-shell` installs it to `~/.local/share/gnome-shell/extensions/` and enables it; GNOME Shell on Wayland loads a newly installed extension only at the next login.
  - **With the extension.** `GnomeShell` calls the service with Tmds.DBus.Protocol, on the thread pool and for at most 2 seconds each; `Connect` returns null without a session bus or the service. `ShellWindows.Find` reads the name in each title with `DockWindow.NameOf`, as on Windows, `Show` activates the window by its ID on `ShellWindows.Monitor`, the connector `MainWindow.DockedOnX11` reports each time the dock places itself, and `FindVsCode` is the front VS Code window. While a call fails, such as after the extension is turned off, `HubWindows` answers as below, under negative handles of `ShellWindows`' own, since a Mutter window ID could equal one of its handles, shows the windows it found and sets `Raises`; the next call tries the shell again. `App` watches `FullScreenChanged` once the window opens, and `GnomeShell` watches the service's `NameOwnerChanged`: the service leaving reports no monitors, and its return reads `FullScreen()` again, which `FullScreenReports` drops when a change or another read came after the read started.
  - **Without the extension.** `HubWindows` finds an item window while its VS Code window is connected, by the name in its workspace file's name; `Show` runs `code <file>` (`VsCodeInstall`, `code` on PATH), which VS Code answers by focusing that window, and reports success because whether it came to the front is not known; `FindVsCode` answers while any window is connected, and because `Raises` is false `Switcher.Run` leaves the command for the next VS Code window to gain focus and says "Switch to a VS Code window to run it." A click on a row whose window is open runs `code <file>` and leaves no `openTabs`, so the window's tabs are not opened again. The dock stays above full-screen windows.
  - **Floating.** `Float`, for a window that is not an X11 window, makes the dock an ordinary window: shown in the taskbar, not on top, as tall as the primary monitor's work area at the kept edge, moved by dragging its title (`BeginMoveDrag`) and resized by its grip from the drag's start, moving its left edge when the grip is on the left, with no auto-hide pin.

- **View.** `dockView(snapshot, now, windows)` in `packages/hub/src/dock.ts` builds what the dock shows from the snapshot with the functions in `rows.ts` ([Rows](#rows)), so the dock has no rules of its own: `notes` (`snapshotErrors`, then `setupNotes`), the `groups` in list order, each with its codicon `glyph` (`GROUP_GLYPHS`), and its `empty` text, with Blocked on you and Snoozed only when they have rows, and `snooze`, the R49 choices from `snoozeChoices` with their wake times. A row has `key` (`item:<work item ID>` or `ticket:<pool ticket ID>`), `title`, `priority` (the dot's tooltip, or null), `time` (`rowTime`, or the pool's scope time), `playbook`, `ticket` (`gh 18941`), `source`, `sourceKind` (the source's `kind` from the snapshot's `sourceStates`, else the source name), `url`, `mark` (`kind`, its codicon `glyph` from `MARK_GLYPHS` in `group.ts`, which the browser extension's pills also use, and `title`), `details` (line 3 after the mark: wait reasons, wake time, warning and error, `From <key>`, then, unless the mark is `question`, the capitalised approval summary with its age, else the stage with its age or `No stage report`, or, when there is none of these and the row has a mark, the mark's title, each `{text, title, tone}`, cut at the caps with the whole text as `title`, and the stale age in `warning`), `stripe` (`stripeOf`), `turn` (`turnOf`, null on a snoozed row), `dim` (a Ready for review row on the agent's turn), `movable` (a Ready for review row), `window`, `open`, `controls`, the hub actions of `controlsOf` or `poolControls`, Snooze… without a wake time, `main` with `mainGlyph`, `pastes`, `commands` and `search` (see Controls and Header), each control and command with its codicon `glyph` (`glyphOf` in `dock.ts`, which throws for an icon it has no glyph for, so a new control icon fails the tests), `band` (`doneBand` in `rows.ts` for a Done work item row, else null; the snapshot lists done work items from `doneSince` in `rows.ts`), `comment` (PRD R87: on a work item row with comments, the comment codicon `glyph` and a `title` listing each comment under its `wakeText` time, oldest first, separated by blank lines; else null, as on a pool row), `links` (the ticket's links once each, without one to the ticket itself, each with `label`, named `PR #<n>` or `gh <n>` from a GitHub pull request or issue URL, else by its label, for the menu; `kind`; `badge` and `glyph` for its pill on line 2, a pull request's number after the pull request codicon; and `mentioned`, from the stored link's flag, which the dock draws as a grey pill after the others, PRD R61), `returned` (PRD R51) and `unread` (the work item's unread replies and statuses, [Unread](#unread), 0 on a pool row; the Switchboard pane draws it, the dock does not); and the view's `sources` (`sourceGroups`: the configured sources but ad-hoc grouped by `kind` in order of first appearance, each group with `enabled`, true while any of its sources is on, `title`, a line per source saying whether it is polled and then what a click does, and its `sources`, `{name, enabled}`), `repoRoots` and `schedules` (`{name, playbook}` for Run now, PRD R82). `apps/dock/test/fixtures/dock.json` is a recorded view: `dock.test.ts` compares `dockView` with it and the C# tests parse it.
- **Search before Done** (PRD R63). `POST /dock/search {text, since}` answers `{rows, more}`: the Done rows of work items done from `since` (epoch milliseconds, 0 for all time) up to `doneSince`, whose ticket is not snoozed, whose search text (`searchText` in `dock.ts`, the same text as a row's `search`) holds every word of `text` ignoring case, newest first, built by `dockRow` with `band` the month they were done, such as `October 2026`. It answers at most 100 rows (`SEARCH_LIMIT` in `packages/hub/src/dockSearch.ts`) and `more` when more matched. `DoneSearch` reads the range's search texts in one query (`Store.doneSearchEntries`) once per snapshot, since each store change builds a new one, so the searches made as the operator types filter them in memory; only matches get a whole `WorkItemView` (`workItemViews` in `snapshot.ts`). `POST /dock/select` finds such a row's work item in the store when the snapshot does not list it. On a copy of the operator's store, the first search of all time took 2 ms to read 270 older work items and 5 ms at 100 times that history, and each search then took up to 5 ms to match plus about 1 ms per matching row it builds.
- **Window.** `windowOf`: a hosted conversation's window is its host's ([Hosted conversations](#hosted-conversations)): the name from `Ticket.hostWindow`'s file name, that file, the conversation's folder and `hosted: true`, so `writeWorkspaceFile` leaves the host's file alone and `windowItems` leaves the conversation out. A folder-hosted conversation's host is a folder window (`isFolderHost`: a `hostWindow` that does not end in `.code-workspace`): `file` is null, `folder` the host's canonical folder, `folders` that folder and `name` its last name, which no window title carries, since a folder window has no `sb:` title. Otherwise the first folder is the latest session's `cwd`, else the worktree, and the worktree is the second folder when it differs; no window without either, or while the latest session is `external` and not ended, because the session lives in the operator's own window (PRD R81). `name` is the ticket key, or `adhoc <adhocKey>` for a ticket a ticket report moved so its open window is still found (PRD R83), with a `-` for the space, then `-<first folder name>` when the first folder is not the worktree, then `-` and the first 6 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the first folder's path (lower case, `/` separators, no trailing separator), with any run of characters other than letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-` replaced by `-`, such as `gh-18941-3f2a91`; `title` is the ticket title; `file` is `<Switchboard home>/windows/<name>.code-workspace`. The hash keeps each name's first folder fixed: two folders with the same name, or a moved worktree, get different windows. `open` is `question` (`{workItemId, sessionId, cwd}`) while the active session waits in its background process, `session` (`{sessionId, cwd}`) for the latest session when no session is active or the active one was released, `url` for a row without a window whose focus is a URL, and null otherwise, such as for a row without a window and focus, as while a session runs in its background process.
- **Workspace file.** The hub writes each row's workspace file with `writeWorkspaceFile` before it sends a dock view, and the extension before its own [row click](#vs-code-extension), so one writer serves both: the folders, a `window.title` of `<title>${separator}${activeEditorShort}${separator}sb:<name>` with each `$` in the title replaced by a fullwidth `＄`, because VS Code drops the text after a `$` up to the next `}` (M26), a `window.titleSeparator` of ` · `, and `extensions.ignoreRecommendations: true`, which turns off every extension recommendation notification in the window, because VS Code keeps a dismissed prompt to install a repository's recommended extensions per workspace, so every new item window would ask again (M50) (`workspaceText`). It writes only a file whose text differs, because VS Code applies any change to the open window, and the hub keeps the text of each file it wrote, so a view writes only the files that changed. A file it cannot write is logged. A view from a hub older than the dock names no file, and the dock asks for a window reload.
- **Terminals** (PRD R71). `Switcher.Click` runs `select` by doing nothing, and a `url` open on a row with a window, which only `dock.openVSCode: review` sends, by opening the URL; it runs `terminal` with `Switcher.Terminal`. Open in terminal, from the menu or as a prompt before start's main control, calls `Switcher.OpenInTerminal`: with only `row.terminal` it calls `Switcher.Terminal`, with only the window's `terminal` command it calls `Switcher.Run`, and with both (`dock.openTerminal: auto`) it calls `Terminal` when `FindTerminal` finds the session's terminal, and otherwise `Run` for an `active` session, whose item window's `openTerminal` finds it live in VS Code, or while `Find` finds the item window, and `Terminal` for the rest; without `FindsTerminals` it calls `Run`. The menu shows one Open in terminal either way. `Terminal` refuses when `IWindows.FindsTerminals` is false. A `handoff` first runs the shared `Stop`, which posts `open-question` and waits for the row's `open` to offer the same session either as `session` or as `terminal` without a handoff, then uses that row's terminal. It finds the window with `IWindows.FindTerminal(name)` and shows it; a session resumed elsewhere (`active`) whose terminal is not found opens its item window with `session` instead; otherwise `ITerminal.Open` starts it and the dock looks for it every 100 ms for 20 seconds. `Terminals.Ptyxis` runs `ptyxis --new-window -T <title> -d <cwd> -- <executable> <args>`, and `Terminals.WindowsTerminal` runs `wt.exe -w new --title <title> --suppressApplicationTitle -d <cwd> -- <executable> <args>`; both get the dock's environment from `CliEnvironment`, without `ELECTRON_*`, `VSCODE_*` and Claude Code's session markers (`CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_PID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`, `CLAUDE_CODE_EXECPATH`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SSE_PORT`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ATTENDED`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_*`), keeping other `CLAUDE_CODE_*` settings as the hub's sessions do, and with an absolute executable's folder first on `PATH`. `Win32Windows.FindTerminal` looks for a visible top-level `WindowsTerminal` window whose title names the terminal; the foreground hook already passes every title to `MainWindow.ShowForeground`, and `DockViewModel.SelectWindow` matches a terminal's name on `row.open` or `row.terminal` as well as an item window's. On Linux `ShellWindows` asks the extension's `Terminals`, `GnomeShell.WatchTerminalFocused` passes its `TerminalFocused` signal's title to `ShowForeground`, and `GnomeShell.WatchTerminalUnfocused` calls `MainWindow.TerminalLeft` on `TerminalUnfocused`; an extension without `Terminals` counts as none, and one without `TerminalUnfocused` never reports the focus leaving a terminal. `ShowForeground` also reports to the hub, through `TerminalFocus`, the Switchboard session ID of the Code Mode attach terminal whose name the title carries (`DockViewModel.TerminalSession`, over the rows' `terminal` and `open` and the held sessions' terminals), or null for any other window, posting `POST /dock/focus` only when it changed and in order, and again when the dock connects to a hub ([Voice](#voice)). **Answer in terminal**, a main control that is not a hub control, runs the row's click from the main button and the menu (`MainIsClick`). The dock opens its stream as `/dock/events?click=1` and runs each `click` event's row with `MainWindow.ClickForwarded`, or says "Switchboard has no row for <key>." on its status line.
- **Code Mode sessions** (PRD R94, R95): a `DockTerminal` with `codeMode` runs `<executable> "<home>/hub.js" attach <Switchboard session ID>` in the terminal app; the hub's dock view sets `executable` to `sessions.nodePath` and `args` to the script's path, `attach` and the ID. It is named and titled as for a background session, except that its name ends with the Switchboard session ID instead of the short ID, so the dock finds the same window after a Claude session ID change. More Actions has **Held sessions**, which opens `HeldSessionsWindow`: one row per entry of the view's `held` (`DockHeld`), orphans first, with the work item or "No work item", Switchboard and Claude session IDs, folder, state, idle time, memory and attached terminals, which follows each view. Its Open in terminal opens the row's attach terminal in the terminal app (`Switcher.Terminal` with no row) and is unavailable for an orphan, which no session of the store names, so `POST /attach` would refuse it; Stop asks first in `ConfirmDialog`, naming the session ("Stop held session o1?" for an orphan, "Stop the held session of gh 115?" otherwise, with its ID and folder), then posts `POST /holder/stop {id}` with the Switchboard session ID and shows the result or refusal in the window, and is unavailable once the process has exited (`exited`, from the holder's `exitedAt`, whatever state the store still holds). While an orphan exists, the view's `heldNote` ("1 held session without a work item") shows above the groups, and a click on it opens the held sessions window.
- **Click.** `Switcher.Click` opens the row's `open` URL, the work item's or pool ticket's focus (PRD R79), for a row without a window; the row's `url`, which Open ticket opens, stays the ticket's. Otherwise it uses the row's workspace file; for a hosted conversation's row (`DockWindow.Hosted`) it first posts `POST /dock/select {rowKey}`, and shows the hub's refusal. A folder-hosted row (`DockWindow.Folder`, no file) posts `POST /open` with the folder for a `session` open, then runs `Code.exe <folder>`, which brings forward the folder window already on it or opens one, and waits up to 20 seconds for the view's `windows.folders` to list that folder, compared as the hub's `hostKey` does (`Switcher.FolderKey`: `\` read as `/` and case ignored on Windows and macOS only), else says "VS Code did not open a window on <folder> within 20 seconds."; it finds no window by title and leaves no `openTabs`, and only a folder window on that folder claims what was left for it, never an item window that holds the folder. `Switcher.Run` leaves a command for such a row with `POST /window {workspace: <folder>}` and opens the folder the same way, withdrawing the command when no folder window connects. For `question` it posts `open-question` and waits up to 20 seconds on the stream for the row's `open` to become `session` with the same session ID, then continues as for `session`; for `session` it posts `POST /open {sessionId, cwd, workspace}` with the workspace file, which only the window opened from that file claims when it gains focus or connects, so another window on the same folder does not take it ([Open in VS Code](#claude-code-runner)). `/open` answers the open's `id`; when the click is cancelled, VS Code does not start, or the item window does not come forward or the folder window does not connect within the wait, the dock posts `POST /open/withdraw {id}`, which drops that open unless a window claimed it or a newer open for the same window replaced it, so no window opens a session from a click the operator gave up on when it connects or gains focus within the open's two minutes; an item window's tabs stay left for the window VS Code still opens. It then finds the visible top-level `Code` process window whose title `DockWindow.NameOf` reads as the row's name; without one it runs `Code.exe <file>`, which opens it in the profile VS Code gives the workspace, with `Code.exe` beside the `bin\code.cmd` on PATH and no `ELECTRON_*` or `VSCODE_*` variables, and looks again every 100 ms for 20 seconds. Before it opens a window, it posts `POST /window {workspace, command: {type: 'openTabs'}}` for that workspace file, so the new window opens its [tabs](#vs-code-extension) (PRD R72), and withdraws it when the click is cancelled before VS Code starts or VS Code does not start; once VS Code starts, the window it opens claims the tabs even when a later click cancels this one or the window appears after the 20-second wait; a hub that refuses it does not stop the click. The hub refuses `openTabs` without a workspace. `Win32Windows.Show` restores a minimized window; moves a window whose nearest monitor is not the dock's (`Win32Windows.DockMonitor`, which the window sets each time it docks) into that monitor's work area with `SetWindowPos`, after restoring it if it was maximized, because VS Code opens a new window on the monitor of the window used last; maximizes it; calls `SetForegroundWindow`, and checks `GetForegroundWindow` for 100 ms, twice. Each failure, including a missing workspace file, VS Code or the browser not starting, and any exception, shows on the dock's status line. The status line is `DockViewModel.Status`, shown in `StatusToast` with a Dismiss button that clears it; setting it raises a change even for the same message, which restarts `MainWindow`'s countdown, and a 1-second timer clears it once it has shown for 10 seconds while the dock was not hidden and the pointer was off the toast (`CheckStatus`). The countdown also restarts when the pointer leaves the toast and when a hidden dock shows, so a hover between checks counts. Tests replace `MainWindow.StatusClock` and call `CheckStatus`, as the headless platform runs no timers. A click on another row cancels the running click, which then makes no further request or switch and shows nothing; a click on the row already being switched to is ignored, and a click on a question whose stop is still under way, such as after a click on another row in between, waits for that stop instead of sending another, so a question is never stopped twice. Windows lets a process bring another window forward only while its own input is the latest (M26), so this runs from the click.
- **Foreground.** `Win32Windows.WatchForeground` installs an out-of-context `SetWinEventHook` for `EVENT_SYSTEM_FOREGROUND` on the UI thread and passes each new foreground window's title to `MainWindow.ShowForeground`. `DockWindow.NameOf` reads the name from the last `sb:<name>` in a title that is at its start or after ` · ` and ends at a space or the end, which allows the text VS Code adds before and after the title (M26); `DockViewModel.SelectWindow` selects the first row in list order with that window, never a folder-hosted row, whose folder window has no `sb:` title, opening its group as a kept choice, and the row is scrolled into view. Any other title changes nothing. A new item window's title lacks the name for its first half second, so its own foreground change selects nothing; the click that opened it already selected the row. `Win32Windows.WatchClosed` keeps each item window's name by handle (`WindowNames`), because a destroyed window's title cannot be read: it reads the names of the open item windows at start, then updates a window's name on each `EVENT_OBJECT_NAMECHANGE`, forgetting it when the title names none, such as after the window opens another workspace. On `EVENT_OBJECT_DESTROY` for a kept window, both for `OBJID_WINDOW` and `CHILDID_SELF`, it passes the name to `DockViewModel.WindowClosed`, which clears the selection when the selected row has that window (M26). An item window is a visible, unowned `Code` process window whose title `DockWindow.NameOf` reads, as for `Find`.
- **Controls** (PRD R71). A row's `main` is the label of the main control (`mainControl`, or a pool row's from `poolControls`), shown as a button while the row is selected, with `mainTitle` as its tooltip (`mainTitle` in `rows.ts`, which for Approve names the prompt it runs): Open in Claude, Open in Codex and Answer in terminal run the click, a prompt before start's Open in terminal runs `Switcher.OpenInTerminal`, and any other posts the matching control's action. `pastes` holds an `awaiting-approval` row's drafts: Copy reads the file when clicked (`Paste.Read`, without a byte order mark or trailing whitespace) and puts it on Avalonia's clipboard, from a menu of labels when there is more than one. `commands` holds the controls a VS Code window runs, with the message the extension's handler takes: Open changes from `controlsOf` on a row with a worktree (not Show in Source Control: the item window's Source Control view already holds the worktree), History on every row, and Open report (`openUrl` with the approval's draft). `Switcher.Run` posts `POST /window {workspace, command}` with the row's workspace file and switches to its item window as a click does; when the item window is not open, it first posts `openTabs`, as a click does, and withdraws it with the command only when VS Code has not started; for a row without a window, and for a Settings file, it posts a null workspace and brings the VS Code window nearest the front forward (`Win32Windows.FindVsCode`, the first visible top-level window of a `Code` process in z-order), or says to open one when there is none. A control that runs in VS Code replaces a row click or control still switching windows, as another click does, so an earlier switch does not take the front back. `/window` answers the command's `id`; when the switch is cancelled or fails, the dock posts `POST /window/withdraw {id}`, so the window that comes to the front next does not run it. A right-click selects the row and opens a context menu (`MainWindow.MenuOf`) in groups with a separator between each: the main action (Open in Claude or Answer in terminal on a row whose session waits on a question, else the hub control the row's `main` names, or a prompt before start's Open in terminal); the work controls (Start, Start with guidance…, Start with prompt…, Approve, Retry, Stop, Triage again and any control the dock does not group); the state controls (`mark-done`, `reopen`, `snooze` and `restore` actions); the notes (Add comment…, and controls with a `group` as a submenu of that name, each a check item, checked when `checked`, such as a Code Mode row's Persona menu ([Personas](#personas))); the order controls of a Ready for review row (`move`, `reset-position`); what the row opens (Copy, Open in terminal from `row.terminal` when the `commands` hold no window `terminal` command and the main action is not an Answer in terminal whose `open` names the same terminal, the `commands`, whose Open in terminal also runs `Switcher.OpenInTerminal`, Open ticket and an Open item per entry of `links`, which opens its URL in the browser, its label cut at `LinkLabelMax` (48) characters with the whole label in its tooltip, and with more than two links all of them in an Open link submenu named by their labels); and last each control with `confirm` `{question, detail, action}`, such as Delete (PRD R76), which posts its action only after `ConfirmDialog` returns true. Snooze…, recognized by its `snooze` action, opens `SnoozeDialog` and posts the action with `until` and `comment`, or nothing on Cancel; a control with `input` `{question, detail, field, action, text?, choice?}`, such as Start with guidance…, Start with prompt… or Add comment…, opens `InputDialog` with `text` in its box, whose button is unavailable while the text is blank and any `choice` `{field, label, value, values}` still has `value` selected, and posts the action with the trimmed text as `field` and a changed choice as `choice.field`, or nothing on Cancel. A failure or refusal shows on the status line in the warning colours (`DockViewModel.Status`); `Copied <label>.` and a needs-you message show in the toast's neutral colours (`ShowInfo`), since they report no problem. The row menu (`MainWindow.RowMenu`) is anchored to the window at the pointer's last position, which the window tracks from `PointerMoved` and `PointerPressed`, because `ContextRequested`'s own position was wrong for a right-click; a menu opened from the keyboard opens at the row's corner. Every dock menu goes through `RowMenu`: the row menu, More Actions, a source kind's menu, the searched range's menu and the Copy button's menu, the last four below their button. A header button's menu is its `ContextMenu` only while open: a right-click or the context-menu key on the button builds and places it afresh, since Avalonia would open a kept menu with its own placement and its first opening's limits. `MenuPlacement` keeps each menu and its submenus (Copy, Persona) on the monitor at its anchor, placing them itself (`PlacementMode.Custom`) in physical pixels: the area a menu may take is the monitor's work area, widened to the monitor's edge on the dock's side because the work area leaves out the dock's app bar strip, and, as an X11 dock window, the work area is Mutter's for that monitor (`DockPlacement.WorkAreaOn`), as for the dock itself. Before the popup opens, its `MaxWidth` and `MaxHeight` are set to that area in device-independent pixels at that monitor's scale, which a popup created on another monitor takes once placed, so a taller menu scrolls; Avalonia's own limit on Windows is the work area of the monitor the popup window was created on, which on a phone-sized screen over Remote Desktop left a menu only as wide as the screen beside the dock's strip, and on another monitor can be taller than the dock's. A menu opens right of its anchor on a left dock and left of it on a right dock, downward from the anchor; when it does not fit there it opens on the other side or upward, and when neither fits it moves into the area, over the dock or its parent menu. A submenu opens beside its item the same way, overlapping it by the theme's 4 pixels, its top at the item's top or its bottom at the item's bottom. Avalonia's horizontal adjustments are not used, because they keep a popup inside the work area and moved every menu beside the dock. With no monitor known, a menu opens at its anchor away from the dock's edge with `FlipY | SlideY`, and a submenu at its item's far edge. Tooltips in the dock follow it too: a window style sets every control's `ToolTip.Placement` to `Custom`, and `MainWindow.PlaceTip` anchors the tooltip at the pointer with the menu's gravity and `FlipY | SlideY`, because the default `Pointer` placement slid it out of the strip.
- **Order** (PRD R73). A press on a `movable` row and a vertical move of more than 4 pixels starts a drag instead of a click, and Escape cancels it; a line between Ready for review rows shows the drop place, and a drop there posts `{type: 'move', workItemId, after}` with the row above the line, or null at the top, from the group's shown order; a drop elsewhere posts nothing. Alt+Up and Alt+Down on the selected row post its Move up or Move down control. F8 and Shift+F8 (`DockViewModel.SelectNextTurn`) select the next or previous row whose `turn` is `operator` in Blocked on you and Ready for review, after the selection in list order and wrapping, open its group, clear a search that hides it, scroll to it and run its click.
- **Keyboard** (PRD R71). Rows, group and date band headings and every pill take the keyboard focus, which shows as a ring in `DockFocus`. A row the keyboard reaches is selected and scrolled into view, so its buttons (Approve, Start, Retry, Copy, Open report) show and Tab reaches them. On a focused row or heading Up and Down move the focus to the previous or next row or heading; on a row Enter runs its click, and the Menu key or Shift+F10 opens its menu at its corner; on a heading Enter or Space opens or closes it; on a pill Enter or Space opens its link (`MainWindow.ListKey`). A hub view keeps the focused row's controls; when it moves the row, or the row holding the focused button or pill (`FocusedRow`), to another group or band, `RestoreFocus` puts the focus on the row's new controls, or on its group's heading when the group or band is closed.
- **Line 2** (PRD R27, R52). A row's second line (`PillLine`) gives the playbook the width its comment icon and pills leave, ending in an ellipsis. When the line has too little room its pills shrink to their icon instead of being cut: first the pills wider than 56 pixels, longest first, while the playbook keeps its width; then, once the playbook is down to 60 pixels, the rest longest first; Returned last. Pills that do not fit even as icons, as beside a selected row's buttons on a narrow dock, are left out whole, the last first and Returned only when it alone does not fit; the line decides again when its parent arranges it narrower than it measured it. A compact pill shows only its icon (the source's logo for the ticket key, or a tag for a source without one; the pull request, issue or link icon for a link), keeps its link and click, and has its full text in its tooltip, which names the pill and its URL in every state (`RowViewModel.TicketTip`, `DockLink.Tooltip`, `RowViewModel.ReturnedTitle`). A pill's label is never cut, and the pills show whole again when the dock widens.- **Docking.** `AppBar` subclasses the window with `SetWindowSubclass`, registers it with `SHAppBarMessage(ABM_NEW)` and a callback message, and reserves `ABE_LEFT` or `ABE_RIGHT` on the kept monitor (`Native.MonitorNamed`, which finds the monitor whose `MONITORINFOEX` device name matches, else the primary one): `ABM_QUERYPOS` with the monitor's height, then `ABM_SETPOS` with the width from the outer edge Windows lets it keep (`DockPlacement.Strip`); the window takes the returned bounds through `SetWindowPos`, and `AppBar.Strip` holds every later move and resize to them in `WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGING`, keeping only what the call's `SWP_NOMOVE` or `SWP_NOSIZE` leaves alone, because Avalonia moves a window it resizes into the monitor's work area, which starts at the dock's own inner edge, and at some fractional scales its rounded size is one pixel short, such as 452 of 453 pixels at 175%. The width in pixels is the kept width in device-independent pixels at the monitor's scale (`GetDpiForMonitor`). When the window reaches a monitor at another scale, Avalonia takes the scale on `WM_DPICHANGED` but updates its client size only on `WM_SIZE`, which the held size prevents, so `AppBar` sends `WM_SIZE` with the current client size after Avalonia handles `WM_DPICHANGED` (M26). The window is cloaked (`DWMWA_CLOAK`) from its creation, with DWM transitions off, until it has drawn two frames in its strip, or for at most a second, so it never shows at Avalonia's first size and position. It reserves the strip again, resolving the kept monitor by name again, on `ABN_POSCHANGED`, on `TaskbarCreated` and on `WM_DISPLAYCHANGE`, so a dock whose monitor was unplugged docks on the primary one; it rechecks the visible windows on `ABN_FULLSCREENAPP` before changing topmost, forwards `WM_ACTIVATE` and `WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGED`, and removes the bar on close. Dragging the inner edge (the right edge of a dock on the left, the left edge of one on the right) resizes the window the same way, keeping its outer edge; on release, or when another window takes the pointer capture, the width is kept and the strip reserved again, so other windows move once.
- **Dialogs.** Every dock dialog (New ad-hoc session, New conversation in a folder, Snooze, the input and confirm dialogs, the Settings window's save and delete questions) uses `DialogShell`: a title in 16-pixel semibold with an optional muted subtitle, labelled fields 14 pixels apart, and a footer in `DockDialogFooter` above a rule with its buttons on the right, Cancel before the main action, all 84 pixels wide at least. The focus starts on the first field that shows and takes input, a choice group's checked button; Enter runs the main action, except from a focused button, an open list or a multi-line box, where Ctrl+Enter runs it; Escape cancels. A destructive action (Delete, Stop) is drawn in `DockDanger`, is never run by Enter, and opens with the focus on Cancel. New conversation in a folder names the chosen folder under its title. The Held sessions and Ticket volume report windows open with the same title style and a muted line that says what they show.
- **Header.** The title row (`HeaderPanel`) gives Close, More Actions, Auto-hide, Search and the source icons their full width from the right and the title, which ends in an ellipsis, what is left, so Close always shows on a narrow dock; a control that no longer fits whole, such as the source icons at the minimum width, is hidden rather than cut. It holds one icon per entry of the view's `sources` (an older hub's flat `{name, enabled}` entries read as groups of one, `SourceGroup.Current`), a kind's sources (`SourceViewModel`), drawn as the kind's logo (`SourceGithub`, `SourceZoho` and `SourceForum` geometries in `App.axaml`, from the VS Code panel's icons) in `DockSourceGithub`, `DockKeyZoho` or `DockKeyForum`, or `DockSourceOff` when none of its sources is on, with the group's `title` as its tooltip. A click on a kind with one source posts `POST /sources {name, enabled}` with the opposite state. A click on a kind with several opens `SourceMenu` below the icon, placed as a row's menu is: a check box `MenuItem` per source with `StaysOpenOnClick`, bound to `SourceEntryViewModel.Enabled`, whose click or Space posts that source's opposite state and, on a refusal, shows it on the status line and puts the check back. A snapshot with the same sources per kind updates the icons and entries in place (`SourceViewModel.Update`), so the open menu, its anchor and its focus survive the toggle's snapshot; one that adds or removes a source replaces the icons and closes the menu. The menu is a `ContextMenu` of the dock, so auto-hide waits while it is open, and Escape closes it and returns focus to the icon; then a separator, shown when there are sources, and Search, which sets `DockViewModel.SearchOpen` to show or hide the search box (hidden at start; hiding it clears the filter), Auto-hide (see Auto-hide), More Actions (`MoreButton`, codicon `ellipsis`), a second separator, and Close. More Actions opens `HeaderMenu` below the button, placed as a row's menu is, in groups with a separator between each: New ad-hoc session… and New conversation in a folder…, which asks for a folder and starts a conversation hosted by it ([Hosted conversations](#hosted-conversations)); Held sessions and Ticket volume report; Refresh sources, which posts `POST /poll` and shows a refusal in the status line, and Show hub log, which runs the `log` window command with no row, so the VS Code window nearest the front shows the hub log; and last Open Settings, which opens the [Settings window](#settings-window). The search box sets `DockViewModel.Query`, which applies `Filter` 250 ms after the last key (`SearchDelay`, on the injected `TimeProvider`) once the text has 3 characters (`SearchMinimum`), at once as the bare number when it is a number or a listed row's ticket or link key with or without `gh`, `PR` or `#`, and as empty for shorter text; a later key cancels the wait, even one that types the same text again (`typedRun`). Under it `SearchRangeButton` shows `DockViewModel.Ranges[Range]` and opens a menu of the four ranges; with a range past the first and a filter, `SearchHub` (`IHub.Search`, `POST /dock/search`) is asked for the Done rows from a month or three months back (local time) or from 0, a newer filter or range drops an older answer (`searchRun`), and the answer's rows join Done's after the view's (`ShowDone`), also after each new view, until the filter or range changes. `SearchEnd`, after the groups while a filter applies, shows `NoMatchText` when no group has a row and no answer is pending, `SearchNote` (`Searching…`, the hub's refusal, or that more matched), Search further back while the range is not all time, and All time beside it when nothing matched and one step back is not all time, then, when nothing matched, Clear search (`NoMatchesClear`), which empties the search box. A filter: a row shows when its `search` text, the title, `searchDescription` (key, linked pull requests and issues as `PR #<n>` and `gh <n>`, playbook, group) and the worktree branch, contains every word ignoring case; while it filters, every group and date band with a match shows open and the others hide, and Escape clears it and hides the box. A band's `Shown` list and a group's `Bands` change by inserting and removing items (`Lists.Sync`), so a narrower search keeps the rows it still shows instead of drawing every row again, and Done's bands draw only the rows in view (`VirtualizingStackPanel`), so a search that opens Done with hundreds of rows draws a screenful; the other groups draw every row, which dragging in Ready for review measures, and a row out of view is scrolled to with `ItemsControl.ScrollIntoView`. On a headless render of the operator's 276 rows, the first keystroke fell from 2.3 s to 0.2 s and later ones from about 1 s to under 0.1 s. New ad-hoc session opens a modal window (`AdhocDialog`) with labelled fields: Run (below), Repository, which lists the `repoRoots`, then the folders chosen before (`dock.json` `recentFolders`, newest first, at most five) and **Choose a folder…**, which picks any folder with the system folder picker, as `switchboard register` takes any existing folder, selects it and keeps it among the recent folders unless it is a repo root, a cancelled picker leaving the folder selected before, and is set to the repo used last; Provider, with `sessions.provider` selected, which, when the hub cannot list the providers, falls back to the default the hub named last ("Using Codex, the default."), or to Claude when it never named one ("Using Claude."), with the reason under the box in the error colour, so the window stays usable; a Prompt box, a Start automatically check box, unchecked each time, and Create, which posts `adhoc` with the prompt, repo and `start` and closes the window on success; a refusal keeps the window and its prompt and shows the hub's reason in it; Create is disabled while the prompt is blank and until the hub answers, so a second click cannot create a second work item, and Cancel and closing the window wait for the answer too; with no repo roots the Repository list offers only Choose a folder…. When the view's `schedules` is not empty, a list above the repo (`AdhocRunBox`) offers `A prompt` and `Run <name> now · <playbook>` for each schedule; choosing a schedule hides the repo, prompt and check box, renames Create to Run, and Run posts `run-schedule` with the name (PRD R82). With schedules and no repo roots, the window opens on the first schedule; a prompt needs a folder from Choose a folder…. A foreground item window whose row the search hides clears the search, so its row shows selected.
- **Docking.** `AppBar` subclasses the window with `SetWindowSubclass`, registers it with `SHAppBarMessage(ABM_NEW)` and a callback message, and reserves `ABE_LEFT` or `ABE_RIGHT` on the kept monitor (`Native.MonitorNamed`, which finds the monitor whose `MONITORINFOEX` device name matches, else the primary one): `ABM_QUERYPOS` with the monitor's height, then `ABM_SETPOS` with the width from the outer edge Windows lets it keep (`DockPlacement.Strip`); the window takes the returned bounds through `SetWindowPos`, and `AppBar.Strip` holds every later move and resize to them in `WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGING`, keeping only what the call's `SWP_NOMOVE` or `SWP_NOSIZE` leaves alone, because Avalonia moves a window it resizes into the monitor's work area, which starts at the dock's own inner edge, and at some fractional scales its rounded size is one pixel short, such as 452 of 453 pixels at 175%. The width in pixels is the kept width in device-independent pixels at the monitor's scale (`GetDpiForMonitor`). When the window reaches a monitor at another scale, Avalonia takes the scale on `WM_DPICHANGED` but updates its client size only on `WM_SIZE`, which the held size prevents, so `AppBar` sends `WM_SIZE` with the current client size after Avalonia handles `WM_DPICHANGED` (M26). The window is cloaked (`DWMWA_CLOAK`) from its creation, with DWM transitions off, until it has drawn two frames in its strip, or for at most a second, so it never shows at Avalonia's first size and position. It reserves the strip again, resolving the kept monitor by name again, on `ABN_POSCHANGED`, on `TaskbarCreated` and on `WM_DISPLAYCHANGE`, so a dock whose monitor was unplugged docks on the primary one; it rechecks the visible windows on `ABN_FULLSCREENAPP` before changing topmost, forwards `WM_ACTIVATE` and `WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGED`, and removes the bar on close. Dragging the inner edge (the right edge of a dock on the left, the left edge of one on the right) resizes the window the same way, keeping its outer edge; on release, or when another window takes the pointer capture, the width is kept and the strip reserved again, so other windows move once.
- **Auto-hide** (PRD R71). The header's pin (`AutoHideButton`, codicon `pinned` while the strip is reserved, `pin` while auto-hide is on) sets `DockViewModel.AutoHide`, which keeps `autoHide` in `dock.json`, and docks again. With auto-hide on, `AppBar.Dock` reserves nothing: a bar that holds a reservation is removed and registered again, `ABM_QUERYPOS` still places the strip off the taskbar, no `ABM_SETPOS` follows, and the bar registers with `ABM_SETAUTOHIDEBAREX` on its edge and monitor, which Windows refuses when another auto-hide bar holds that edge; the dock then hides itself all the same. Turning it off unregisters it and reserves the strip. The window keeps its full strip and layout while hidden: `SetWindowRgn` limits its drawing and hit testing to the outer 3 device-independent pixels, converted to physical pixels at the current scale (`DockPlacement.Sliver`), and covers that edge with `SliverView`, the same 3 device-independent pixels wide, which draws the dock's background and, from `MainWindow.SliverBars`, each coloured row stripe cut to the list's view at its height in the window, recomputed after each layout while hidden and drawn without a layout of its own. A pointer move there starts a 100 ms timer that shows the dock after 500 ms of continuous hover; leaving cancels the timer. `Activated`, or a `show` event from the hub, which shows it before it asks for activation because Windows can refuse that, hides `SliverView`, clears the region and shows it, with `InvalidateVisual` because the composition surface keeps the clipped frame until Avalonia draws again (M33). While it shows, a 100 ms `DispatcherTimer` asks whether anything keeps it open: a resize, title drag or row drag, an open menu placed on the dock, a context menu or a flyout such as the search box's Cut, Copy and Paste (a class handler on `Popup.IsOpenProperty`, as Avalonia opens a control's own context menu without the dock's code), or the top-level window under the pointer (`WindowFromPoint`, then `GetAncestor(GA_ROOT)`) or in the foreground being the dock or owned by it through its `GW_OWNER` chain (`HideDelay.OwnedBy`), which covers its menus, which are owned popups, and its dialogs, which are owned windows, but not the Settings window, which has no owner; `HideDelay` hides it once nothing has for 400 ms, measured with a `Stopwatch`. Hiding closes those menus. Repeated placement calls do not restart an active hide countdown. A separate 100 ms timer reconciles the native topmost style, even while hidden, because Avalonia caches its `Topmost` property and does not notice external z-order changes. The dock checks visible windows in front-to-back order on its monitor, skipping tool windows, minimized or cloaked windows and the desktop. It yields when the first relevant window is full-screen and restores topmost when an ordinary window covers it or no relevant window remains. Focusing a window on another monitor does not raise it over full-screen content; shell full-screen notifications request the same check.
  - **On Linux,** as an X11 dock window, the dock calls X through `IX11Edge`, which `X11Dock` implements and tests fake. The pin shows only when the X server has the Shape extension (`XShapeQueryExtension`, libXext); without it the dock places itself as with auto-hide off, whatever `autoHide` says. With auto-hide on, `PlaceOnEdge` sets both struts to zeros. Hiding sets the window's bounding and input shapes (`XShapeCombineRectangles`) to the outer 3 device-independent pixels at the docked monitor's scale, measured from the window's width in the X server (`XGetGeometry`) and running past its bottom, which the server clips; `SliverView` covers that edge as on Windows, and a resize while hidden cuts the sliver again. Showing sets both shapes to the current X11 window rectangle instead of removing them, because Mutter kept drawing only the sliver after the shapes were removed (mechanics M49). Once a shape has been set, each resize reapplies the sliver while hidden or the full rectangle while shown, even after auto-hide is turned off; otherwise Mutter can keep clipping the drawing to the old width (M51). Mutter then draws nothing of the hidden part, and picks the actor below it for the pointer. Pointer hover uses the same 500 ms delay as on Windows; `Activated` and a `show` event show it immediately. The 100 ms check keeps it open during a resize, title drag or row drag, while one of its menus is open, or when `X11Dock.KeptOpen` finds the dock's windows under the pointer (each window from the root down, `XQueryPointer`) or holding the focus (`XGetInputFocus`, then its ancestors, `XQueryTree`). A window is the dock's when its `WM_TRANSIENT_FOR` chain reaches the dock (`HideDelay.OwnedBy`, at most 32 owners), which covers its dialogs, or when it is an override-redirect window whose `_NET_WM_PID` is the dock's process, which covers its menus and tooltips, as Avalonia gives them no owner. The Settings and Volume windows have neither, so as on Windows they do not keep it open. Avalonia closes a menu when the dock window loses the focus; while the focus was elsewhere, a click on another app left the menu open. So, with auto-hide on or off, the dock takes the focus (`XSetInputFocus`) as a menu opens, and right after that and in a 100 ms check (`MainWindow.CheckPopups`, `X11Dock.HasFocus`) closes its menus once the focus, after being the dock's or its menu's, is another window's; when the dock never got the focus, the menu stays until it is dismissed. A dock that is hidden when it opens an owned dialog shows first (`ShownForDialog`), because a modal dialog blocks the pointer moves that would show it. The floating dock has no pin.
- **Moving.** While the header's title is dragged, each pointer move and the release dock the dock at the left or right edge, whichever is nearer the pointer (`DockPlacement.EdgeAt`), of the monitor under it (`GetCursorPos`, then `MonitorFromPoint`, nearest), read from Windows because the window moves under the pointer during the drag. A move that changes the edge or monitor saves the edge and the monitor's device name, or no name for the primary monitor, and reserves the strip there; losing the pointer capture ends the drag where the dock last docked.
- **State.** `<Switchboard home>/dock.json` keeps `width` (200 to 800, default 320), `edge` (`left` or `right`; anything else reads as `left`), `monitor` (a device name such as `\\.\DISPLAY2` on Windows, a RandR output name such as `HDMI-2` on Linux, or null for the primary monitor), `autoHide` (default false), `theme` (default `system`), `recentFolders`, the folders New ad-hoc session's Choose a folder… picked, newest first and at most five, and `groups`, the groups the operator opened or closed; Blocked on you, Ready for review, In progress and Unstarted start open. Done's date bands are not kept: `DockViewModel` holds their open state in memory under `done:<band>`, so each dock start opens Today and Yesterday and closes the others, and `DockSettings.Load` drops `done:<band>` keys an older dock kept. Kept choices under keys the view does not send, such as `needs` and `working`, have no effect. `GroupViewModel` splits its shown rows into `BandViewModel`s by `band`; a group other than Done has one band with no heading. One dock runs per desktop (`Local\Switchboard.Dock` mutex); closing it ends the app, even with the Settings window open. `--home` or `SWITCHBOARD_HOME` names the folder holding `.switchboard`, as for the hub.
- **Connection.** `HubClient` reads `hub.json`, opens `/dock/events` with the token, and reads `hub.json` again 2 seconds after the stream ends or fails. Besides `dock`, the stream sends `show`, from a VS Code window's status bar item (`POST /dock/show`), which activates the dock, `config`, which makes an open Settings window read the files again, and `click`, a click VS Code forwarded (it opens the stream as `/dock/events?click=1`); the dock skips any other event. Each attempt sets the state: a 404 means a hub older than the dock, and the dock asks for a window reload; anything else without a stream reads as not running, and the dock starts a hub ([Dock start](#hub-lifecycle)). While the dock has no current view the last rows stay, dimmed to half opacity and disabled (`DockViewModel.IsLive`), so their clicks, buttons, menus, F8 and Alt+Up/Down do nothing, a row menu open when the hub goes away closes, and a row control posted meanwhile is refused (`MainWindow.Act`); while no hub runs a **Start hub** button under the connection line starts one at once (`HubClient.StartHub`, `HubStarter.StartNow`, which skips the start's grace and wait unless the hub it started last still runs) and shows why it could not.
- **Needs you** (PRD R65). The hub compares each built snapshot with the one before (`newlyNeeding` in `packages/hub/src/needs.ts`) and sends `needs {items: [{rowKey, message}]}` for the work items that started needing the operator, such as `Switchboard: Fix the inbox badge — Approve to send` (`needsMessage`); the first build after a hub start sends none. `HubClient` raises `NeedsYou` for each, and the dock shows the message in its status toast; a click on it runs the row's click (`ClickForwarded`), and another message in the toast opens nothing.
- **Look.** Colours are VS Code's Light Modern and Dark Modern theme values, chosen by the Settings window's General → Dock → Theme setting (`system`, `vscode`, `light` or `dark`, kept in `dock.json`, default `system`). The Settings window drafts the dock theme alongside the configuration draft; Save persists it to `dock.json` and applies it to every dock window and popup. Discard restores the saved choice, and closing with a changed theme asks to save or discard it. A failed dock save keeps the previous theme and reports the error. System follows the operating system; Light and Dark override it. In VS Code mode, the dock uses the focused window's `activeColorTheme.kind`, reported by the extension on focus, theme changes and connection to the authenticated `POST /window/theme` route as `{theme: 'light' | 'dark', at}`. High-contrast kinds map to light or dark. The hub retains the latest timestamped report in memory and includes `theme` in dock snapshots, ignoring older reports. The dock uses System until a report arrives and while disconnected. The last report remains in force when VS Code loses focus or closes, until another window reports or the hub restarts. Custom palette colours are not copied. `App.axaml` defines the dock's own brushes and also redefines the Fluent theme's control keys (`TextControl*`, `ComboBox*`, `Button*`, `AccentButton*`), so the Settings window's inputs and buttons take VS Code's input, secondary-button and button colours without per-control styles; a `link` button class draws the Settings window's file path in VS Code's link colour; marks use `codicon.ttf` from `@vscode/codicons`, and Running and Triaging spin.

## Browser extension

`extensions/chrome` is a Manifest V3 extension (PRD R56, R74), built by `npm run build` into `extensions/chrome/dist`, which the operator loads unpacked from `chrome://extensions` with Developer mode on, and reloads there after a rebuild.

`extensions/firefox` builds the same source for Firefox into `extensions/firefox/dist`. Release Firefox keeps only extensions that addons.mozilla.org signed, so there are three ways to run it, from the `@switchboard/firefox` workspace:

- `npm run sign` builds `@switchboard/core`, whose compiled modules the bundle imports, and `dist/`, has addons.mozilla.org sign it as an unlisted add-on, which is not published, through `web-ext sign --channel unlisted`, saves it as `switchboard-firefox-signed.xpi`, which the operator installs from `about:addons` and which stays installed, and publishes it as a release of the private GitHub repository tagged `firefox-v<version>` at the commit it was built from, which holds every signed version. It needs the operator's addons.mozilla.org API key and secret as `WEB_EXT_API_KEY` and `WEB_EXT_API_SECRET`, from the environment or from `~/.switchboard/amo.env` (`SWITCHBOARD_AMO_KEYS` names another file), which every checkout shares and which it refuses on Linux and macOS when other users can read it, though not on Windows, which has no mode bits, and a signed-in `gh`, and refuses a checkout with uncommitted changes or a commit GitHub does not have. addons.mozilla.org signs each version once, so each build's version is Chrome's with a fourth number, the minutes since 2026-01-01 UTC (`buildNumber`), and `signingRefusal` stops a signing whose version is already released, read from every release including drafts, as from a build in the same minute, or lower than a released one, as after the clock went back. `src/release.mjs` holds these decisions, with the git, `gh` and `web-ext` commands injected from `steps.mjs`. The signed file is kept until it is copied, and when the copy fails the error names the temporary file. Beside the `.xpi`, `switchboard-firefox-signed.json` records its version and commit, so when the release fails, `npm run release` finishes it, replacing a draft the failure left. Signing removes the old record before it copies the new file, and refuses while the recorded version has no published release, so a second signing cannot replace a version that only this checkout holds. Firefox cannot download from the private repository, so the add-on has no `update_url` and does not update itself.
- `npm run dev` rebuilds `dist/` when the Chrome extension's bundled source, manifest, `content.css` or icons change, watching their folders so a file an editor replaces is still seen, and runs `web-ext run`, which starts Firefox with `dist/` as a temporary add-on and reloads it after each rebuild. Its profile, which keeps sign-ins between runs, is `SWITCHBOARD_FIREFOX_PROFILE`, else `~/snap/firefox/common/switchboard-dev` for snap Firefox, which cannot read hidden folders in the home folder, else `~/.switchboard/firefox-dev`; arguments after `--` go to `web-ext run`. Closing its Firefox ends it. On Linux and macOS, Ctrl+C or SIGTERM reaches `web-ext` as SIGINT, on which it closes Firefox; on Windows, Ctrl+C reaches `web-ext` from the console and has not been tried. Ctrl+C in VS Code's terminal on Ubuntu leaves snap Firefox open, because the snap takes no signals from VS Code's AppArmor label and `web-ext` fails with `kill EACCES` ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M53).
- With the signed copy installed, `dist/manifest.json` loaded from `about:debugging` runs in its place until Firefox restarts, with the signed copy's host permissions and `storage.local`, and its Reload loads a new build. A restart brings back the signed copy. Remove does too, but without its host permissions or `storage.local`, so the board shows nothing until the operator uninstalls the signed copy and installs the `.xpi` again ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M53).
- `npm run package` zips `dist/` into `switchboard-firefox.xpi`, unsigned, for Firefox Developer Edition or Nightly with `xpinstall.signatures.required` set to `false` in `about:config`.

`npm run lint` runs addons.mozilla.org's checks on `dist/` with `web-ext lint`. The build changes only what Firefox requires ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M52), and `src/firefox.mjs` refuses a Chrome manifest or `content.css` it no longer recognises:

- The manifest is Chrome's with the build number added to its version, `background.scripts` in place of `background.service_worker`, an add-on ID `switchboard@switchboard`, a minimum version of 140, and the hub's host permission without its port, `http://127.0.0.1/*`, because Firefox matches nothing with a pattern that has a port. Below, "service worker" means Firefox's background script, which also stops when idle and is woken by the same events.
- esbuild replaces the global `chrome` with `browser`, whose calls return promises, and sets `FIREFOX`, so a query mark's removal passes `loadReplace` to `tabs.update`: Firefox can commit the marked URL before the load restarts, and the unmarked load then replaces its history entry. `content.css` loads the font from `moz-extension://__MSG_@@extension_id__/codicon.ttf`, which Firefox resolves to the extension's UUID.
- Firefox lists MV3 host permissions as optional; when they are not granted, the board shows nothing, and the operator grants them in the extension's Permissions tab in `about:addons`.
- Firefox has no `tab.pendingUrl`, so a tab's kind is read from `tab.url`, which is `about:blank` for a new tab whose first navigation has not committed.

- The content script runs on every `https://github.com/*` page, because GitHub opens a project without a page load, and acts only while the page has a board column (`[data-board-column]`) or a project table (`#project-items-region`), even one a filter left empty (`PROJECT` in `src/board.ts`). The service worker reads `GET /snoozed` and sends `POST /snooze` for it, because a content script on github.com cannot read a loopback address; the manifest's only host permission is `http://127.0.0.1:47615/*` in Chrome and `http://127.0.0.1/*` in Firefox.
- It reads the snoozed URLs when a board appears, every 60 seconds, when the tab becomes visible and after a snooze from the page. A failed read counts as none snoozed.
- A card's issue is its title link (`h3[id^="board-card-title-"]` inside an `a`), compared as `owner/repo#number` in lower case. GitHub empties the content of a card out of view, a hidden one included, so the script keeps each card's issue by `data-board-card-id`; a card it has never seen rendered stays shown until it renders.
- Table view: GitHub renders a table (`#project-items-region`, `role="grid"`) in chunks of five `[role="row"]` elements, each with its title in `[role="rowheader"]`, and replaces chunks away from the view with empty placeholders. A row's chunk sits in its list: the table's body, or, in a grouped table, the group's list after its sticky header row, whose first `[data-component="Text"]` is the group's name. A row's issue is the rowheader's link (`itemHref`, which reads a card's or a row's). `applySnoozedRows` marks a snoozed row `data-switchboard-snoozed`, its list `data-switchboard-rows` and, when shown, `data-switchboard-shown`; `content.css` hides the marked rows of a list not shown. Groups are shown by name, the ungrouped table by `''`. Because rows out of view are not in the page, the count comes from `seen`, each issue's group when its row last rendered, which `watchBoard` clears when the view changes: the path or query other than the side panel's `pane`, `itemId` and `issue` (`viewKey`). The button reads its group from its `data-switchboard-group` on click, because GitHub keeps a group's list when the group is renamed. It goes last in the element that holds the group header's name and count, or the Title column header's name in an ungrouped table, marked `data-switchboard-toggle`, and stops its click and `pointerdown` from reaching GitHub's header handlers. It cannot follow the last row: a short table's add-item bar (`[class*="OmnibarContainer"]`) is absolutely positioned at `top` computed from GitHub's own row count, so anything added after the rows sits under it. For the same reason hidden rows would leave a gap above the bar, so `applySnoozedRows` sets `--switchboard-hidden-rows` on the grid to the rendered rows it hides, and `content.css` moves the bar up by that many `--table-cell-height`s, except when the bar is fixed to the bottom of the view (it then holds an `isFixed` class). A button whose holder no longer has a group or rows is removed. A shown row dims its cells' content, not the row, whose opaque background would otherwise let the grid lines behind it show through.
- It marks a snoozed card `data-switchboard-snoozed` and a shown column `data-switchboard-shown`, and `content.css` hides marked cards in columns that are not shown.
