The link is a centred button appended to the column's scrolling card list (`[data-dnd-drop-type="card"]`), so it follows the last card shown. A `MutationObserver` on child lists reapplies the marks 50 ms after GitHub renders; a pass that changes nothing makes no mutation, so the script does not trigger itself.
- Card pills (PRD R67): `src/pills.ts` reads `GET /cards` through the service worker (message type `cards`), with the same triggers as the snoozed read, and runs its own `MutationObserver`. It puts a `.switchboard-pill` span after the card header's issue reference (`[id^="board-card-header-title-"]`) of each card, or, pushed to the right of the title cell, after the issue number that follows a table row's title link, of each item whose title link's issue key matches a card's `url`; GitHub issue URLs are the only ones that match. The pill's text is the playbook, its namespace in a muted span, or the group name when the playbook is null; `data-switchboard-group` is the group, or `snoozed`; a card's `stripe`, the dock row's `stripeOf` in `rows.ts` (null on a pool ticket), is the pill's `data-switchboard-tone`, which `content.css` gives GitHub's danger, attention or accent colours for `error`, `warning` or `turn`; a card without one, as from an older hub, has the default colours, and an unknown one fails the read. `aria-label` is the playbook (or group name), the group or Snoozed, the mark's title and "open in Switchboard", the menu's verb, comma-separated. A card's `mark` is the row's mark from `markOf` in `group.ts`, so the board and the dock share one rule; the pill puts it first as a `span.switchboard-mark` with `data-switchboard-mark` holding the same codicon's glyph from `MARK_GLYPHS` in `packages/core/src/group.ts`, at 16px in the codicon font, which the build copies from `@vscode/codicons` to `dist/codicon.ttf` and `content.css` loads through `web_accessible_resources`, and the tooltip adds `MARK_TITLES[mark]`. The `running` and `triaging` marks spin even when the browser reports `prefers-reduced-motion`, as the dock's do. The font, unlike an SVG of the same paths, renders the 1px strokes evenly at fractional display scales, and GitHub's content security policy does not block the extension's font. A card with a null or missing mark, as from an older hub, has no icon; an unknown mark fails the read. A pass leaves an unchanged pill alone, so it makes no mutation, and updates a changed one in place, matching its mark, namespace and label by kind and changing only what differs, so a focused pill keeps its focus and a selection in an unchanged part stays. A failed read, including a 404 from an older hub, removes every pill. `groupOf`, `triageLabel` and the `Card` type are in `packages/core/src/group.ts`, which imports only types, so the History webview and the browser extension import it as `@switchboard/core/group` without bundling the store.
- Card menu (PRD R66, R69): `src/menu.ts` watches `body` with a `MutationObserver` for a `ul[role="menu"]` that GitHub's Primer renders in `#__primerPortalRoot__`, outside the card, when a card's More actions button or a table row's Row actions button opens. `menuCard` finds the card or row by the button in a `[data-board-card-id]` or a table's `[role="row"]` whose `aria-labelledby`, the id of its More actions tooltip, matches the menu's. Before the menu paints, it appends a copy of the menu's divider and items cloned from the menu's first plain item, so they keep GitHub's hashed class names, with ids, shortcuts and trailing visuals removed and a codicon as the leading visual: **Open in Switchboard** (`vscode`) for any card whose issue `GET /cards` lists, and outside `done`, **Snooze in Switchboard…** (`clockface`) or, when the card's `snoozed` is true, **Unsnooze in Switchboard** (`debug-restart`). A menu with no plain item to copy gets none. Each pass compares the wanted items, kept in the menu's `data-switchboard-items`, and changes them only when they differ, so an open menu follows a cards read that changes the card's group or `snoozed` or drops it, and a pass that changes nothing makes no mutation. A change updates the items in place: Open in Switchboard stays, and the Snooze or Unsnooze item (`data-switchboard-slot="state"`) changes its label, icon and action, whose runner a `WeakMap` holds, so a focused item keeps its focus; Done removes it. The issue's card comes from the cards read that the pills already make (`watchPills` passes each read it accepts, not one a later read overtook, to an `onCards` callback, from which `main.ts` keeps `cardsByIssue`), so a hub that does not answer, or one older than `/cards`, adds no items. Primer's focus zone includes the appended items for ArrowDown. An item runs on click, Enter or Space, stops the event reaching GitHub, and dispatches Escape on the menu, which GitHub closes, returning focus to its button.
- Snooze (PRD R66): `snoozeMenus` in `src/snooze.ts` opens a fixed-position popup in `body` under the button of the menu that opened it, or above it when the viewport has no room below, and focuses its first choice after GitHub's focus return. It lists `snoozeChoices` from `@switchboard/core/snooze`, a subpath export that keeps the store and other Node modules out of the browser bundle. A choice goes to the service worker as `{type: 'snooze', url, until}`, which it posts to `POST /snooze`; Unsnooze sends `{type: 'unsnooze', url}` for `POST /unsnooze` with no popup, both with `Content-Type: application/json`. On success the popup closes, the board reads `/snoozed` again, which hides or shows the card, and the pills read `/cards` again, so the menu and pill take the new state; on failure the popup shows the hub's error. A scroll outside it, Escape or a click outside closes it.
- Open in Switchboard (PRD R69): besides the card menu's item, a click on a `.switchboard-pill`, or Enter on it, since the pill is focusable (`tabindex=0`, `role=link`), is caught by capturing listeners on the document in `src/open.ts`, before GitHub's handlers would open the card's side panel, and navigates to the `vscode://` URI of the card's or table row's title link; the pill's tooltip says it opens in VS Code. The browser extension does not read a card's `links`.
- Switchboard tab group (PRD R74): the dock's `Switcher.OpenUrl`, which every link the dock opens goes through, adds `Switcher.TabBookmark` (`#switchboard-tab`) to a URL with no bookmark, and otherwise `Switcher.TabParameter` (`switchboard-tab=1`) last in the query, before the bookmark, which stays unchanged; `TAB_BOOKMARK` and `TAB_PARAMETER` in `src/tabs.ts` hold the same strings. The service worker checks every top-level `webNavigation.onBeforeNavigate`, because Chrome's URL filters ignore the bookmark, and for a URL `dockLink` recognises on the first navigation of a tab that `tabs.onCreated` reported (`firstNavigations`), as Chrome creates one for a link from Windows, so a reload, Back or Forward to a URL that still has the bookmark is not placed again, runs `placeTab`, one navigation at a time (`tabPlacer`), so a second click that arrives first finds the group and tab the first left: a query mark is removed with `tabs.update` at once, which restarts the navigation; the tab joins the window's group titled `Switchboard`, found with `tabGroups.query` or created cyan; and when the group has another tab of the same kind, the new tab moves to that tab's index, read again after joining, and that tab closes; when it has left the group meanwhile, nothing moves or closes. `webNavigation.onCommitted` removes a bookmark mark by loading the URL from `bookmarkRemoved`, with a bare `#`, a same-document navigation that adds a history entry; without the `#` Chrome loads the page again. A tab is never focused, because Chrome opens a link from Windows in a new active tab and brings its window forward. A link's kind is `tabKind`: `github.com/issues`, `github.com/pull`, else host and first path segment. A placed tab keeps the kind of the link it was placed for, because a site such as Zoho Desk redirects the link to another address; `placedKinds` keeps it in `chrome.storage.session` under one `placedKind:<tab ID>` key per tab, so concurrent writes for different tabs do not overwrite each other, and session storage outlives the service worker. `tabs.onRemoved` and `tabs.onUpdated` with a `groupId` of -1 or of another group drop a tab's kind when it closes or leaves the group. A tab with no kind, dragged in or placed before the browser restarted or the extension reloaded, which clear session storage, has the kind of its address. The `tabs` permission lets the extension read the group's URLs, `tabGroups` find and name the group, `webNavigation` see the navigation, and `storage` keep the kinds ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M28).
- The DOM hooks are GitHub's, not a contract; `test/fixtures/column.html` records them from the public roadmap board on 2026-10-07, `table.html` and `table-grouped.html` its table view, ungrouped and grouped by Status, on 2026-11-12, `row-menu.html` a row of the operator's signed-in table and its opened Row actions menu on 2026-11-12, scrubbed to `github/roadmap#1090`, `omnibar.html` that table's add-item bar, positioned and fixed, on 2026-11-12, and `card-menu.html` a card of the operator's signed-in board and its opened More actions menu on 2026-10-08, scrubbed to `github/roadmap#1090`.

## Settings window

PRD R54. A separate, resizable dock window, **Switchboard Settings**, one at a time; Open Settings in the dock's header menu brings the open one forward. The page logic is plain C# in `apps/dock/src/Switchboard.Dock/Settings/` (`EditorState`, `SettingsFields`, `SettingsPages`, `SettingsEditor`), and `Views/SettingsWindow.cs` draws it.

- The hub reads and writes settings.json through `packages/hub`'s `config.ts`: `POST /config/read` answers `settings` (`path`, `version`, `value` and `error`, valid or not), the permission modes, Codex's permission modes, the providers, template variables and source kinds the fields offer, `inUse`, the open work items per playbook, `enabled`, whether each configured source is on, and `configDir`; `POST /config/check {settings}` answers `{errors}`; `POST /config/save {value, version, configDir}` writes the whole file as formatted JSON and answers `{saved, version}` with the version of the text written, or 409 `{errors}`. A save for another `configDir` is refused with 409. Checks (`checkSettings`) use what the hub applies on load, `parseSettings`, the own `parse*Config` of each source that is on and `parsePlaybook` for each entry, plus `templateError` for `setupPrompt` and each template, because the hub finds an unknown variable only when rendering the classifier instructions or a session prompt fails. A playbook's errors read `playbooks.<name>: <error>`. The hub's watcher reloads what the window writes, as it does a hand edit, and then sends `config` on the dock stream, which makes the window read the file again; so does each new connection of the dock's stream, such as to a hub started on another config folder. A read from another folder drops the draft and says so on the window's status line. The window needs a running hub.
- The navigation follows the path a ticket takes: **General**; under **Sources**, each configured source, titled by its kind's label, such as GitHub, or by its name and that label when the two differ, such as `personal (GitHub)`, with a **New <kind> source** entry for each kind that no source key names and **New source**; under **Playbooks**, the playbooks by namespace with **New playbook**; under **Schedules**, the schedules with **New schedule**; and last, under **Voice**, **Voice settings**, the personas and **New persona**. Each namespace's name opens its table page. A page whose part of the file has unsaved changes (`sessions`, `triage` and `dock`, `voice` apart from `voice.personas`, `voice.personas[<index>]`, `sources.<name>`, `schedules.<name>`, `playbooks.<name>`, or any playbook of a namespace) shows a dot, and a page with errors shows an error mark.
- A schedule page (`schedule:<name>`, PRD R82) edits `schedules.<name>`: Playbook, a choice of the file's task playbooks; Repo root; Runs (`every`), whose Only by hand removes the key; and, by `every`, Day as a weekday choice for `week` or a whole number for `month`, and Time (`at`) for any period. Changing Runs removes `day`, and Only by hand also removes `at`, and redraws the page. **Remove schedule** removes the entry from the draft. The New schedule page (`new-schedule`) takes a name, checked as lowercase letters, digits and hyphens not already used, and Create adds an empty entry and opens its page, whose errors name the fields to fill.
- The window holds one draft of the whole file (`EditorState.Draft`) and the version it started from, and never saves on its own. A bar across the bottom of the window holds the status (**Saving…**, **Saved** or **Unsaved changes**, with the number of errors, or that the file cannot be read), a warning when settings.json changed on disk under the draft, and **Save** and **Discard**. Save, or Ctrl+S from any field, is unavailable while settings.json changed on disk under the draft; otherwise it first runs a check due for an edit, lists the errors in the bar instead of saving while any stands, and otherwise sends the draft; a refusal stays in the bar until the next edit, discard or successful save, and the draft is kept. An edit made during a save continues from the version written; a second Save during a save, including Save in the close question, waits for it. Discard drops the draft and reads the file again. Closing the window with unsaved changes asks **Save changes to settings.json?** with Save, Discard and Keep editing; a refused Save keeps it open. Closing the dock closes the window without asking. Add source, Remove source, Create on the New playbook page and Delete change only the draft; Delete asks in a modal window, which names how many open work items use the playbook, read from the hub when Delete is pressed. Text typed on the New playbook page before Create is not a change to the file.
- Each field is a row: its label on the left, with an info icon when the field has help (`Field.Help`), shown only in the icon's tooltip, and the input on the right. A multiline box grows with its text, up to 20 lines for a prompt and 8 for a list, and then scrolls. Adjacent checkboxes with the same `Field.Group` share one row under that label, each explained by its own tooltip. An `Advanced` section (`Section.Advanced`), on General or a source page, starts collapsed, stays open across redraws once opened, and opens when a check places a new error on one of its fields.
- The settings pages edit their sections of the draft. A field left empty removes its key, so the default applies, and the placeholder shows that default. `automationAgentIds` is the exception: its default is not empty, so an absent key shows the default list, and an emptied list is saved as `[]`. Keys the window has no field for are kept. A source's own errors block a save only while the source is on, so a source that is off can be saved incomplete. Source pages have no on/off field; the navigation notes a source that is off. A source page other than `adhoc` shows a Kind field, whose empty choice uses the name; changing it keeps only the common source keys (`SettingsFields.CommonSourceKeys`), because each plugin rejects another's keys, and redraws the page with the new kind's fields (`SettingsFields.SourceKind`). A page notes a kind the hub does not offer. The New source page (`new-source`) chooses a kind from the hub's source kinds and takes a name, checked as lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, not `schedule` or `adhoc` and not already used; Create adds `{}` when the name is the kind and `{"kind": <kind>}` otherwise, and opens its page. Adding a source adds `{}`, off until the operator turns it on in the dock's header; removing a source deletes its key, and its work items stay.
- A playbook page edits its entry under `playbooks`. A triage playbook shows only its description, its kind, Rates importance and its classifier instructions. A task playbook has a **Start** row with Automatically (`autoStart`) and Needs a worktree (`requiresWorktree`), a **Reports** row with a checkbox for each of its `reports`, and, while `approval` is checked, an **Approve sends the work** checkbox below them; unchecking `approval` clears `approvalSends`. **Return of** lists `returnOf`, one playbook per line, and **Focus page** chooses `focus`: Any page, The ticket or The pull request; changing the kind to triage clears both. The window writes an entry with only non-default keys, except `autoStart` and `requiresWorktree` on a task playbook, in the order description, kind, autoStart, requiresWorktree, workdir, reports, approvalSends, returnOf, focus, rates, provider, permissionMode, model, template; the permission mode offers the modes of the playbook's agent, and its default and the model's name that agent's `sessions` settings; the template is kept as typed. An edit that returns an entry to its fields on disk restores the entry as the file has it. An entry the window cannot read into fields, including one with a key the hub does not accept, shows its error and Delete, so the operator fixes it by hand or deletes it.
- A namespace page (`namespace:<namespace>`) is a table of the namespace's playbooks: one row per playbook, whose name opens its page and whose description is the name's tooltip, and one checkbox column per flag (`SettingsFields.TableColumns`): Auto and Needs worktree under **Start**, each report and Sends (`approvalSends`, enabled only with Approval) under **Reports**, and Repo root (`workdir: repoRoot`), for task playbooks; Rates for triage playbooks. A cell that does not apply to the row's kind, or a row whose entry cannot be read, has no checkbox. A checkbox edits the draft, the row shows a dot while it differs from the file, and the row's errors show under it; an update changes the drawn checkboxes in place, so a click during a check is not lost. A namespace with no playbooks left says so.
- The draft is checked 250 ms after the last edit and after each read; an answer to an older check is dropped. The window places each error on the field whose path it names (`FieldOfError`, `PlaybookFieldOfError`); an error that names no field shows at the top of its page. While the operator types, only the navigation, bar, notes and errors are redrawn, so the field keeps focus; a full redraw, such as after a reload, puts focus and the caret back in the same field.
- settings.json is read with a SHA-256 version. A save sends the version the draft started from, the hub refuses it when the file on disk no longer matches, and a successful save answers the version it wrote. With no draft, a read shows the file, and a draft equal to the file as read before or after is dropped; any other draft whose file changed is kept, with the bar's warning, and cannot be saved, so the operator discards it to take the file on disk. Writes go through a temporary file and a rename, like the source toggles.
- General groups its fields as **Sessions** (Default agent, Concurrency, Code Mode (experimental), Friction debriefs), **Claude sessions** and **Codex sessions** (Model, Permission mode), **Triage** (Concurrency, Model, Timeout (seconds)), **Dock** (Theme, the dock theme kept in `dock.json`; Rows open in, `dock.openVSCode`; Terminal for "Open in terminal", `dock.openTerminal`), and a collapsed **Advanced** section (Install hooks, Claude Code path, Codex path, Node.js path).
- The **Voice settings** page (`voice`, PRD R105, R106), under the Voice heading, edits the `voice` section but its roster: Enabled, Voice and Style (the lists of core's `TTS_VOICES` and `SPEECH_STYLES`, which the dock copies as `SettingsFields.TtsVoices` and `SpeechStyles`), Speed, a decimal field, Expressive delivery, Expand replies, Away timeout, Needs-you sound and Output device; under **Spoken alerts** (PRD R112), Spoken alerts, Your name, Alert when a turn finishes, Alert on a question, Alert on a permission prompt and Answer window; under **Dictation**, Mic idle timeout, End-of-message pause and Input device; under **Voice check**, an advanced group, Voice match, Low pitch factor and Least low share; and under **Playbook personas** a choice per entry of `voice.playbookPersonas`, the roster's personas or None, which removes the entry, with an **Assign** row that gives a task playbook without one a persona. An entry whose playbook the file no longer has is marked missing. The **Voice** heading also lists each entry of `voice.personas` by its name, else its ID, as a page `persona:<index>` with Name, Voice and Style, whose empty choice uses the Voice page's, and Personality; field paths index the list, as the hub's errors do (`voice.personas[0].name`), and an error of an entry that names no field, such as one that is not an object, shows at the top of its page. **New persona** (`new-persona`) takes an ID, checked as lowercase letters, digits and hyphens not already used, and Create adds `{id}` and opens its page, whose errors name the fields to fill. The ID cannot be edited afterwards, because sessions store it as their persona and pin and assignments name it. A persona whose name is a voice's, compared without case, gets a note to choose another, since it reads as a voice choice. **Remove persona** removes the entry from the draft with the assignments that name its ID, unless another entry has that ID, and drops the `voice` containers it leaves empty that the file does not have. A read that moves the persona shown finds its page again by ID, or opens Voice. An assignment's key is read whole, so a playbook name with a dot is one key. Under the fields, **Your voice** shows whether a voiceprint is enrolled and runs enrolment ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)). The page has no voice preview.
- The settings.json path, shown on each page, opens the file in the VS Code window used last, through the dock's `openUrl` window command.

## Store

- One SQLite database, `~/.switchboard/switchboard.db`, opened through Node's built-in `node:sqlite` (M1). No native module, so VS Code's Electron upgrades do not require rebuilds.
- The hub is the only process that opens the database. The extension and the `switchboard report` CLI go through the hub's HTTP server, so there is no cross-process locking.
- WAL mode; one write transaction per flow step, so a crash leaves each work item in its last committed state.
- Core accesses the database only through a store interface, which isolates changes to the `node:sqlite` API. Tests use `:memory:`.
- Schema changes are numbered migrations applied by the hub at startup, recorded in `PRAGMA user_version`.
- Activity log (PRD R59): `events` holds each flow event that wrote a row or was refused (`at`, `type`, `actor` of `operator`, `session`, `source`, `triage` or `hub`, the ticket, work item and session it names, `detail` as JSON without prompts, contexts or polled lists, except a `start`'s `prompt` when it differs from the stored one after trimming (PRD R77), and `refused`), and `changes` each row it wrote (`entity`, `entityId`, `op` of insert, update or delete, the owning ticket, work item and session, and `fields` mapping each changed field to its old and new value). `handle` opens an event with `Store.beginEvent` before applying it, and a `wake` event first for snoozes that end; `#insert`, `#update`, the worktree, link and paste upserts and `deleteTriage` read the row before writing and log the difference in the same transaction. Operator actions, reports, failed starts and failed stops are stored even when they change nothing; other events only when they change a logged field. Comments (PRD R87) are written with `Store.addComment`, which does not log the row: the `comment` event's detail holds the text, as the snooze event's holds `Ticket.snoozeComment`. Fields that change on every poll or status change (`lastPolledAt`, `sortAt`, `scopeAt`, `statusAt`, `runStartedAt`, `stageAt`, `queuedAt`, `stateAt`, `releasing`, a link's or paste's `reportedAt`) and the order fields `readyAt` and `orderAt` are not logged, so a move shows in History as its `move` event, with `after` as its detail; `taskPrompt`, `approvalPrompt` and `context` are logged as `true`; text over 2000 characters is cut. A refusal naming no known ticket is not stored. The hub logs a refusal the flow never sees with `Runtime.refuseReport`, which calls `recordRefusal`: a `/report` request that fails validation and names a session, a worktree report naming no git worktree, and a link report its source refuses. It keeps only the report's kind and short text fields, never a prompt. `deleteWorkItem` deletes the work item's changes and its triages' (found by `entityId`, because a pick attaches pool triages logged without a work item) and events left with no changes. The migration stores a `recording` event, whose time is `recordedSince`, and `reconstructed` events with a `fact` for what the existing rows showed: work item created, done, last stage and an approval, blocked or failed status; triage start and end; session start and end; links and pastes. The tables have no foreign keys and are kept forever. `historyRevisions` holds a counter per ticket that every write to its log advances, deletions included; `historyRevision` reads it.
- A Code Mode session's conversation (`messages`, with a typed message's `delivery`, `error`, `opId` and `spokenRequested`, and a dictated one's `pieces` and `partial`, `replies` and `statuses`, each reply and status with when it was seen, `seenAt`, and each reply with its `speak` passage, `tone` and its speech, `spokenState`, `spokenAtMs` and `saidChars`), its transcript readers' `checkpoints`, the windows' `windowBindings` and the web page's sign-ins, `pageSessions` ([Web page](#web-page)), are written outside the activity log ([Conversation](#conversation)); `Store.openReadOnly` serves the snapshot worker.
- Which sources are on is in the `sourceStates` table, because the dock's header toggles it (PRD R3).
- Not in the database: settings and playbooks, in `settings.json`, which the operator edits and can keep in git; session transcripts, which Claude Code stores, and which sessions find by `nativeSessionId`; the [debrief](#friction-debriefs) state and log; and the pending opens and window commands in `window-queue.json` ([Open in VS Code](#claude-code-runner)), which expire after 2 minutes.

## Repo roots

PRD R16, R21. `packages/core/src/repo.ts` owns a ticket's folder. A ticket records its repository in `tickets.repo`, `owner/name` in lowercase; migration 38 adds the column empty, which means the source's home repository, so the migration reads no settings (the store opens before them). `homeRepo` reads a source's `repo` setting, the one plugin key core reads; `repoOf` is a ticket's repository or that home. `ticketRoot(settings, ticket, stored)` answers, in order, the ticket's own `repoRoot`; the source's `repoRoot` with `{owner}` and `{name}` replaced, when it holds `{name}`; that `repoRoot` for the home repository when it does not; otherwise an error naming the setting and repository. `stored` is each source's repositories with tickets (`Store.reposBySource`): two repositories, in any sources, that resolve to the same folder, compared as Windows does, are refused for both. A refused root fails the start even when the ticket has a linked worktree. Session start (`workdir` and the setup session), the R68 task report's default and allowlist, the R6 repo list (`repoRoots(settings, stored)`, each home root and each stored repository's root, never a pattern or a shared root) and worktree linking use it. `parseSettings` allows `{name}` and `{owner}` once each in a source's `repoRoot`, `{owner}` only with `{name}`, and neither in `sources.adhoc.repoRoot` or a schedule's `repoRoot`. When a `{name}` pattern gave the session's folder, the `start-session` action names the repository in `clone`, and the hub fails the start with `No clone of <repo> at <path>.` when the folder has no `.git`; any other folder that does not exist fails with the runner's `The directory <path> was not found.`

## Playbooks

A playbook is an entry under `playbooks` in settings.json, keyed by its name, `<namespace>:<name>`; `template` holds its prompt template and the other keys its settings. Switchboard has no built-in namespaces or playbooks; the entries define them.

```json
"playbooks": {
  "support:defect": {
    "description": "Zoho ticket reporting behavior that may be a defect",
    "autoStart": false,
    "requiresWorktree": false,
    "permissionMode": "auto",
    "reports": ["done"],
    "template": "Research Zoho ticket {{ticket.sourceKey}}.\n{{ticket.context}}\nReport progress with `{{report}} stage {{session.id}} <stage>`."
  }
}
```

- Template variables: `{{ticket.source}}`, `{{ticket.sourceKey}}`, `{{ticket.ref}}`, `{{ticket.repo}}` (the ticket's repository, `owner/name` in lowercase, its source's home repository when it records none, empty for a source without repositories), `{{ticket.number}}` (the text after the last `#` of `ref` for a ticket with a repository, else empty), `{{ticket.title}}`, `{{ticket.url}}`, `{{ticket.context}}`, `{{session.id}}` and `{{report}}`. `{{report}}` is the report command prefix, `<node> "<home>/hub.js" report`, where `<node>` is `sessions.nodePath`. Rendering fails on an unknown variable.
- Keys: `description` (required, one line), `template` (required), `autoStart` (default false), `requiresWorktree` (default false), `provider` (`claude` or `codex`; default `sessions.provider`, PRD R22), `permissionMode` and `model` (defaults from the `sessions` settings of the playbook's agent; a Codex playbook's mode must be `auto`, `plan`, `dontAsk` or `bypassPermissions`), `kind` (`task` by default, or `triage`), `reports` (a task playbook's list of `done`, `approval`, `worktree`, `playbook` and `paste`; none by default), `approvalSends` (Approve sends the session's work, which sets the approval row's mark; false by default, and allowed only when `reports` lists `approval`), `rates` (a triage playbook's classifier rates immediacy and severity, which its body must define; false by default), `returnOf` (a task playbook's list of playbooks whose earlier work items Returned also counts, PRD R51; each a valid name other than its own, none by default), `focus` (a task playbook's page, `ticket` or `pr`, that triage's focus must be on, PRD R79; any page by default), and `workdir` (`worktree` by default: the ticket's worktree when one exists, otherwise the repo root; or `repoRoot`: always the source's repo root, for playbooks whose skills belong to that repository). Any other key, or a value of the wrong JSON type, leaves the playbook out. `parsePlaybooks` in core reads each entry on its own, in name order, which is the order the classifier lists them, so an invalid playbook is left out with its error, `<name>: <error>` in the dock, and the rest load.
- `description` tells the classifier when to choose the playbook. Adding a playbook is adding its entry: the hub reloads settings.json, and the classifier sees its description. Edit the triage playbook only for a rule that must take precedence over the descriptions.
- A triage playbook's body may use only `{{playbooks}}`, which renders the task playbooks in the source's namespace as `- name: description` lines where the instructions need them. A body without it gets the same list appended under "Choose one playbook:". Any other variable fails the triage, and the settings editor refuses to save it.
- A source's triage playbook has `kind: triage`. Its body is the classifier's system prompt. Triage playbooks are never offered to the classifier as choices.
- Each source's `namespace` setting limits the classifier's choices to that namespace's task playbooks, such as `dev` for GitHub and `support` for Zoho Desk and Forum.
- Every Claude session gets the report contract as `--append-system-prompt`, and every Codex session as `-c developer_instructions=…` on each run, apart from its prompt, so the session list shows only the prompt, and each gets it again from the contract hook after each compaction (mechanics M30, M31). A Codex session's contract offers the `question` report, with its question also written as its final message, in place of `AskUserQuestion`. The contract opens with the line `Report to Switchboard with these commands:`, which skills may look for, so it does not change: the commands the session may send, with the session ID, each with its condition and the cap on its text (PRD R23). The contract leaves out `task`, which the hub still accepts, so a session sends one only through a skill the operator invokes (PRD R68). A playbook's task session gets `stage`, `link`, `task` and the playbook's `reports`; with neither `done` nor `approval`, the contract tells it to end its turn when the work is finished, so the work item becomes `finished`, in Ready for review. Setup and approved-prompt sessions get `stage`, `worktree`, `done`, `link` and `task`, and an ad-hoc prompt gets all but `playbook`. The session row stores its reports, and the hub refuses any other report with "This session's report contract does not offer the <kind> report." and CLI exit code 3. It reports a `link` for each GitHub issue or pull request that tracks or delivers the work, not a related, base or parent one. Before applying a `link`, the hub calls the ticket's source's `checkLink`, when it has one: the GitHub source reads a pull request in its configured repository (`PULL_REQUEST_QUERY`) and refuses it unless it closes the issue in that repository or its head branch names the issue by `branchPattern`, which covers a stacked pull request that does not close the issue, and refuses a pull request number GitHub cannot resolve. A pull request URL is recognised by `new URL` on `http` or `https` and the host `github.com`, so a port, a tab such as `/files` or a fragment does not skip the check. A pull request that closes more issues than the 50 one read lists, none of them the ticket, is left unchecked. The hub logs the refusal with `Runtime.refuseReport` and the CLI exits 3. Other links, other sources, a failed check and a check over `REPORT_CHECK_MS` accept the link, the last two with a log line (PRD R52). When the session needs the operator for a question or a choice, or cannot continue without them, it asks with `AskUserQuestion` and waits, which keeps its context live and gives the row the Answer a question mark as `waiting`, in Blocked on you unless the work item has been ready; it writes no file and sends no report for a question. When its work is ready but posting or publishing it needs the operator's approval, it reports `approval` with the prompt that would do it, such as `/review-pr pr:123 publish`, a summary of what the prompt publishes, and the file holding the drafted text, if any, and ends its turn; Approve runs the prompt in a new session with no memory of this one, so the prompt names what it acts on. Before an approval, a session offered `paste` reports each drafted text the operator pastes into the source by hand, such as a Support customer reply, with its label and file. `{{report}}` lets a template add its own report calls.

## Triage

Triage follows Ground Control's classifier (GC R38, M31).

1. The source plugin builds the triage context: it fetches the ticket's text, clips each field at a word boundary to a size limit, and formats it with authors, status changes and assignees. Ground Control clips the issue body and each comment to 2,000 characters. The hub puts a `Current time: YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM UTC` line before the context, in the form sources use for event times, so the triage playbook can tell how recent a request is.
2. The hub chooses the classifier from `sessions.provider` before reading the source context. `triage.model` is its optional model override; `triageModel` drops recognized names belonging to the other provider, logs the fallback and records a null model on the triage. Unknown names pass through. The configured value is preserved. For Claude, the classifier runs `claude -p --output-format json --json-schema <schema> --no-session-persistence --setting-sources "" --tools "" --strict-mcp-config --system-prompt <triage playbook with the playbook descriptions rendered in, then, when the context has anchors, how to choose the focus>`, with the context on stdin. Stdin avoids the Windows 32,767-character command-line limit (M31). Because `--setting-sources ""` also skips the `env` block of Claude's user settings (M3), `claudeRouting` passes that block's `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `HTTP_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY` and `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` string values in the run's environment, over the hub's own. Model variables stay out, so triage keeps its model; Bedrock and Vertex are not carried, because they need further provider-specific variables.
   For Codex, `classifyCodex` runs `codex exec --json --ephemeral --ignore-user-config --ignore-rules --skip-git-repo-check` in a temporary folder under the Switchboard home, with the configured Codex executable and environment. It uses a read-only sandbox and no approvals, disables project instructions, web search, shell tools, apps and hooks, and sends the triage prompt and context on stdin. `--output-schema` and `--output-last-message` name temporary files removed after success or failure. `codexRouting` parses `config.toml` and carries the model endpoint that `--ignore-user-config` drops (M42) as `-c` overrides placed before the classifier's own: `model_provider`, `openai_base_url`, and the selected provider's `model_providers` entry written as one inline table, so its credentials appear in the process's arguments. A missing settings file or `config.toml` carries nothing; one that cannot be read or parsed fails triage without running the classifier, because the run would bypass the operator's endpoint, and the error names the file without quoting it. A failed or incomplete turn, process error, timeout or invalid result fails triage; it does not retry on Claude.
3. The schema is `{playbook: enum of the source namespace's task playbook names}`, plus `immediacy?: URGENT | BLOCKING | NON-BLOCKING` and `severity?: HIGH | MED | LOW` when the triage playbook has `rates: true` (PRD R50), and `focus?: enum of the context's anchor ids` when the source returns anchors (PRD R79). Claude reads `structured_output`; Codex reads its last-message file and requires every schema property. Both use core's `validateClassification`. Without `rates`, it discards any rating the model returns; a value outside the enum is stored as empty.
4. The focus (PRD R79): a source's `ContextResult.anchors` lists `{id, url, page}` for each page and comment it tags in the context, such as `[c3]`; the GitHub source tags the issue `[ticket]`, its pull request `[pr]`, and every comment with a URL `c1`, `c2`… by `createdAt` across the issue, pull request and review-thread comments, ties in section order. Zoho Desk and Forum return none, so their schema has no `focus`. A choice whose playbook has `focus` renders as `- name: description (page: pull request)` or `(page: ticket)`. The hub sends the chosen id and the anchors with `triage-succeeded`, and the flow stores `focusUrl(anchors, id, playbook.focus)` in `Triage.focus`: the chosen anchor when it is on the playbook's page or the playbook has none, else the playbook's page anchor, else the ticket's.
5. Nothing new (PRD R80): the core's `triage` action carries `handled`, `{at, playbook}` of the ticket's other work item with the latest `at`, where `at` is the `startedAt` of its first task session with a `shortId`, so a start that failed does not count, or its `doneAt` when the operator marked it done without a session, or of a handled-assignment registration (PRD R85) at its event time with the reporter's playbook, recorded as `reportedByPlaybook`; null for a pool triage or when no work item or registration qualifies. A source's `ContextResult.activityAt` is the time of the latest activity by anyone but the operator that can ask for an action, null when there is none, and omitted by a source that does not report it. The GitHub source takes the latest `createdAt` of the issue, pull request and review-thread comments and `submittedAt` of the pull request's reviews, and the `committedDate` of its head commit, which the pull request line shows as `Head commit: <login>, <time>`, whose author is not one of `logins`, bots included, with a commit whose author has no GitHub account counted as someone else's; Zoho Desk and Forum omit it. Only activity the context holds counts, so a non-operator comment clipped out by five later issue comments is missed; a reply in a review thread past its first three comments counts through its review. A rebase updates `committedDate`, and a pushed commit made long before the push keeps its old date. With `handled`, the hub puts an `Earlier work: …` line after the current time naming the earlier playbook and time, which makes earlier activity background. When `nothingNew(handled, activityAt)` holds, `activityAt` is null or no later than `handled.at`, compared to the millisecond, the line adds that nothing is newer and the hub sends `stale: true` with `triage-succeeded`; the flow then leaves the work item `unstarted` whatever the playbook's `autoStart`, with `error` set to `MESSAGES.nothingNew`, which the row's line 3 shows and Start clears, and logs `stale` in the event's detail, which History shows.

Triage has no tools, settings, MCP servers or saved transcript, so it is not a session and does not count toward the one-session-in-a-turn rule (PRD R17). The hub runs at most one triage per work item at a time, at most `triage.concurrency` at once (default 2), with a `triage.timeoutSeconds` limit (default 180).

A classifier error, an unparsable result, a timeout, or a playbook name outside the enum fails the work item with that error (PRD R37). A triage interrupted by a hub shutdown is not a failure: the work item stays `triaging`, and triage runs again when the hub starts.

## Flow

1. Poll a source. For each ticket, compare its assigned state with `Ticket.assigned`. A poll that fails or is incomplete, such as one that hits the page limit, updates nothing.
2. When a ticket enters the assigned state, create a work item (`triaging`) and run triage.
3. Store the triage result. If the playbook's `autoStart` is false, or the triage found nothing new (PRD R80), the work item is `unstarted` until the operator starts it (`start`). `awaiting-approval` is used only for an approval report, and `approve` applies only to it.
4. If the playbook's `requiresWorktree` is true and the ticket has none, start a setup session (`setup`) running the source's `setupPrompt`. The setup session succeeds when a worktree is linked, by report or by the branch convention, and the session's process ends, or, for a released session, its turn ends (`setupState`), since an interactive tab keeps the process. A Code Mode setup session (PRD R95) also succeeds at its turn end with a worktree linked, since its process keeps running between turns, unless the turn stopped at a usage limit ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)). If its process ends with no worktree linked while the ticket is assigned, the work item fails; a turn end without one leaves it in `setup`. The failure reads "Setup ended without a worktree."; a later resume of that session (`running` or `waiting` after `ended` or a turn end) returns the work item to `setup`.
5. Render the playbook and start the task session (`running`). `sessionPrompt` fills its session ID and then appends `WorkItem.guidance`, when set, after a blank line; an approved prompt takes none (PRD R77). It reports stages, a worktree link if it creates one, and done or approval.
6. When the task session reports done, the work item is done with reason `session`. When its turn or process ends with no report, the work item is `blocked`, with the done or approval report the prompt offered as its reason, or `finished` when the prompt offered neither (`endRun`). A turn that ends `failed` blocks it with the error kind, because the session can still be resumed. A `blocked` or `finished` work item whose session runs again becomes `running`. A Code Mode or Codex app-server task turn that ends `idle` while `FlowContext.questionCard` finds an unanswered question card in its conversation (`questionCardOpen` in `runtime.ts`) instead stores the session's `stateDetail` as `question-card` and leaves the work item `running`, waiting on the operator, unless the session reported, the ticket left the assigned state, an approval is held for the turn or the work item is a hosted conversation; the next turn clears the detail, an `idle` reading once the card is answered settles the turn as above, and the session's end, holder loss included, settles it as `ended` does (PRD R103).
   - When a session of an `awaiting-approval` work item changes to `running` with no detail, as when the operator answers its question or resumes it, the work item becomes `running` with `approvalHeld` set and keeps its approval fields. A change to `waiting` leaves it in `awaiting-approval`. A new approval or done report from that run applies as usual. A turn end, a process end or an operator stop with no report returns it to `awaiting-approval` with the kept approval, and `error` holds the cause of a failed turn or a stop. Any other status, such as `blocked` after a done report while the ticket is assigned, keeps the approval fields; only Approve, or Retry after the approved run failed to start, starts their prompt. Core clears `approvalHeld` on every other status change. A run whose `running` state Switchboard never sees change, such as a resume between two roster reads with hooks off, holds nothing.
7. When a ticket leaves the assigned state, an open work item that never had a session is deleted with its triages and pastes, and one with no active session is done with reason `unassigned` and no warning. A sessionless work item whose triage is running gets `unassignedAt` instead, so the triage row keeps its `triage.concurrency` slot; the triage's success, failure or cancellation deletes it, unless the ticket returned and cleared `unassignedAt`. Work item IDs are `AUTOINCREMENT`, so a deleted ID is never reused, and a late result for a deleted triage finds no row and is ignored. With an active session, that is deferred until the session reports or ends (PRD R10).

Pool tickets (PRD R47, R48):

- When a poll shows a ticket newly in the pool, the core starts a pool triage for the ticket; its result or error stays on the ticket.
- A pick of a pool ticket whose triage has a result creates the work item with that playbook, attaches the pool triage, and queues it as an operator start, skipping the wait in step 3. Switchboard does not claim the ticket (PRD R48).
- A ticket that enters the assigned state while it has an open work item gets no second work item; the claim made by the task session is seen this way.

An ad-hoc prompt creates a ticket and an `unstarted` work item, with the typed prompt as the task prompt and the chosen repo root as the working directory; Start then goes to step 5. The `adhoc` action's `start: true`, sent when the operator checks Start automatically, makes the work item `queued` at once, so it goes straight to step 5 (PRD R6). A `task` report creates the same ticket and `unstarted` work item, with its `parentId` set (PRD R68). A `register` event creates the same ticket and work item, `running`, with no task prompt and the session's working directory as the repo, and the session already started, released and external (PRD R81). A task report's ticket has `sourceId` `task`, and Delete sets it on each follow-up of the work item it deletes, so the origin outlasts `parentId`. With no playbook, the row's playbook slot is `adhocLabel` in `group.ts`: `task` when `parentId` is set or the ticket's `sourceId` is `task`, `registered` with no task prompt, else `ad-hoc`; History's section is titled the same way from the history's `prompted` flag.

Schedules (PRD R82) live in `packages/core/src/schedule.ts` and the flow's `runSchedules`. On each `tick`, before the event itself, `handle` walks `settings.schedules`. Each schedule's ticket has source `schedule` (`SCHEDULE_SOURCE`), key and ref its name, no URL, `assigned` true, the schedule's `repoRoot`, and the title of its playbook's description, else its name; the walk inserts it when first seen and updates its title and repo root when they change. `Ticket.lastPolledAt` is when the walk last checked the schedule, set at insert and on every tick, and `Ticket.sourceId` is `scheduleText(schedule)`, such as `every week on fri at 12:00` or `by hand`. A `sourceId` that differs from the schedule's text, because the schedule moved or was removed and added again, counts as no check: the walk stores the new text and the time and makes no run. A schedule ticket whose name settings.json no longer lists gets a null `sourceId`. History shows each change of it as `Runs <text>` or `The schedule was removed from settings.json`. `latestDue(schedule, now)` gives the latest local time at or before now that the schedule came due: today's or yesterday's time, the latest such weekday, or this or last month's day, cut to the month's last day; null for a schedule with no `every`. When it is later than the last check, the walk logs a `schedule` event with `{name, dueAt, skipped}` and, unless the ticket has an open work item (`skipped`), inserts a run: a work item with the schedule's playbook and no triage, `queued` when the playbook has `autoStart`, else `unstarted`, and `failed` with the cause when the playbook is missing or not a task playbook; the ticket's `scopeAt` becomes now, so the in-queue time counts from the run. A time before the schedule was first seen, moved or added again is not after the last check, so it does not run; one that passed while the hub was stopped is, so it runs once. The `run-schedule` action (Run now) refuses an unknown name and a ticket with an open work item, and otherwise inserts a `queued` run. A run starts in step 5 with `cwd` the ticket's `repoRoot`. The done report's source read finds no `schedule` source, so it is accepted unchecked (PRD R10); the snapshot counts no `returned` for it, and `retry` queues a failed run, which fails again while its playbook is missing or not a task playbook, as `startSession` checks.

Steps 4 and 5 start a session only when no other session of the ticket is `starting`, `running` or `waiting` and a slot is free; otherwise the work item is `queued` (PRD R17, R38). An `idle` or `failed` session, such as the approval session kept open in the VS Code Claude view, holds nothing back, so a work item can have several live sessions. Its active session is its latest session while that has not ended; only the active session holds a slot, and only the latest session changes the work item's status.

## Core decisions

`packages/core` decides; the hub acts. `handle(store, event, context)` runs inside one store transaction, applies one event, and returns the actions for the hub to run, or a refusal for an operator action or report that does not apply. The context carries the clock, settings, loaded playbooks, the report command prefix and a session ID generator. The hub runs each action and sends its result back as an event, so every failure reaches the work item.

| Events | From |
|---|---|
| `poll`, `poll-failed` | A source read. `poll` lists the source's tickets from a complete read; a ticket missing from it has left the assigned state. `poll-failed` changes nothing. |
| `triage-succeeded`, `triage-failed`, `triage-cancelled` | The classifier. A cancelled triage runs again. A triage that cannot start, because the source's `triagePlaybook` is not set or loaded, no task playbook matches, or the triage prompt does not render, is stored as a failed triage with the error, for a work item as for a pool ticket, so the work item counts as failed at triage. |
| `session-started`, `session-start-failed` | The runner's `start-session`. |
| `roster`, `marker` | A successful roster read, with each listed session's state, and `forks`, each session's newest live fork with its own state (PRD R78); a hook marker for a live session. The flow continues a session as its fork only when the session is its work item's latest, by `sessionsFor`, across providers and `starting` sessions, and holds no background process (`released` and not `releasing`); it then appends the native ID to `retiredIds`, sets `nativeSessionId` and `shortId` to the fork's, and applies the fork's state in place of the copy's. Otherwise the fork is ignored, a held one sets `pendingFork`, and the copy's own state applies; a read that looked for the session and lists no fork clears it. While `pendingFork` is set the row offers no Open in Claude and the flow refuses `open-question`, because it would open the copy the fork retires; Stop releases it. |
| `report` | The report command: `stage`, `worktree`, `done`, `approval`, `link`, `playbook`, `paste` or `task`. |
| `stop-succeeded`, `stop-failed` | The runner's `stop-session`, for an operator stop, a release, or Open in Claude (`open`). Each carries the `shortId` the stop targeted; a result for a short ID that is no longer the session's, because a fork retired that copy, changes nothing. A result with `leftovers`, of a Stop of an ended Code Mode session's leftover processes, clears `lastTree` on success, and on failure shows the error on the work item; it changes nothing else ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). An `open` stop that succeeds ends the session with outcome `opened`, keeping its `stateAt`, and leaves the work item's status as it was. The outcome is cleared when the roster lists the session as interactive and running or waiting, or the session reports; the roster ignores any other entry for the ended session, such as an idle tab or a background entry read before the stop. |
| `register` | The register command, through `Runtime.register` (PRD R81): a native session ID, the short ID and working directory from the Claude roster, and a title. It refuses an empty title and a native ID that is a session's current or retired one. `stop` refuses an external session. The session is a `task` session, `running`, `released` and `external`, offered the ad-hoc reports. It is logged with the operator as actor, because the operator ran the skill. |
| `background`, `move-launching`, `session-resumed`, `resume-failed`, `move-cancelled` | Continue in Background (PRD R89). `background`, through `Runtime.background`, names the session the hub found in the item window's Claude tab, its native ID, the tab process's `pid` and the window's work item IDs. It refuses a session that is not one of those work items' latest, has another native ID, is Codex or `external`, is not `released` or is `releasing`, has a `pendingFork` or a `move`, or is not `running` and was not listed `busy`. It sets `Session.move` (`{id, pid, phase: 'waiting', at, deadline: null}`, `id` from the session ID generator) and gives `resume-session`. The results carry the move's `id`; one for another attempt changes nothing. `move-launching` sets phase `launching` and its `deadline`. `session-resumed` clears `move`, sets `movedAt`, the short ID, `running`, and `released` and `releasing` false, so R39 releases the session again at its turn end. `resume-failed` clears `move` and ends the session with outcome `failed` and the cause "Continue in Background failed: <message>"; a setup session's end then decides setup (`ended`), an `awaiting-approval` work item or one with `approvalHeld` returns to `awaiting-approval` with the cause, and a `running` one is `blocked` with it. `move-cancelled`, always logged, clears `move` and leaves the session as it was. While `move` is set, roster entries, a missing entry, markers and fork adoption skip the session; reports apply. `stop` ends a move in phase `waiting` as a successful operator stop, with no action, and refuses one in phase `launching`. `delete` sends no stop for a moving session, which is released. |
| `ticket` | The ticket report, through `Runtime.ticket` (PRD R83): the session, the source and the ticket's key, ref, title and URL as the source read them, and the playbook or null. It refuses a session that is not its work item's latest, a work item whose ticket is not ad-hoc or that is done, a playbook that is not a task playbook in the source's `namespace` (any task playbook when the source has none), and a source ticket with a work item, triage, worktree or link; a source ticket with none, such as one a poll stored assigned to the operator on a status outside scope, is deleted with its activity (`Store.deleteBareTicket`). It updates the ad-hoc ticket in place: source, key, ref, title and URL from the event, `sourceId` null, `assigned` and `pool` false, `adhocKey` the old key, and `repoRoot` kept, so later sessions start where the ad-hoc ones did. Stored out of the assigned state, the ticket's first poll in scope finds its open work item and `createWorkItem` makes none. With a playbook, the work item takes it; an `external` session's `reports` become the playbook's, because its contract is only in its conversation, and a session Switchboard started keeps its own, which its system prompt holds. A repeat for the ticket the work item already has changes nothing. Logged with the session as actor, its entry hides the ticket's field changes. |
| `worktree-found` | The branch convention. It links a worktree only to a ticket that has none. |
| `held-resuming`, `held-resumed` | `Runtime.attach` (PRD R95). `held-resuming` `{sessionId, launchId}` stores the launch on an ended Code Mode session before the spawn and changes nothing else. `held-resumed` `{sessionId, launchId}` comes after `CodeModeRunner.resumeHeld` spawned an ended Code Mode session again, or when the hub finds an ended Code Mode session live in the holder, with its stored launch. It makes the session `idle` with that launch and clears `endedAt`, `released`, `releasing` and `lastTree`; outcome and work item status stand. Each is ignored for a session that is gone, not Code Mode or not `ended` ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). |
| `holder-lost`, `held-idle` | The hub (PRD R94, R95, [Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). `holder-lost`, for each active session of a terminal holder that is gone, ends the session with "The terminal holder stopped.": a session in a turn or waiting blocks its work item, a `starting` one fails its start, and one whose turn had ended is `released` with its work item's status and `approvalHeld` kept. `held-idle`, for a held session the holder lists with no terminal attached and no input or output for the idle limit, ends it like a release after its turn, or in any state while hooks are off; for a Codex app-server thread whose turn ended the idle limit ago, from `Runtime.wake`, it releases the subscription; neither while the session waits on its usage limit. |
| `limit-found`, `limit-attempt`, `limit-skipped` | The hub ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)). `limit-found` stores a limit read after the turn end's marker; `limit-attempt` records that the hub typed the continue text, or why it could not; `limit-skipped` ends the wait of a session the operator typed in. A `marker` can carry the limit its turn ended at, and each `tick` gives up on waits whose attempts did not start a turn. All are always logged. |
| `move`, `reset-position` | The dock (PRD R73). Both apply only to a work item in Ready for review, open and unsnoozed, and `move` only after another such work item or null. `move` lists `readyWorkItems`, every open work item with `readyAt`, snoozed and blocked ones included, without the moved one, and sets its `orderAt`: the midpoint of the keys (`orderAt ?? readyAt`) around its new place, the first key − 60 000 ms at the top, the midpoint of the last key and now at the bottom. When the keys before and after are under 2 ms apart, it first sets every listed work item's `orderAt` to `now − (n − i) × 1000`, keeping the order. `reset-position` clears `orderAt`. |
| `comment` | The dock (PRD R87): `{workItemId, text}`. It applies to any work item, open or done, stores the trimmed text with the event's time in `comments`, and refuses a blank text with "The comment is empty." |
| `schedule` | Logged by `runSchedules` on a `tick`, not sent: with `{name}` for its ticket's insert or change, and with `{name, dueAt, skipped}` when a schedule came due. |
| `adhoc`, `run-schedule`, `start`, `approve`, `mark-done`, `reopen`, `retry`, `stop`, `delete`, `open-question`, `pick`, `retriage-pool`, `snooze`, `restore`, `tick` | The dock; `tick` re-runs scheduling after settings or playbooks change, and every minute wakes due snoozes and checks the schedules. `run-schedule` carries `{name}` and logs under the schedule's ticket. `stop` with `sessionId` (PRD R94) stops that Code Mode session of the work item, which must not have ended, as Stop stops the active one; for an older session it gives `stop-session` with reason `operator` for it alone, which ends it as stopped without changing the work item. `retry` (PRD R34) applies to a `failed` work item with no active session: one that failed at triage (`failedAtTriage`) becomes `triaging` with its error cleared; any other is queued (`queue`), keeping its playbook, approval fields, guidance and `readyAt`, so `startSession` runs setup, the approved prompt or the task session. `open-question` applies only while the active session is the one the row showed (its native session ID), runs in its background process (started, with a short ID, not released) and is `waiting` (PRD R70). `reopen` (PRD R86) applies to a done work item that is its ticket's latest, with no open work item and no `handledAssignmentKey` on the ticket (`reopenRefusal`, which also sets the row view's `reopenable`); it clears the latest session's outcome, except `opened`, so its turn end and resume settle the work item again. `done` keeps `approvalHeld` on the work item when it ends without a done report, settled or awaiting its recheck, while it is `awaiting-approval`, holds an approval, or is `queued` with an `approvalPrompt` (an approved run not yet started); Reopen then returns it to `awaiting-approval`, or to `running` with `approvalHeld` while its task session is in a turn. A done report clears it, because it covers the publication. `delete` (PRD R76) applies to any work item: it refuses while the active session is `starting`, gives `stop-session` with reason `operator` for an active session that is not released (PRD R95: for a Code Mode session it neither refuses while `starting` nor skips a released session the holder lists live, and the stop names the Switchboard session ID), and calls `Store.deleteWorkItem`, which deletes the work item's triages, sessions, pastes and logged activity and clears `parentId` on its children. It ends the ticket's snooze when the ticket has no work item left and is not in the pool. Its event names only the ticket, so History keeps it, with any event whose ticket-owned changes, such as a link or worktree a deleted session reported, survive the purge; a later event for a deleted session is refused as from an unknown session and not stored, and the runtime logs a failed stop of a deleted session. |

Actions are `triage`, `start-session`, `stop-session` and `resume-session`. `open-question` gives `stop-session` with reason `open`; `background` gives `resume-session` with the move's `id`, the tab process's `pid` and `RESUME_PROMPT`. After every event the core starts triage for `triaging` work items, then pool triage for pool tickets without a result, up to `triage.concurrency` in all; then it starts `queued` work items in queue order while fewer than `sessions.concurrency` active sessions hold a slot (each work item's active session holds one while `holdsSlot` holds for its work item, `setup` or `running` with no `waiting` active session, and its ticket is not snoozed), skipping a ticket that has another session `starting`, `running` or `waiting` (PRD R17). A task that follows a setup session starts when the setup session ends, without waiting for a slot, and for a Code Mode setup session when its turn ends with a worktree linked (PRD R95).

## Claude Code runner

- Start: `claude --bg --permission-mode <mode> --settings '{"worktree":{"bgIsolation":"none"}}' -n <name> [--model <model>] --append-system-prompt <contract> <prompt>`, with no shell, so a leading slash command survives. Claude records the system prompt with the contract and sends that record on later turns and resumes, also from a process started without the flag, until a compaction; a woken background session reuses its saved flags (mechanics M30). The name is `switchboard · <playbook> · <source> <key>`. Claude's background service owns the process, so sessions survive VS Code and the hub. `--bg` ignores `--session-id`; the runner parses the short ID from `backgrounded · <id> · <name>` and matches it as a prefix against `claude agents --json` (GC M1, M33; mechanics M5). The start is confirmed when the roster lists it with a pid; a new entry has no pid for its first seconds. A session listed `blocked` with no pid on two reads in a row is waiting on a prompt before it starts, such as approving the project's MCP servers (mechanics M41): the start succeeds with `startPrompt`, and the core stores the session as `waiting` with the state detail `START_PROMPT` (`@switchboard/core/group`). The roster lists a gone process the same way, so `observe` keeps a `blocked` entry without a pid alive only for a session stored so; once the operator answers through Open in terminal, the pid appears and the roster and markers decide its state. Hook markers do not change it until then. The rows offer Open in terminal as its main control and no Open in Claude, and the core refuses `open-question` for it. A process that dies at the prompt leaves the same entry, so that session waits until the operator stops it. Otherwise a session not listed with a pid within 60 seconds fails the start.
- Permission mode: `auto` by default. In 101 Ground Control runs to 2026-10-03, the 95 in `auto` never waited for or were refused a permission; the only permission stall was in manual mode. Questions (`AskUserQuestion`) stall in every mode and show as `waiting`. The operator answers them, so Switchboard neither disallows the tool nor keeps a Bash allowlist.
- CLI: each `claude` command the hub runs (starts, stops, roster reads, triage, debriefs) runs `sessions.claudePath`, read at each use: `claude` by default, looked up on the hub's PATH. An absolute path's directory goes first on that command's PATH (`executableDirOnPath`), as for Codex. A command that cannot find the CLI reports `Claude Code was not found at <path>. Install it or set sessions.claudePath.`; one whose working directory is missing reports that directory instead. Open in terminal runs `sessions.claudePath` in the item window's terminal, which looks a bare name up on that window's PATH; the hub adds it to the terminal command as `executable` when a window claims the command, so a window gets the setting as it is then. An absolute path's directory goes first on the terminal's PATH, as for the hub's own runs.
- Environment: pass `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` through exactly as the hub inherited it, and never set it. Setting it, even to `~/.claude`, moves where Claude reads folder trust (GC M62). Strip `ELECTRON_*`, `VSCODE_*` and `NODE_OPTIONS`.
- Trust: `--bg` exits 1 with `Workspace not trusted` in a directory Claude has not trusted; a linked worktree inherits its main checkout's trust (GC M62). The failed start shows that message (PRD R41).
- Background sessions inherit the service's environment, not the caller's, so per-run values such as the Switchboard session ID travel in the prompt (M33).
- Liveness: poll `claude agents --json` (without `--all`) every 30 seconds for every session a start confirmed, so a session resumed after its release is seen again. The roster alone decides liveness: a session is active while the last successful roster read lists it with a pid, and `ended` once a successful read that looked for it does not, so a start confirmed during a read is not ended by it. An entry without a pid is a process that is gone (GC M33). A failed read changes no session. Reads run one at a time; a read asked for during another runs once that one finishes, so an older result never ends a session a newer read revived. Hook markers only set the state of a live session, because a killed process leaves its last marker behind (GC M20). The roster `state` is the fallback when no hook marker exists: `working` maps to `running`, `blocked` to `waiting`, and `done` or `stopped` to `idle`.
- State: hook markers decide the observed state, following GC's mapping (GC M2, M20). The hub watches `<home>/activity/` and reads all markers 50 ms after a change, and also every 5 seconds. When the folder is removed or replaced, or the watch fails, it watches again after 1 second. Each new marker from an ended session can mean the operator resumed it, so the hub reads the roster once for it instead of waiting for the 30-second timer, and reads it again when another such marker arrives during that read:
  - `UserPromptSubmit`, `PostToolBatch` and `PermissionDenied` mean `running`, as do `SessionStart` with `source=compact` and `PostToolUse` or `PostToolUseFailure` for `AskUserQuestion` or `ExitPlanMode`, which end a question's wait before the rest of its batch resolves. A result of another tool does not end a wait, because a parallel tool can finish while one still waits for permission. After the operator approves a permission, the session stays `waiting` until the approved tool's batch resolves.
  - `PermissionRequest`, `Elicitation`, `PreToolUse` for `AskUserQuestion` or `ExitPlanMode`, and `Notification` of type `permission_prompt`, `worker_permission_prompt` or `agent_needs_input` mean `waiting`.
  - A wait's detail tells a permission prompt from a question (mechanics M57): `PermissionRequest` for a tool other than `AskUserQuestion` or `ExitPlanMode`, and `worker_permission_prompt`, give `PERMISSION_PROMPT`; the others give none, except `permission_prompt`, which Claude sends after a question's permission request as well as a tool call's, so its detail comes from the marker's `promptTool`; a notification marker written before the writer recorded `promptTool` says nothing about state, so the roster decides. Once the roster lists a background session `blocked`, its `waitingFor` decides: `permission prompt` gives `PERMISSION_PROMPT`, any other value none.
  - `Stop` means `idle`, or `running` with the detail `turn ended with background tasks` while background tasks remain.
  - On an interactive roster entry, `SessionStart` with another source means `idle`: a tab or terminal that resumed the session has run no turn, though the roster lists it `busy` while it loads (mechanics M25).
  - `StopFailure` means `failed`, with its error kind and message; `rate_limit` is a usage or rate limit, whose reset the transcript gives ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)).
- Hooks:
  - The hub installs async command hooks for those events plus `SessionEnd` in `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR or ~/.claude>/settings.json`. It preserves other entries, refuses to edit malformed JSON, adds missing entries at startup, and gives its own entries `sessions.nodePath` as their command. Its own entries are those whose command names `node` or `node.exe` and whose only argument is its script, so an operator's entry that only mentions the script stays. At each install it also removes the entries of another Switchboard home, a script `hook.mjs`, `contract.mjs` or `codex-hook.mjs` in a folder named `.switchboard`, when no process holds that home's hub lock (`hubRunning` in `packages/core`). A hub takes its lock before it installs hooks, so a starting hub keeps its entries. The profile's own `~/.switchboard` is never removed, so an acceptance hub that shares the operator's settings leaves the operator's hooks while the operator's hub is down. A hub that stops leaves its entries; the next install by another hub removes them. The `sessions.installHooks` setting is on by default; when it is off, the roster alone decides state. Turning it on while the hub runs installs both agents' hooks at once; turning it off leaves them installed, stops reading their markers and clears the hook errors.
  - The hook runs `<sessions.nodePath> "<home>/hook.mjs"`. It skips subagent events, always exits 0 and prints nothing, and writes `<home>/activity/<nativeSessionId>.json` atomically: `{v, sessionId, event, at, cwd, notificationType, source, toolName, promptTool, backgroundTasks, error, errorMessage}`, with `errorMessage` capped at 500 characters. `promptTool` is a `PermissionRequest`'s tool, which a `Notification` right after it, or after such a notification, copies from the marker it replaces, taking an older permission request's `toolName` when it has no `promptTool`. `SessionEnd` deletes the marker.
  - A debrief fork runs with `FRICTION_DEBRIEF=1`, and the hook writes no marker for it.
  - The hub also installs a synchronous `SessionStart` hook with matcher `compact|resume` (`CONTRACT_MATCHER`; an entry installed with `compact` alone gets the new matcher, in a group of its own when its group holds other entries, which keep theirs), `<sessions.nodePath> "<home>/contract.mjs"` with a 10-second timeout, because compaction drops the contract from the system prompt and a process resumed without `--append-system-prompt`, such as a VS Code tab, rebuilds the prompt without it (mechanics M30). The hook skips subagent events and other sources, posts the session ID and `source` to `POST /contract` with the token from `<home>/hub.json`, waits up to 5 seconds, and prints `{hookSpecificOutput: {hookEventName: "SessionStart", additionalContext: <contract>}}`; Claude saves that context in the transcript after the compaction, so later resumes keep it. For another session, or when the hub does not answer, it prints nothing and exits 0. After a `resume` the hub answers only for a Code Mode session, with the conversation rules alone ([Conversation](#conversation)), because a resume keeps the prompt the session started with; after a compaction it answers a Code Mode session's contract followed by the conversation rules. With `sessions.installHooks` off, a session compacted outside its background process loses its contract.
- Forks (PRD R78, mechanics M36): `findForks` in `packages/claude/src/fork.ts` runs on each roster read. A candidate is a roster entry with a pid, not `background`, whose session ID is no session's current or retired native ID, in the project directory of a target: each work item's latest Claude session with a native ID (`forkTargets`). `relationOf` answers `newer`, `older` or `unrelated`: a retired ID is `older`; a copy belongs to the session's family when its first assistant `requestId` (outside subagents) is in the transcript of the current copy or of a retired one in the same project directory, and is `newer` when its transcript's birth time is after the current copy's. The newest `newer` copy of each session goes in the roster event's `forks`, with the state its marker gives, else the roster's.
  - `Transcripts` indexes each transcript once per pass: the path (`<projects>/<slug>/<id>.jsonl`, then the case-insensitive listing, or every project directory when the caller has no working directory), the birth time, and the request IDs, read incrementally from the last offset with an incomplete last line kept for the next read. A file with another identity (`ino` or birth time) or a shorter one is read again from the start; a rewrite in place to the same or a larger size is not detected, because Claude only appends. A transcript that is not found is looked for again after 60 seconds.
  - The hook marker records the session's `cwd`. A changed marker of a native ID that is no session's, in a Claude session's project directory, starts a roster read, so a fork is found at its first hook rather than the 30-second timer.
  - The report command sends `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, which Claude sets in the Bash tool's environment, as `nativeSessionId`. For a Claude session whose current copy it is not, `relation` decides: `older` refuses the report with "Session <id> was replaced by fork <shortId>, so the report was not recorded.", `newer` records it and starts a roster read, and `unrelated` records it. The hub checks again just before it applies the report, because a roster read can continue the session as a fork while the report's worktree, link or assignment check runs. A Codex session's report ignores it, because a Codex process can inherit it.
  - `POST /contract` resolves a native ID that is no session's through `forkOf`, so a fork that compacts before it is continued gets its session's contract.
- Release: when a session's turn ends (`idle` or `failed`), run `claude stop <id>` once (PRD R39). A `waiting` session is not released, because it is mid-turn. A session whose background process has ended, by release, stop, failed start or leaving the roster, is never released again, and neither is a session the roster lists as interactive, so a session the operator resumed in a VS Code tab is never stopped by Switchboard. Before a release stop, the hub also reads the roster and skips the stop unless the session is listed as a background process. An interactive roster entry, such as a VS Code tab, has no `state`; its `status` (`idle`, `busy` or `waiting`) gives `idle`, `running` or `waiting`.
- Continue in Background (PRD R89, mechanics M55): `POST /background {label, hostPid, workItemIds}`, with `label` null from the Claude sidebar, from an item window, with the window's extension host PID, runs `Runtime.background`. `ClaudeRunner.tab` reads `claude agents --json --all` and lists the live entries that are not `background` whose `name` is the tab's label, or starts with it when the tab cut the label with `…`, each marked by whether a `kind: background` entry has the same `sessionId`, and keeps those whose process's parent is `hostPid` (`parentPids`: `/proc/<pid>/stat`, or a CIM query on Windows), because a Claude tab's or sidebar's process is a child of its window's extension host. A null label, from the sidebar, takes every such entry as a candidate. Another live interactive session under `hostPid`, whatever its name, such as one in the window's Claude sidebar, refuses, because the window's close would end it; several candidates refuse; one whose session is not one of the window's work items refuses; one without a background entry refuses, because only a session that ran `claude --bg` has saved options to resume with.
