The selection starts at the primary, else the newest conversation; a successor work item that replaces a selected primary is selected in its place, and a selected conversation that leaves the list gives way to the primary. `scopeGen` rises whenever the selected conversation or its current session changes, by `select`, a new conversation, a successor primary or a newer session. Within the selected conversation the window sees its current session only, the one started last.
- **Folder windows.** A window with no workspace file that opens `/events?folder=<path>` is a folder window, bound to the folder's canonical path, its real path (`canonicalFolder` in `windows.ts`, `realpathSync.native`), so another spelling of the same folder, in another case or through a link or junction, reaches the same binding; a path that is not a readable folder, a folder whose name ends in `.code-workspace`, or a window that names none, is bound to nothing and sees no work item. Known limit: a folder named `*.code-workspace` cannot host conversations, because a host link names a folder or an item window's file in one column and `isFolderHost` tells them apart by that suffix. A folder window has no primary work item: its conversations are only the ad hoc work items whose `Ticket.hostWindow` is that canonical folder, compared by `windowFileKey`, never the item work in the folder, even when an item window's worktree or the Support folder is the same folder, and item windows never list them. Its scope has `kind: 'folder'`, `folder` the canonical folder and `title` its last name, and is sent even with no conversation, with empty `conversations` and null `workItemId`, `cwd` and `sessionId`, so the view offers New conversation…. Bindings and host links are compared by `hostKey` (`packages/core`): `windowFileKey` for an item window's file, and for a folder host the same except that it keeps its case off Windows and macOS, so two Linux folders that differ only in case stay apart; a drive root or `/` compares without its trailing separator. `isFolderHost` tells the two kinds of host apart: an item window's is always a `.code-workspace` file.
- **State.** A window stream's `state` is `WindowScopes.scoped`: the snapshot with the window's conversations' work items alone, no pool, no held sessions, no source notes or source errors, which can name other tickets, and `needsCount`, how many work items need the operator, without naming them.
- **History.** `/history` answers the primary's ticket, a conversation's ticket, or a ticket whose History command the window claimed through `/claim`, which names the window key (`WindowScopes.grant`); a claim that names no key a connected window holds leaves History commands queued for the next one. It refuses any other with 403 "Not in this window.".
- **Opens and claims.** A pending open names its destination window, an item window's workspace file or a folder window's canonical folder, and `/claim` hands it only to the window whose key's binding is that destination, never by a folder or a workspace the claim names; the `claim` nudge reaches only that window's streams ([Claude Code runner](#claude-code-runner)). So a Support window, or a folder window on an item's worktree, never claims another work item's terminal.
- **Moved to the dock.** Notifications of work items that start needing the operator and the needs count leave the windows for the dock ([Dock](#dock)). Open in Switchboard asks the hub for the one row the operator's URL names ([VS Code extension](#vs-code-extension)).
- The window key separates windows; the hub token stays a machine credential, so the rule is not a defence against software running as the operator. The dock's and the CLI's routes (`/dock/events`, `/holder/*`, `/config/*`) stay global; the extension does not call them.

### Hosted conversations

An item window's or a folder window's New conversation… and Switchboard: New Conversation… start an ad hoc conversation in the window, and the dock's New conversation in a folder one in a folder (voice-design § 3.1, slices 6a to 6c), a work item like any other, with a dock row and the usual controls.

- **Making one.** `POST /v1/conversation/new-conversation` sends the flow event `new-conversation {hostWindow, folder, provider?}`: an ad hoc ticket titled "New session" (`NEW_CONVERSATION_TITLE`) with `repoRoot` the window's first folder, read from its workspace file, or a folder window's canonical folder, and `hostWindow` the window's binding, its workspace file or canonical folder, and a `running` work item whose task session starts at once, without waiting for a free slot. The session uses the chosen provider, falling back to `sessions.provider` for a caller that omits it: a promptless Codex app-server thread (R107), or Claude in Code Mode. For Claude, the event is refused with "New sessions need Code Mode (sessions.codeMode)." while Code Mode is off and with "Code Mode sessions need sessions.installHooks." while hooks are off. It starts with no prompt (`Session.promptless`), and its contract offers no `done` or `approval` report.
- **Provider choice.** New Conversation… and the Conversation view's plus read `POST /v1/conversation/providers {}` and open a native Claude/Codex picker with its `defaultProvider` preselected. Cancellation sends no creation request. The dock uses the same metadata for its folder-session picker and the Provider field in New ad-hoc session; the folder-session picker is skipped when `installed` holds one provider, which it then uses. An explicit choice is stored as nullable `WorkItem.provider`; queued starts and retries preserve it, while old work items keep the default. A work item with a playbook starts its sessions in the playbook's provider, else `sessions.provider`, the provider its permission mode and model were checked against when it loaded; the work item's choice applies only while it has no playbook, so a chosen Codex work item that a ticket report moves onto a playbook starts its next session in that playbook's provider. Changing this choice never changes settings or an existing session.
- **Idle start.** `codeModeStartArgs` leaves out an empty prompt, so the session waits at an empty input box; a promptless session's `SessionStart` `startup` reads as idle (`heldState`), and `session-started` makes it idle, not running (mechanics M87). The contract and the conversation rules reach it only through `--append-system-prompt` and, on resume and compaction, the contract hook.
- **Open between turns.** A hosted conversation's turn end and its session's end leave its work item `running`, never `finished` or blocked for a missing report; a failed turn still blocks it. It holds a slot only while its session is in a turn (`heldSlots`), so an idle conversation never holds back the queue. Its row stays in In progress, with the `idle` mark and no turn while its promptless session is idle or ended, a question card included (PRD R103); `markOf` and `turnOf` distinguish an open work item from a working session. It ends by Mark done, Delete, or the held session's idle end (R95), after which Open in terminal resumes it.
- **Title.** The first message added to its conversation, typed or sent in the terminal, titles it by its first line (flow event `conversation-title`), while it is still "New session".
- **Resume before a message.** A promptless session that ended before its first message has no transcript, which `claude --resume` refuses (mechanics M87), so `resumeHeld` starts it again under its session ID with its contract and no prompt when Claude keeps no transcript of it.
- **Its window.** A hosted work item has no window of its own: `windowOf` returns its host's, marked `hosted`, so the dock and the extension open the host window and never write its workspace file, a folder-hosted one's by its folder, and a row click first posts `POST /dock/select {rowKey}`, which stores the conversation, done or not, as the host binding's selection, or selects it in a connected host window, raising its `scopeGen`, and answers `{selected}`, false for a conversation no binding hosts. An item window closes on its own only when its primary and every hosted conversation are done (`DoneWindow`); a folder window never closes on its own.
- **From the dock.** `POST /dock/new-conversation {folder, opId, provider?}` (`ConversationService.dockConversation`) needs no connected window: it canonicalizes `folder` as a folder window's is (`canonicalFolder`), sends `new-conversation` with that canonical folder as both host and folder, stores the new work item as that folder binding's selection, making the binding when none is stored yet (`WindowScopes.selectMade`), and selects it in the folder windows open on it, never in an item window that holds the folder, and answers `{workItemId, folder}` with the canonical folder. A repeated `opId` answers the first work item and makes none, remembered with the windows' `new-conversation` by canonical folder, so another spelling of the folder answers it too; the last 100 are kept, in memory only. It answers 400 for a malformed body or a path that is not a folder a session can run in, and 409 with the flow's refusal, storing nothing. The dock's **New conversation in a folder…** in More Actions ([Dock](#dock)) asks for a folder with the system folder picker and a provider with the default preselected, posts both with a new UUID as `opId`, sends the same request once more when its answer is lost (`HubClient.NewConversation`), and then opens the canonical folder as a folder-hosted row's click does (`Switcher.OpenFolder`): `Code.exe <folder>`, waiting up to 20 seconds for a folder window on it, with no `POST /open`, so nothing is left to claim and the window shows the conversation from its stored selection. It runs as a row's click does, so a row clicked while the hub answers replaces it: the conversation is made, but its window is not opened over that row's. A refusal shows on the dock's status line and opens nothing; choosing it again while the picker is open or the hub answers does nothing.

### Reading a session

`ConversationService` owns scope, delivery, events, unread, artifacts and speech. The Claude adapter owns the mechanics below; the Codex adapter uses native history and events ([Codex runner](#codex-runner)). `ConversationLog` stores authoritative entries for either provider and applies tags, statuses and rewrites.

`ConversationService` (`packages/hub/src/conversation.ts`) runs the conversation library (`packages/conversation`, ported from claude-collab's `wrapper.js`, `screen-reader.js`, `screen-hold.js` and `rewrite.js`) in the hub. Every 300 ms (`CONVERSATION_POLL_MS`), one read at a time, it reads each active Code Mode session that has a Claude session ID and is past `starting`, and a last time when one ends:

- **Transcript, the authority.** `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR or ~/.claude>/projects/<slug of cwd>/<Claude session ID>.jsonl` (`findTranscript`), followed through the ID changes the hub confirms (`/clear`, `/branch`, `/resume`, mechanics M73), so a nested run's transcript is never read. A checkpoint per session (`checkpoints`: file, byte offset after the last whole line, last entry UUID and time) lets a restarted hub resume where it stopped; an entry whose UUID the store already holds is never imported again, and a reply after a restart answers the last stored message. The first transcript of a session is read from its start. A new transcript after an ID change is read from its start when it holds nothing older than the last entry read (`/clear`), else from its end, because `/branch` and `/resume` copy history, with a note "Conversation continued from another session."
- **Read before a turn end.** `Runtime.send` calls `ConversationService.readNow` for a held session that a `marker` or `roster` event finds `idle` after a turn, which reads its transcript at once, since the marker read 50 ms after a hook (`MARKER_SETTLE_MS`) comes before the next poll; the flow then asks `questionCardOpen`, which applies `unansweredCard` (`packages/conversation/src/questions.ts`) to the session's items from its newest answering message on, paging back 100 at a time, and its newest status: a reply that is not an aside, a note, withdrawn or replaced by a rewrite, or the status, that asks lettered questions after the newest message that is not withdrawn, as the view draws and answers cards, except that a message still `open`, not yet typed, answers nothing, since it may come back not sent (PRD R103). A Codex app-server session's reply arrives with its `item/completed` event, before `turn/completed`, unless the adapter holds live items back while it follows the thread or imports its history (`CodexConversationAdapter.pending`); `Runtime.send` then holds that session's `idle` marker until `ConversationService.catchUp` has imported them, and drops it when the session's state changed meanwhile. `readMarkers` sends no `idle` marker for a session waiting on a card, whose marker reads `idle` until its next turn.
- **Entries.** An assistant line's text blocks are a reply, and the text lines of one API message (`message.id`), which Claude Code writes one per content block, are one reply, with its first line's place, UUID and answer; the store keeps each such reply's API message and lines (`apiMessageId`, `blocks`), so a line read after a hub restart still joins it and a line read again is not added twice; one with no text whose `thinkingDisplay` is `updates` is a reply saved as thinking, and its thinking text is the reply (claude-collab findings 2026-11-17). A user line whose content is text is a message, origin `terminal`, with the `pasted_content` tags of a pasted block removed, when its `origin.kind` is `human` or, before Claude Code 2.1.326, it has no `origin`; a slash command shows as `/name args`; an assistant line Claude Code made up (`isVirtual`, `madeBy: 'other'`, or the model `<synthetic>`), such as an added Poll call or "No response requested.", is neither a reply nor counted as one; tool results, meta, sidechain and compaction entries, command output, shell escapes, reminders and interruption notes are left out. A message the operator sends while Claude is busy is written twice (claude-collab findings 2026-11-19): a `queue-operation` `enqueue` line when it is sent, which adds it `provisional`, and, where Claude received it, a user line or, mid-turn, an `attachment` line of type `queued_command` whose `origin.kind` is `human` (or, with no `origin`, whose `commandMode` is `prompt`), which moves that message there and clears `provisional`. A queue line has no UUID, so the message's ID is made from its time and text, and a transcript read again from its start never adds it twice. A queue line that starts with `<` or `[Cross-session delivery notice]`, once a pasted block's tags are removed, is Claude Code's own, such as a task notification, and is left out; a sent message whose text Claude receives as a line that is left out, or that Claude has not received when the session ends, is `withdrawn`.
- **Order.** The conversation is in transcript line order, never by the lines' `timestamp`, which is not in order: an assistant line is stamped near when its block started and written when it finished, so it often follows a queue line stamped later (claude-collab findings 2026-11-19). The store gives each item the next `ord` when it is added and again when its transcript line is read, and lists the items in the transcript by `ord`, then the items not in it yet, a provisional message or a reply's screen copy, by `ord`, which is the order they were first seen; `at` is only shown. A withdrawn item counts as in the transcript, so it never holds back the items after it, and a page leaves withdrawn items out before its limit.
- **Screen, an early copy.** While a session works and for 5 seconds after, the hub reads its screen with the holder's `snapshot` (400 lines of scrollback, `SNAPSHOT_SCROLLBACK`), through the connection the roster reads keep and only from a holder that names `snapshot` in its features, and skips a revision it has read. `readSnapshot` replays it into a headless terminal and reads each line, with backticks put back around inline code drawn in Claude Code's code colours, and whether a line's `●` is drawn in the text colour, which tells a reply from a tool call or a Claude Code prompt. Code Mode sessions run in the classic renderer (`tui: "default"`), whose finished output scrolls into the scrollback. `ScreenReader` finds finished reply blocks, joins wrapped lines, grows a block still streaming in place, and after a size change takes the screen as read for a second.
- **Hold.** A screen copy waits up to 300 ms for its transcript copy, which is then published alone; one that comes later replaces the screen copy in place (`ScreenHold`). A screen copy that gets no transcript copy is withdrawn once an assistant line written more than a second after it lands without it, once the session has been idle for 5 seconds, or after 20 seconds; a withdrawn rewrite frees the reply it replaced.
- **Replies.** `parseReply` splits a reply into an aside or a reply, its rewrite tag, its text (`opening`), which the view shows whole, its status ([Statuses](#statuses)), its `[speak]` passage (`speak`, several joined, one never closed marking nothing, one in inline code or a fenced block only text; a marker alone on its line goes with its line, and a reply with none keeps its text as written) and its tone line (`tone`, `[tone: …]` alone on its first line, before or after a rewrite tag, cut to 120 characters), whose markers `opening` leaves out; a tag quoted as code or mentioned mid-sentence is a word, and any other bracketed word is text. A rewrite marks the newest earlier reply that is not an aside, withdrawn or rewritten (`rewrittenBy`, `replaces`). A reply `answers` the first operator's message line on its first line's `parentUuid` chain, past tool calls and other lines (claude-collab findings 2026-11-19: it matched the API requests for every reply checked), so several messages delivered together are answered by one reply, and one message can have several replies. The reader indexes each line's parent for the current transcript, and after a hub restart rebuilds the index from the lines before its checkpoint; when the chain still reaches no message, such as into history copied by `/branch`, the reply answers the latest message before it; a screen copy answers the latest message until its transcript line is read. A rewrite marks a reply before it in conversation order, chosen again when its transcript copy moves it into place. A session's end withdraws the screen copies still waiting. The `messages` and `replies` tables keep the newest 500 items per session, in conversation order, outside the activity log, and go with their work item.
- A window whose current session ended while the hub was not reading it gets the rest of its transcript read when its stream opens.

### Typing a message

The composer's `send` command (PRD R97) types a message into the window's current session, from claude-collab's `session.js`. `ConversationService.send` stores the message, origin `typed` and delivery `open`, answers with it at once, and queues it in the session's `SessionInput` (`packages/conversation/src/input.ts`), which types one message at a time per session, in order. Each message is one input transaction:

1. **Guards.** Every 200 ms it reads the visible screen with the holder's `snapshot` and waits while a numbered choice or confirmation is open (Claude Code's wait for a usage limit's reset, whose lines say `Esc to cancel`, is not one, because it takes a message and waits again, mechanics M100), while the input box (from the `❯` line under a rule to the next rule, every line of it, read with dim cells blanked, so the placeholder and a suggested prompt are not text) holds a draft, or while an attached terminal typed a key in the last 1.5 seconds (`TYPING_PAUSE_MS`, the snapshot's `keyAt`). Typing in the attached terminal is not blocked; this pause keeps a paste from starting while the operator types there. A message waits as long as the session can take it. A screen read or write the holder does not answer within 5 seconds (`HOLDER_ANSWER_MS`) fails the message.
2. **Input reservation.** `Runtime.reserveInput` waits for an end, Stop, Delete or resume of the session in progress, as an attach does ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions), Attach serialization), refuses a session that ended or was resumed under another launch than the view showed, and while held defers a release as an attached terminal does ("end of held session … deferred: a message is being typed"); the next roster read tries the end again. The guards are read again under the reservation, which is released when they no longer hold or the read fails.
3. **Paste, settle, Enter.** The text, line breaks kept as newlines, other control characters removed and a first `/`, `!`, `#` or `&` escaped with a backslash so it is never a command (`pasteText`), goes in one holder `write` as a bracketed paste; after 60 ms (`PASTE_SETTLE_MS`) the screen is read every 20 ms (`ECHO_POLL_MS`), as claude-collab does, until the paste shows in the box, for 1 second at most, and Enter goes as its own write. `pasteAndSubmit` retries Enter alone, up to 4 attempts, while the text still sits in the box; the screen answers for an Enter only once the revision rose after it and the box changed, so a stale screen never sends a blind Enter, and an Enter that drew no reaction is retried after 10 seconds. Before every write, Enter retries included, the message's session, launch and current-session checks run again, so no key reaches a session resumed meanwhile. The write sets the holder's `lastInputAt`, so the end rule's 60-second settle (PRD R95) sees it. A first `@` is a file mention, as anywhere in the text, and is kept, because the escape loses the mention ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M88); a slash command chosen from the composer's list (`send` with `command`) keeps its `/`, so it runs ([Slash commands](#slash-commands)).
4. **Confirmation.** The message is `sent` once the transcript's last 256 KiB hold it as a user entry, or as the `enqueue` queue operation Claude Code writes for a message typed while it works, written at or after the paste (`sentInTranscript`), with the `pasted_content` tags Claude Code wraps a paste of several lines in removed (`unwrapPasted`, [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M86) and a slash command read from the `<command-name>` and `<command-args>` tags Claude Code records it in as `/name args` (`typedText`, M88), waited for up to 10 seconds after the Enter (`CONFIRM_SENT_MS`). After each Enter the transcript is read every 50 ms (`SENT_POLL_MS`) until the screen check 400 ms later, and after the Enter retries every 50 ms for a second and every 200 ms after that, so a message shows `sent` as soon as Claude Code saves it ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M99). Otherwise, after the Enter retries, the screen settles it (`#settle`), and it is never typed again. An empty input box, or one holding other text, means it went: it is `sent`, and its transcript line places it if it lands. A box holding exactly the message (`isMessage`: its text with the box's wrapping ignored, or only the `[Pasted text #1 +29 lines]` placeholder Claude Code shows for a long paste), with no key typed in an attached terminal since the paste, is cleared with Ctrl+U (`CLEAR_LINE`, [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M90), which deletes from the end, one key at a time and at most two per line and two more, each only after the session, launch and current-session checks pass, the transcript still lacks the message, the box still holds what is left of it from its start (`leftOfMessage`) and no terminal key came, and the message is `not-sent` ("Claude Code did not take the message, so Switchboard cleared it from the input box. Send it again."). A box holding the message with other text (`boxHolds`), one a terminal typed into, or one that changed under a key, is left as it is, and the message is `not-sent` ("… Switchboard left the input box as it is: send or clear the message in the terminal."). A box that cannot be read leaves it `not-sent` with why. The transcript is read again before and after each key and after a failed key or screen read, so a message Claude takes late is `sent`. A message refused before its paste, or whose session ended, was resumed or was replaced by a newer session of its work item while it waited, is `not-sent` with why; a write the holder refused (`NotWritten`) before anything was written makes it `not-sent`, and any other failed write, including one whose answer was lost, is settled from the screen in the same way, a check that fails before a clearing key leaving it `not-sent` with why.

**Timing line.** Each input transaction records when its steps happened (`InputSteps`), and the hub logs one line per typed message when it settles: `session <id>: message <id> <delivery>: ` then, for a message turn detection ended, the voice's steps ([Dictation](#dictation)), then the queue wait, the guards (with the first guard that held it), paste to echo (the paste showing in the box, or `not seen` with what the last read showed: a menu, no input box, or an empty box), paste to Enter (with the number of Enters past one), Enter to transcript, the screen reads and their time, and the whole (`stepsText`), such as `queue 0 ms, guards 48 ms, paste→echo 92 ms, paste→Enter 92 ms, Enter→transcript 183 ms, 3 screen reads in 66 ms, 328 ms in all`.

A message sent while its reply will be heard ([Voice](#voice)) is typed with `[spoken] ` before its pasted text, and `[expressive] ` after that while `voice.expressive` is on, and kept with `spokenRequested`; a slash command chosen from the list never is. A message that holds dictated text, sent by a spoken turn's end or by the composer's Enter, is typed with `[voice] ` after those tags, and a part of one still being spoken with `[voice] [partial] ` and never `[spoken]`, with origin `voice` and its `pieces` ([Dictation](#dictation)). `typedText` reads a message line without those tags, `[voice]` and `[partial]` included, so the message shows and matches as the operator wrote it; a stopped hub's next hub settles such a message by the text it typed, tags included, `[expressive]` as `voice.expressive` says now. A typed message is added `provisional`, so it waits after the items in the transcript ([Order](#reading-a-session)), and the hub records when its paste was written (`pastedAt`). The transcript reader matches it in the same model as a message sent in the terminal while Claude is busy: a line with a typed message's pasted text, escaped or as a chosen command, so no store column says which it was (the same words sent both ways within 2 seconds can take each other's lines), the latest such message pasted before it, written no earlier than 2 seconds before that paste, and for a message not yet `sent` no later than 2 minutes after it (`TYPED_LATE_MS`), is that message. Its queue line makes it `sent` and adds no message; the user line or `queued_command` line where Claude received it makes it `sent`, gives it that line's UUID, clears `provisional` and moves it there, instead of adding a terminal message, and replies whose chain leads to that line answer it. A message still waiting to be pasted is never matched, a typed message is never withdrawn as a terminal one is, and when the session moves to a new transcript file, no message typed before is matched by it. One that ends `not-sent`, which typed nothing or which Claude Code did not take, leaves the conversation: the hub deletes it from the store, so no `hello` or `history` shows it, and sends a last `message` event with it as `not-sent`, with its text and why, to the windows showing its session and to the window that sent it, on which every window drops it and the window that sent it puts its text back in the composer ([Conversation view](#conversation-view)). The hub remembers the session and `opId` of the last 100 such messages, so a repeated `send` is refused with why rather than typed again. A session's queue is kept while it holds a message, so its messages are never typed by two queues. A typed message keeps the launch it was sent to (`launch`). A message a stopped hub left `open` (`openMessages`) is settled by the next hub once a window showing its session opens its stream, so a message that was not sent reaches a composer: one never pasted is `not-sent` ("Switchboard restarted before typing the message. Send it again."); a pasted one is settled from the transcript and the screen as above, only in the launch it was typed into, queued behind its session's other messages, after waiting up to 30 seconds (`RESOLVE_WAIT_MS`) for the holder, which a new hub connects to on first use, to show the screen, and `not-sent` with why when it never does, as is one with no launch kept. Since this hub does not know which window sent it, its last `message` event carries `giveBack: true`, and every window showing the session puts its text back in the composer; one settled while no window showing the session has a stream open is sent again, in memory, to the next that opens one. A key typed in an attached terminal between the last screen read and a clearing key is not caught, as a paste is not (voice-design decision 2); the clearing stops at the next read. Typed messages that an earlier hub left `uncertain` became `sent` in a migration. The holder's `write` needs a holder that names it in `welcome.features`. A holder of the same protocol from an older build is never replaced while the hub runs, so an older one refuses `send` with the way out (`holderCannotType`): "The terminal holder is from an older build and cannot type. To switch to this build, run `<sessions.nodePath> "<home>/.switchboard/hub.js" holder stop --all` in a terminal: it lists the sessions that end with the holder, each of which can be resumed, and ends them when run again with --yes. Until then, open the session in the terminal." ([Visibility of held sessions](#visibility-of-held-sessions)).

### Slash commands

The composer's `/` list (PRD R101) shows the slash commands of the window's current session. `POST /v1/conversation/commands` answers them from the session's `SessionCommands` (`packages/conversation/src/commands.ts`): first the built-ins `/clear` and `/compact`, which finish without a menu in the terminal (those that open one, such as `/model` or `/resume`, are left out, since the view cannot answer it), then the skills the session's transcript names in its `skill_listing` attachments, in their order, with their descriptions. An attachment with `isInitial: false` adds to the skills or updates a description, and any other replaces them (`skillListing`, [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M88). The hub reads each session's transcript on from where the last request stopped, and from the start when the session moves to another transcript file; before the session's first message, and after `/clear` until the next one, the file has no listing, so only the built-ins show. Choosing a command fills `/name ` into the box; the composer sends the text with `command: true` while it is one line that still starts with that `/name`, and `send` refuses `command` for any other text. A typed `/clear` is matched to its line in the new transcript file it starts, while other messages typed into the earlier file are let go. A `/name` typed by hand without choosing it, or recalled with Up, goes escaped, as a message.

### Statuses

A status is the part of a reply after its own `[status]` line, outside fenced code and up to three spaces in, to the end of the reply's text (`splitStatus`, from claude-collab's `status.js`); the text before the line is the reply's `opening`, empty for a reply that is only a status. A tag quoted inline, in a code block or with text on its line is text. The screen reader keeps a `[status]` line apart when it joins wrapped lines, so a screen copy shows the reply's text alone; a status is taken only from a reply's transcript copy, never its screen copy, which can still be withdrawn or growing. Each reply has at most one status, `st-<reply ID>`, so a later line of the same API message that adds to it updates it in place, and the newest status read is the session's. A reply that is only a status rewrites nothing and is not a rewrite target, and the view leaves it out of the timeline. The `statuses` table keeps each session's newest 500 statuses, in the order read, outside the activity log, and they go with their work item.

Before a session's first status, a window's Status tab shows its work item's stage (`WorkItem.stage`, from `stage` reports) as a fallback, with the ID `stage-<work item ID>` and the stage's time; with no stage either, the tab says the session has posted no status yet. The session's state is not repeated there; the header's codicon shows it.

### Unread

PRD R100. A reply or status is unread until the operator sees it in the Conversation view; `seenAt` on the `replies` and `statuses` rows records when, and the migration that added it counts every earlier one as seen.

- **What counts.** A reply the timeline shows on its own (`countsUnread` in core): not a withdrawn screen copy, a reply a rewrite replaced, a reply that is only a status or a note Switchboard added. Of the statuses, only the session's latest, which the Status tab shows. A screen copy replaced in place by its transcript copy keeps its ID, so it is seen once; a later line of a seen status or reply keeps it seen. `Store.unreadCount(sessionId)` counts them.
- **Seen.** The page counts an item as seen once it has been visible for a second while the window has the focus: the status while the Status tab shows it, or a reply with at least half of itself, or half the timeline's height for a taller one, inside the timeline's viewport while the Conversation tab shows it; an item on a tab not shown is not on the page. The extension sends the page `focus {focused}` from `vscode.window.state` on load and on each change, and the page checks on each redraw, tab change, scroll, fold, window resize, change of the timeline's size (`ResizeObserver`) and visibility change, and every 250 ms while the window has the focus and an unreported unread item is on the page, so an item hidden between two checks starts its second over. It posts `seen {ids, target}`, with the scope generation and session it showed them in, once per item for that session, and the extension sends `seen` ([Conversation API](#conversation-api)); when the hub refuses it or does not answer, the extension posts `seen-failed {ids}` and the page reports them again after another second in view, and after a reconnect the page reports again whatever is still unread. Each item is marked on its own, so seeing a newer one never clears an older one; being in the window, a newer item or the window's focus alone does not count. A view that was never opened in a window, or is hidden, marks nothing.
- **Mark.** Unread has one mark and one colour everywhere: a filled round badge in VS Code's activity bar badge colours (`activityBarBadge.background` and `activityBarBadge.foreground`, the colours of VS Code's own view badges; Dark Modern `#0078D4`, Light Modern `#005FB8`). It holds the count, or "New" for the Status tab's new status, 16 px tall with an 8 px radius; where it marks one item with no count, a reply's timeline dot or an artifact row, it is a 7 px dot. It marks the Conversation view's badge, the Status and Artifacts tab badges, the Status tab's New mark, a reply's dot, an artifact row's dot, the dot of the **New messages** pill and the Switchboard pane's work items; on the web page, the Status tab's New mark and a reply's dot. The dock's rows, the Conversation view's dropdown and the web page's list, dropdown and tabs show no unread count.
- **Counts.** The hub sends each window that lists a session's work item, while it is that work item's current session, `unread {workItemId, count}` when its count changes, checked after every message, reply or status added or changed, since a new item can push an unread one past retention, and after a `seen`. The hub keeps the count of each work item's current session (`WorkItemView.unread`, which `buildSnapshot` reads, 0 for a session that is not Code Mode), so an earlier session's items, which no window shows, never count; the dock view carries it, but the dock shows no unread count, and neither does the web page; `ConversationService` calls `unreadChanged` when a session's count differs from the last it saw, and the hub builds the views again.

### Artifacts

An artifact is a file one level down in its work item's artifact folder, `<owner>/<file>` (PRD R99), from claude-collab's `artifacts.js`. The folder is `<session folder>/.wip/artifacts/w<work item ID>` (`artifactsDir` in core), its own per work item, so a later work item that shares the worktree never lists an earlier one's; the session finds it in `CLAUDE_ARTIFACTS_DIR` and in the conversation rules ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). Artifacts are not stored; the folder is the record.

- **Watch.** `ArtifactFolder` (`packages/conversation/src/artifacts.ts`) watches the folder recursively, settling each file's changes for 150 ms, and reads each artifact's type by its extension (`markdown`, `html`, `image`, `audio`, `video`, else `file`), and from a Markdown or HTML file under 4 MB its kind and title: front matter `kind` and `title`, else its first `#` heading or `<title>`, the kind else the file name's first word and the title else the file name. A name with a segment that starts with a dot, such as the `.gitignore`, is not an artifact, nor a file deeper down; a change under `<owner>/<stem>/`, the folder of files an artifact uses, or to that folder itself, is a change to every artifact of that stem. A file is read again when its time, change time or size differ from those read before, so a rewrite that keeps the file's time still counts. A file is resolved by real path, so a link or junction that leaves the folder lists nothing. `updatedAt` is the file's time, or when it was read when a rewrite kept an older time or a file it uses changed. A folder that is missing, or deleted, has its artifacts removed and is looked for each second.
- **Hub.** `ConversationService` watches the folder of each work item that a window with a stream shows, from the scope's `cwd`, the current session's folder or, before the first session, the worktree's, and stops once its last such stream closes, the window moves, or its window key closes with its `/events` stream. `hello` carries `artifactsDir` and `artifacts`, newest first, and each change goes as an `artifact` event to the windows of that work item only.
- **Opening** (`Artifacts.open` in `extensions/switchboard/src/artifacts.ts`). Only an artifact of the list the window was sent, resolved by real path inside `artifactsDir` (`artifactFile`), opens: Markdown with `vscode.openWith` in `vscode.markdown.preview.editor`, as `openLink` opens a report; a picture in `imagePreview.previewEditor`, which also draws an SVG; an HTML page in its own panel; audio, video and anything else with `vscode.open`, VS Code's media preview for the audio and video types it knows.
- **HTML panel** (voice-design § 4.4). One `switchboard.artifact` webview panel per artifact, apart from the Conversation view's message bridge, titled with the artifact's title: `enableScripts` on, `enableForms` off, and `localResourceRoots` only the folder beside it, `<owner>/<stem>/`, when its real path is inside the artifact folder (`assetsRoot`). `artifactPage` (`src/artifactModel.ts`) puts a Content Security Policy, `default-src 'none'; script-src 'unsafe-inline' <cspSource>; style-src 'unsafe-inline' <cspSource>; img-src <cspSource> data:; media-src <cspSource>; font-src <cspSource>; form-action 'none'; base-uri 'none'`, with no `connect-src`, so no network, and a script that refuses a link click that is not an in-page `#` link, stopping it in the document's capture phase because VS Code's own webview script opens a clicked link even when it was prevented, and `window.open`, before the page, after its doctype; it rewrites each `src`, `href` and `poster` of a tag, leaving the text of scripts, styles and comments as written: one relative to the artifact that resolves by real path inside the folder beside it to its `asWebviewUri`, a fragment, `data:` or `blob:` URL as written, and any other to empty. An open panel is drawn again when its artifact changes, and says so when it is deleted. A script that sets `location` itself is not stopped.
- **Mermaid** (voice-design § 4.4). The extension declares `markdown.markdownItPlugins`, and `activate` returns `extendMarkdownIt` (`src/markdownMermaid.ts`), which renders a `` ```mermaid `` fence as `<pre class="switchboard-mermaid">` holding its escaped source and leaves other fences to the renderer. `markdown.previewScripts` loads `dist/mermaid/mermaid.min.js`, Mermaid 12's self-contained build copied from `node_modules` at build time, and `dist/mermaidPreview.js` (`src/webview/mermaidPreview.ts`), which draws each placeholder once with `securityLevel: 'strict'` in the `dark` theme for a dark or high contrast preview, else `default`, offline, on load and on `vscode.markdown.updateContent`, waiting up to 10 seconds for Mermaid, since the preview loads both scripts `async`. A diagram Mermaid cannot read keeps its source with Mermaid's error under it. The plugin applies to every Markdown preview of the window, statuses included.

### Conversation API

Version 1, loopback, with the hub token; the web page reaches the same routes with its cookie and a page key in place of a window key, and adds `page-focus`, the list, the artifact and file routes ([Web page](#web-page)).

- `GET /v1/conversation/events?window=<key>`: an SSE stream for the window key, refused with 403 for a key no connected window holds. Each event is `{v: 1, seq, type, at, …}` with `id: <seq>`: `hello {protocol: 1, scope, sessions, items, more, status, unread, artifactsDir, artifacts, voice, settings}` when it opens, `voice` the window's `VoiceView` and `settings` `{expandReplies}` ([Voice](#voice)), where `scope` is `{kind: 'item', key, folder, title, scopeGen, conversations, workItemId, cwd, sessionId}` or null, where `folder` is the window's first folder, `title` the primary's ticket title, `conversations` the dropdown's entries, `{workItemId, title, primary, sessionId, state, conversation, unread}` each, the primary first ([Window scope](#window-scope)), and `workItemId`, `cwd` and `sessionId` the selected conversation's, `items` the current session's newest 100 messages and replies in conversation order, withdrawn ones left out, `status` the Status tab's status or null, and `artifactsDir` and `artifacts` the work item's artifact folder, or null, and its artifacts newest first ([Artifacts](#artifacts)); `scope {scope}` followed by a fresh `hello` when the window's selected conversation or its current session changes; `conversations {conversations}` when an entry of the dropdown changes and the selection does not: a conversation added or done, or a title, state or unread count; `session {session}` when the current session's view changes; `message {message}` and `reply {reply}` when one is added or changed; `status {status}` when the Status tab's status changes: a new or updated status of the current session, or its work item's stage before the first, checked after each snapshot; `artifact {artifact}` when an artifact of the work item is written, rewritten or changed through a file it uses, and `artifact {artifact: {path}, removed: true}` when it is gone; `unread {workItemId, count}` when a conversation's current session's unread count changes, which `hello`'s `unread`, the selected conversation's, and its entries start ([Unread](#unread)); `voice {voice}` when the window's voice state changes, `settings {settings}` when the settings are reloaded, `progress {replyId, chars, atMs}` at most every 150 ms while a reply of the window's session plays, and, to the window dictation goes to ([Dictation](#dictation)), `words {messageId, text}` as the live words of an utterance change and `draft {text}` when the hub changed the composer's draft. A reconnect gets a fresh `hello`.
- `SessionView`: `{id, kind, title, state, waitingFor, launch, current, conversation, reason, persona, canPin}`; `conversation` is `full` for a live Code Mode session with hooks on and for a Codex app-server session with a thread ID, ended or not, since a message resumes an ended one, `read-only` for a Code Mode session that ended or is unobserved, and `none` for a background session, each with its reason; `persona` is `{id, name, pinned, tonePending}` while `voice.enabled` is on and the session has a persona in the roster, else null, and `canPin` is true while `voice.enabled` is on, the roster is not empty and the session is a Code Mode or app-server session, the sessions the flow pins, whether it has a persona or not ([Personas](#personas)).
- A message is `{type: 'message', id, sessionId, workItemId, at, ord, origin: 'terminal' | 'typed' | 'voice', text, provisional, withdrawn, delivery, error, spokenRequested, pieces?, partial?, interrupted?}`, `pieces` the recognised pieces `{text, score, f0, dropped}` of a dictated message, `partial` true for a `[partial]` part ([Dictation](#dictation)) and `interrupted` `{replyId, said, rest}` for one that spoke over a reply ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)), each left out of other messages, where a typed message's `delivery` is `open` or `sent`, both it and `error` null for a terminal message, and a `message` event with `delivery: 'not-sent'` and `error` removes a message that typed nothing or that Claude Code did not take, with `giveBack: true` when every window showing the session is to put its text back in the composer, and one with `delivery: 'refused'`, sent only to the window that dictated it, is a spoken turn the hub refused before storing anything, whose reason the composer shows while its draft keeps the text; a reply `{type: 'reply', id, sessionId, at, ord, provisional, kind: 'reply' | 'aside' | 'note', answers, opening, source: 'transcript' | 'screen', replaces, rewrittenBy, withdrawn, unread, speak, tone, spoken: {state, atMs, saidChars}}`, `unread` true for one that counts and was not seen, `speak` and `tone` its passage and tone line or null, and `spoken.state` one of `none`, `queued`, `playing`, `played`, `interrupted`, `skipped`, `stopped-moved` and `failed`; `provisional` is true while an item is not in the transcript yet (`inTranscript`). A window places each item it gets by its `ord` and whether it is in the transcript yet ([Order](#reading-a-session)), so an item whose transcript line was read moves.
- `POST /v1/conversation/history {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId, before, limit?}` answers `{items, more}`, the items before the item `before` names (its ID, or null; one that is gone, or another session's, has none before it), in conversation order, at most 100; 403 "Not in this window." for a key no window holds or a session that is not the window's current one, such as a forged ID, and 409 "The window's conversation changed." for a stale `scopeGen`. It only reads, so a repeated `opId` reads again.
- An artifact is `{path, owner, kind, title, ext, type, updatedAt}`, where `path` is `<owner>/<file>` in `artifactsDir` and `type` is `markdown`, `html`, `image`, `audio`, `video` or `file`.
- A status is `{id, sessionId, at, markdown, fallback, unread}`; `fallback` is true for the work item's stage ([Statuses](#statuses)), which is never `unread`.
- `POST /v1/conversation/seen {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId, ids}` marks at most 200 replies and statuses of the window's current session seen, each on its own, and answers `{unread}`, the session's count; the windows showing it get each marked reply and status again and `unread`. Refused as `history` is, and with 403 "Not in this window." for an ID that is not a reply or status of that session, marking none; 400 for a malformed body or more IDs. Marking again changes nothing, so a repeated `opId` is safe.
- `POST /v1/conversation/statuses {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId, before, limit?}` answers `{statuses, more}`, the window's current session's statuses before the status `before` names (its ID, or null for the latest; one that is gone, or another session's, has none before it), newest first, at most 50, for the Markdown preview; refused as `history` is. It only reads.
- `POST /v1/conversation/send {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId, launch, text}` types `text`, at most 10,000 characters, into the window's current session ([Typing a message](#typing-a-message)) and answers `{message}` at once, with `delivery: 'open'`; `message` events bring its delivery. A repeated `opId` that passes the scope checks answers the message it added to that session (opIds are looked up per session) and types nothing again. Refused with 403 "Not in this window." outside the scope, 409 "The window's conversation changed." for a stale `scopeGen`, 409 with the session's reason when it is not `full` (such as "The session has ended. Open it in the terminal to resume it."), 409 "The session was resumed since the view showed it; send the message again." when `launch` is not the session's, and 400 for an empty or longer text. `command: true` types a slash command chosen from the composer's list without the escape ([Slash commands](#slash-commands)); 400 "A slash command is one line that starts with /name." for any other text with it.
- `POST /v1/conversation/commands {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId}` answers `{commands}`, the window's current session's slash commands as `{name, description}` without the slash, the built-ins first ([Slash commands](#slash-commands)); refused as `history` is. It only reads.
- `POST /v1/conversation/providers {}` reads `{defaultProvider, installed}`, the current `sessions.provider` and the providers whose CLI the hub finds (`installedProviders`: `sessions.claudePath` and `sessions.codexPath` as a path, or a bare name on the hub's `PATH` with `PATHEXT` on Windows), without creating work or changing settings. Creation routes accept only `claude` or `codex` when `provider` is present; other values, including null, return 400. A repeated creation `opId` retains its first result even when a retry changes provider.
- `POST /v1/conversation/select {window, scopeGen, opId, workItemId}` selects one of the window's conversations and answers `{scopeGen}`; the window gets `scope` and a fresh `hello`. 403 "Not in this window." for a work item that is not one of its conversations; a stale `scopeGen` is not refused, since the command names its whole target.
- `POST /v1/conversation/new-conversation {window, scopeGen, opId, provider?}` makes a [hosted conversation](#hosted-conversations) for the window, selects it and answers `{workItemId}`; a repeated `opId` answers the first work item (the last 100 are remembered). 403 for a key with no scope, 409 with the flow's refusal. In a folder window the conversation is hosted by, and runs in, the window's canonical folder.
- `POST /v1/conversation/mic {window, scopeGen, opId, on}` turns the mic on, moving the voice to the window's session first, or off, refused with 409 "Voice is not in this window." from a window the voice is not in; `POST /v1/conversation/composer {window, scopeGen, opId, focused, draft}` tells the hub the composer's focus and draft for the focus rule, `draft` at most 10,000 characters ([Dictation](#dictation)). Both are refused as the commands below are, and answer `{on}` and `{done: true}`.
- `POST /v1/conversation/talk-here {window, scopeGen, opId}`, `stop-speaking {window, scopeGen, opId}`, `speak-replies {window, scopeGen, opId, on}` and `replay {window, scopeGen, opId, replyId}` ([Voice](#voice)): 403 outside the scope or, for `replay`, for a reply of another session, 409 for a stale `scopeGen` and with the voice's refusal ("Voice is not in this window.", "Voice is off (voice.enabled).", "The voice speaks only for a live session.", "This reply has no spoken passage."), 404 from a hub with no voice; `speak-replies` answers `{on}`.
- `POST /v1/conversation/personas {window, scopeGen, opId}` answers `{personas}`, the roster as `{id, name}`, empty while `voice.enabled` is off; `POST /v1/conversation/persona {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId, personaId}` pins the persona to the window's current session, or clears its pin with a null `personaId`, and answers `{persona}`, the session's new `SessionView.persona` ([Personas](#personas)): 403 outside the scope, 409 for a stale `scopeGen`, "Voice is off (voice.enabled).", and the flow's refusal, such as "No persona vega in voice.personas.".

### Conversation view

`ConversationView` (`extensions/switchboard/src/conversation.ts`) is a webview view in the `switchboard-conversation` container of `viewsContainers.secondarySidebar`, as Claude Code's own panel is; before VS Code 1.106 the `switchboard.noSecondarySidebar` context key shows it in the `switchboard-conversation-activity` activity bar container instead (mechanics M85). It is its container's only view; the status and the artifacts are tabs in it. After each `/events` `hello` the extension opens the conversation stream for its window key (`openConversation`), applies its events to `ConversationState` (`src/conversationModel.ts`) and posts the whole state to the page, which draws it (`src/webview/conversationRender.ts`); a stream that ends or does not open is opened again after a second, up to five times with a longer wait each time, and then waits for the next `hello`.

- **Header:** above the tab strip, three controls: the conversation dropdown, the persona and Open in terminal. The dropdown's button shows the selected conversation exactly as its row in the menu does, from one renderer (`conversationLabel`, shared in kind with the web page's): its current session's state codicon, its title (its `ConversationEntry`'s, else the scope's), and its provider's mark, then a chevron, with no unread count; it takes the free width, down to 6em, and the title takes the button's free width with an ellipsis, so the provider's mark and the chevron sit at its right end, as a select box's arrow does, and in the menu each row's provider mark sits at the row's right end, in one column, the rows' titles lined up with New conversation…'s. The provider's mark is a 16 px monochrome SVG drawn in the theme's foreground colour, with the name, Claude or Codex, as its tooltip and accessible name (`providerMark`, `packages/conversation/src/providerIcons.ts`, shared with the web page): Claude's is the path of `resources/claude-logo.svg` in the Claude Code VS Code extension (`anthropic.claude-code`), Codex's the path of `resources/blossom-white.svg` in the Codex VS Code extension (`openai.chatgpt`); a provider with no mark shows its name as text, and an older hub that omits the provider shows nothing. The state codicon comes from `sessionIcon` and `SESSION_ICONS` in `packages/core/src/group.ts`, which take the codicon of the dock mark with the same meaning from `MARK_ICONS`, so the view, the web page, the pane and the dock draw one icon per meaning: the running mark's `loading~spin` starting or working, also the "Thinking…" row's spinner, `question` waiting for an answer or a start prompt, `shield` waiting for permission, the Idle mark's `circle-large-outline` idle, `error` failed, `stop-circle` ended, `circle-slash` with no session, or `watch` while the session waits for a usage limit to reset, each drawn at 13 px in a 16 px column, in the secondary text colour unless its tone (`SESSION_TONES` and `MARK_TONES`) is `attention`, the warning colour, for a question, a permission, Approve to send, Blocked and a rate limit, or `error` for a failure, with a smooth linear spinner; `check` is only Done's, with `Rate limited · resumes 1:20 PM` as its text (`SessionView.limit`, [Rate limits](#rate-limits)); the selected conversation's codicon and provider come from its current session in the button and its row alike. The button opens a menu ([Menus](#menus)) of the window's conversations, the primary first, each row's state codicon in the menu's gutter, the selected one marked by a highlight in `list.inactiveSelectionBackground` rather than a check mark, then a separator and **New conversation…**, which opens the menu of the providers from `providers` under the same button, the default first and marked Default, and posts `new-conversation` with the one picked; a conversation chosen posts `select`, after which the view and all three tabs start from that conversation's `hello`. The persona, when the session has one ([Personas](#personas)), is an icon at every width, `person`, or `pinned` when pinned, dimmed while `tonePending`, with "Persona: <name>" and what a click does as its tooltip and accessible name; a session with none that can take a pin (`canPin`), such as one started before the roster or whose persona left it, shows a plain `person` with "No persona. Click to choose one.", and a session that cannot shows no persona button; it opens the roster from `personas` as a menu right-aligned under it, the session's persona checked, Clear pin after a separator while one is pinned, and posts `persona` with the one picked. Open in terminal opens the selected conversation's session's attach terminal. The persona and provider menus load showing "Loading…", put the focus on their first choice once loaded, and show a refusal in the menu; a refusal of a pin or a new conversation shows above the composer. The view opens no quick pick or dialog of its own: there is no Choose Persona command, and Switchboard: New Conversation…, for the keyboard, still asks for the provider in a quick pick and shows a refusal as a warning. A session the view cannot talk to says why. A folder window with no conversation shows the dropdown with its folder's name, whose menu holds only New conversation…, with "No conversation in this folder yet. Start one with New conversation… in the menu above." and no composer.
- **Menus:** every menu the view opens (the conversation dropdown, the persona and provider menus, and the Status tab's earlier statuses) is drawn as VS Code's context menus from its `menu.*` colours (`menu.background`, `menu.foreground`, `menu.selectionBackground` and `menu.selectionForeground`, `menu.separatorBackground`, `menu.border`) and `widget.shadow`, on the shared **glass** (`.glass`) every floating layer over the content wears (the menus, the `@` and `/` lists and the **New messages** pill, never bubbles or cards): `menu.background` at 88% over an 8 px backdrop blur, fully opaque where `backdrop-filter` is missing, a `menu.border` hairline and a soft shadow; a panel as wide as its content, at least 160 px and at most 360 px, never wider than the view less 16 px, anchored under the button that opened it (left-aligned under the dropdown, right-aligned under a button at the header's right) and overlapping what is below, an 8 px radius, 4 px of padding; 13 px single-line rows 28 px tall in normal weight, a 28 px gutter on the left for a check mark on the chosen row (or the `add` codicon of New conversation…), a muted note such as Default at the right, the row under the pointer or with the focus in the selection colours with a 4 px rounded highlight inset from the panel's edges, and separators inset 10 px with 4 px above and below. Up and Down move between the rows, Enter or a click chooses, and Escape or a press outside closes the menu, giving the focus back to its button.
- **Tabs:** under the header, a tab strip drawn as VS Code's panel tabs (uppercase labels in the `panelTitle` colours, the selected one underlined in `panelTitle-activeBorder`): **Conversation**, **Status** and **Artifacts**, and only the selected tab's panel below it, taking the view's full height; the composer stays at the bottom on every tab, with the mic, live words and the mic's state, so the operator can type or dictate from any tab. A message sent from the Status or Artifacts tab leaves that tab shown, and a line over the composer says "Sent. Its reply shows on the Conversation tab." for 4 seconds (`SENT_NOTE_MS`). An ended session's reason shows just above the composer on every tab, one line in its width, with a **Resume** button that opens the session's attach terminal, which resumes it; an ended Codex app-server session, which has no reason since a message resumes it, says "The session has ended. A message or Resume continues it."; any other reason the view cannot talk to a session heads the conversation. The strip is one tab stop: Left and Right move to the tab before or after, Home and End to the first or last. The Status tab has a **New** badge while its status is New or unread, and the Artifacts tab the count of its artifacts new or changed since last opened. The page keeps the tab shown, and the conversation's scroll position while another tab is shown, with `setState`; a redraw keeps the focused tab or button and the Status or Artifacts tab's scroll position, and replies that came while another tab was shown, with the conversation scrolled up, bring back the **New messages** pill. The page draws each state by changing only the nodes that differ (`morph`, `src/webview/morph.ts`), matching children by `data-key`, `data-id` or `id`, else by position and tag, and leaving a focused field's value alone, so a redraw keeps the focus, a selection, the caret, scroll positions and open menus; each tab's panel and each conversation's scroll area is a node of its own, so another tab or conversation starts afresh. A window with no work item shows no tabs.
- **Status tab:** the session's latest status as Markdown, drawn as replies are, under a line with the `pulse` codicon, its time ("Thu, Nov 19, 1:46 PM"), how long ago ("4 min ago", redrawn every 30 seconds), a **New** mark in the unread mark ([Unread](#unread)), and two buttons: `open-preview` opens the status in the Markdown preview and `history` opens the session's earlier statuses as a menu right-aligned under it ([Menus](#menus)), newest first, each row its first line with its time as the note, with **Older statuses** after a separator loading the page before through `statuses` while there are more, "Loading…" while a page loads, and the hub's refusal in its place; the menu takes the focus on its first status when its button had it, closes on the button, Escape or a press outside it, and keeps each menu inside the view (`fitMenus`, which caps its width to the room on its anchored side); a click opens that status in the Markdown preview (`status-open` with its ID, from the statuses the extension listed for the current session). The list closes with the button and when the session changes. A status that replaces the one the page had for the same session, or the first after the stage, is New, and a screen reader hears "New status: <first line>"; it stays New until a minute after the Status tab first shows it in a visible page, or until it is opened in the preview, so a status that came while another tab was shown is still New there. Before the first status, the tab says the session has posted no status yet and shows the work item's stage, dimmed, with no buttons; with no stage either, only that line. The preview reads a `switchboard-status:/<status ID>/Status <local date and time>.md` document from a `TextDocumentContentProvider` that serves only statuses this window was sent, forgotten, with open documents redrawn, when its work item or current session changes; a document of a status it no longer has, as after a reload, says so, and a status picked from a list opened before the scope moved is not opened. The preview would resolve a relative link against the `switchboard-status:` URI, so the provider turns each inline link to a path, relative to the session's folder or absolute, into a `file:` URL with its line fragment (`previewLinks`), outside fenced code.
- **Timeline:** in conversation order ([Order](#reading-a-session)); our messages as chat bubbles on the right, at most 85% of the width, in the message blue the web page shares (`--sb-message-bubble` in `packages/conversation/src/bubbles.css`, a 15% tint of `button.background`, `#0078D426` in Dark Modern), with the theme's foreground, only a `contrastBorder` border in high contrast themes, and a 12 px radius with a 4 px bottom-right corner as the tail; a message sent or still sending shows only its bubble and time, the reply spinner showing the work, and an error state, of which a typed message has none that lasts, would show at the left of the line under the bubble, with why in the tooltip, as a dictated message's mic mark does; one that was not sent left out, a provisional one dimmed with, for one sent in the terminal, the tooltip "Sent; Claude has not read it yet", and a withdrawn one left out; replies with a grey dot, joined to the dots of the replies next to it by a line, the whole reply as Markdown; all of a message's or reply's metadata on one line under it (the **metadata line**, `.meta`), never over it and never two: its marks on the left, flush with the text's left edge (a reply's copy button first, then its tone line and "Not spoken"; a message's mic mark and error state; the tone and the mic mark only with **Show message details**, the copy button and error marks always), and its `at` as a small time at the right end, flush with the text's right edge ("1:46 PM", the full time in the tooltip, "2026-11-19 13:46:05 EST", the format of every time tooltip in the view and on the web page), the line of a message spanning its bubble, on the first item after each day's divider only, or on every item but a note with Show message details, an item without a time having its full time as the tooltip of its line, or of a message's bubble when it has no line (`metaLine`, `stamped`, `voiceMark` and `toneMark` in `packages/conversation/src/messageMeta.ts`, which the web page draws its line with too); Show message details is off by default, set with Switchboard: Show Message Details and Hide Message Details in the view's title menu, kept in the extension's `globalState` for the user, sent to the page with each `state` message as `details`, and the `switchboard.messageDetails` context key picks which command shows; and a small divider before the first item shown and before each turn, the items from a message on, that starts on another day than the turn before it, naming the day "Today", "Yesterday", the weekday and date this year ("Tue, Nov 17") or with the year before it ("Wed, Dec 31, 2025"), while the times stay time-only; the web page draws the same dividers; asides dimmed and smaller; notes centred; a rewrite in the place of the reply it rewrote, and after several rewrites the last, with the version before it behind **Rewritten**; withdrawn screen copies and replies with no text left out. Each artifact last written no earlier than the oldest item shown is a one-line row after the last entry from no later than its write (`placeArtifacts`, by time, since an artifact has no transcript line): a button with its type's codicon (`markdown`, `globe`, `file-media`, `unmute`, `device-camera-video` or `file`), its title and its kind, which opens it ([Artifacts](#artifacts)), as Enter on its row does; one written before the oldest item shown is only on the Artifacts tab. While the session is in a turn, starting, working (the state the header's `loading~spin` shows) or waiting on a question or a permission, when it stands still, a row drawn as Claude Code's spinner row, a `loading~spin` codicon in the progress bar colour with "Thinking…" ("Starting…" while the session starts) beside it in the foreground colour, 1.85em tall, and a status a screen reader reads as "Thinking", sits at the end of the timeline, after the newest item, so asides, notes and artifacts the session writes mid-turn show above it, until a reply, a rewrite included, `answers` the operator's latest message or a later one (`awaitingReply`), not an aside or a note, or the turn or the session ends; it shows no tool activity and the Up and Down keys skip it. **Show earlier messages** pages back with `history`.
- **Speech** ([Voice](#voice)): a reply with a `[speak]` passage that is not the whole reply shows the passage first, drawn as Markdown, with **Show more** under it, which shows the whole reply and turns into **Show less** (focused mode, voice-design decision 21); with `voice.expandReplies` on it starts whole and the button flips it the other way. The page keeps each flip with the folds. Its tone line shows dimmed and in italics on its metadata line, cut with an ellipsis, whole in the tooltip. Routine speech states (queued, playing, played, skipped, stopped when the voice moved) show nothing; after the operator spoke over the passage (`interrupted`), it shows as plain text with the part not heard dimmed, and a reply whose speech `failed` gets a short "Not spoken" mark on its metadata line, with why in the tooltip. R on a focused reply with a passage posts `replay`, and Escape in the view while this window's session speaks posts `stop-speaking`. While `voice.enabled` is on, the composer's footer keeps the message tools (Attach and `/`) on its left and puts every voice control in one cluster beside Send (voice-design report IA-3): while the voice is here, **Stop** (`debug-stop`) while this window's session speaks, **Speak replies** (`unmute` while on, `mute` while off, pressed while on, its state in the tooltip) and the mic button ([Dictation](#dictation)), as ghost icon buttons as in Claude Code's footer; while it is elsewhere, one compact **Talk here** button in their place that posts `talk-here`, only for a session the voice can speak for (`full`). A window whose session is not `full`, such as an ended one, shows no voice control while the voice is elsewhere, since the hub would refuse Talk here. The web page draws the same cluster, without the mic. There is no voice status text: the mic button carries the mic's state ([Dictation](#dictation)). The footer's buttons are redrawn only when their HTML changes, so a state event between a press and its release does not swallow the click.
- **Question cards** (voice-design § 5): under a reply, not an aside or a note, and under the status on the Status tab, that asks lettered questions, a card drawn as Claude Code's question form: a box in the input colours with the input border and an 8 px radius; each question's text in the foreground at 500 weight; its options as full-width rows with a 16 px radio mark, or a checkbox for a question that says it takes several ("any that apply", "one or more", "choose several"), drawn unpicked in the theme's checkbox colours (`checkbox.background`, with `checkbox.foreground` at 60% as the border, which stays visible on the input background where Dark Modern's `checkbox.border` does not), filled in the button colour once picked, the letter dimmed before the option's text; an Other field under them; and Submit in the button colours. `parseQuestions` (`packages/conversation/src/questions.ts`, which the web page shares) reads the Markdown outside fenced code: a numbered line (`1.` or `1)`, optionally bold or in a bullet) followed by at least two options lettered from A in order, each on its own line (`A)`, `(a)`, `a)`, `A.` or `A:`, only A to H, and `X.` only in capitals so initials are no option, optionally bold or in a bullet), an indented line continuing the option or question above it and any other line ending it; a lone run of options with no number asks the line before it. A numbered list item with no options is no question, a letter out of order spoils its question, and a number used twice or a lone run beside other questions gives no card, since their answers could not be told apart. Picking an option of a single question replaces its pick and its Other text, picking it again clears it, and text in its Other field clears its pick; a question that takes several toggles each. Submit, or Enter in an Other field, is enabled once every question has an answer, as in Claude Code's form, since the next message answers the whole card, and posts `send` as the composer's Enter does, with the scope generation, session and launch the page showed and its own ID, so the message is an ordinary one: `answerText` writes "1A, 2B", "2A, 2C" for several, "2 Other: use port 8799", the letter alone for a lone unnumbered question, and each answer on its own line when an Other text holds a comma or semicolon. The card is locked with "Sending…" from Submit until its message shows; a refusal, or the message coming back `not-sent` while its delivery is still open, shows why in the card and keeps its choices, and that text does not go into the composer. The first message after its reply, or after the reply its status came from, that has left the composer's queue (not `open`, not withdrawn) settles the card (`cardOutcome`): one that answers one of its questions ("1B", "1A and 2B", an Other answer as Submit writes it, whose text is never read for picks) makes it **Answered**, greyed, without Other fields or Submit, with the picks `answersIn` reads from that message and the other options dimmed; one that answers none of them sets it aside, **Skipped**, greyed with no picks and "Skipped: your next message did not answer these questions". Either way the session's wait on the card ends (`unansweredCard`, PRD R103), and a later reply can ask again in a card of its own. The text over a card leaves out the questions the card draws (`questionSegments`, which cuts the Markdown into runs of text and runs of questions, blocks with only blank lines between them being one run): the last run, when only blank lines follow it, as the conversation rules ask, is not drawn in the text at all, and every other run stays in place, folded into one line, "2 questions — answer below", that opens it, with the text between runs kept. The choices live in the page until it reloads. A card takes no answer, showing no Other fields or Submit, while the composer could not send: a session that is not `full`, or a closed stream. A card still unanswered when the session's turn ends gives its dock row the Answer a question mark until the next message (PRD R103), while the view shows the session idle, with no "Waiting for your answer" row.
- **Codex requests:** a Codex session's pending questions and approvals (`SessionView.requests`) show as cards at the end of the timeline, in the question card's style, in place of the turn row: a permission with its title (the command, the diff or the reason) and its decisions as buttons (Allow once, Allow for session, Decline, Cancel); questions with their options as radio rows and a field for a typed answer, hidden for a secret one, and Submit once every question has one; a request the view cannot answer with Open in terminal. A click posts `answer-request` with the answer, which the extension sends to `answer` with no dialog; the card is locked while it goes, and a refusal shows in the card and unlocks it. A taken request leaves with the hub's next state.
- **Waiting in the terminal:** while the selected conversation's session waits on the operator in the terminal (`waitingFor` `question`, such as an AskUserQuestion menu, `permission` or `start-prompt`) and has no Codex request the view answers itself, the turn's row at the end of the timeline says "Waiting for your answer", "Waiting for permission" or "Waiting in the terminal" in place of "Thinking…", with a still `question`, `shield` or `terminal` codicon in the warning colour, a status a screen reader reads with what the terminal asks, and an **Open in terminal** button that opens the session's attach terminal, as the header's does; it shows whether or not a reply is due, so a wait before the first message shows too. The view does not answer the menu (voice-design § 5.1).
- **Composer:** at the bottom, measured from Claude Code's input: at most 680 px wide and centred, a box in the input colours with an 8 px radius, a light shadow and a 3 px ring in the focus colour when focused, its text area growing with its text up to 200 px over a footer behind a hairline, whose left side has the `add` (Attach files…) and `/` (Show slash commands) buttons as 26 px ghost buttons, later slices' controls coming after them, and whose right side has a send button with the `arrow-up` codicon in the button colours, enabled only while the box has text the session can take; placeholder "Message <title>…", the selected conversation's title without leading tags such as `[Feedback]` and cut at a word after 40 characters. The model is not shown: the hub does not keep it from the transcript. Enter posts the text with the scope generation, session and launch the page showed, which the extension sends with `send` unchanged, so a message is refused rather than sent to a session the page did not show, posting it again once with the same `opId` when the hub does not answer; Shift+Enter adds a line. A draft stays in the box when the window moves to a newer session, and Enter then sends it to the session the page shows. The box is read-only until the hub answers, empties once it took the message (the answer's message is shown unless the stream already brought it), and on a refusal keeps the text and shows why above the box, with a `close` codicon that dismisses it; the next send that the hub takes clears it too. A message that ends `not-sent` comes back: the window that sent it, which hears it even after its scope moved to another session, puts its text in the box, kept by the extension until a loaded page takes it, since a hidden view's page is not kept, after any draft and a blank line, and shows why above the box in the same way; one that ends while another is being sent comes back once the hub answered that one. When the stream says a message was not sent before the hub answered its `send`, its text comes back in the same way once the hub answered. It is disabled, with the session's reason as placeholder, for a session that is not `full`, and hidden in a window with no conversation. The box keeps a draft per conversation: switching conversations keeps the box's text, any refusal shown above it and the command chosen for it with the conversation it belonged to and shows the newly selected one's; a message being sent shows read-only in its own conversation until the hub answers, and the text of a message that ends `not-sent` goes back only to its own conversation's draft, named by the message's `workItemId`, never into the composer of another conversation selected since. The extras (PRD R101), as Claude Code's panel has them, with the composer on every tab, are in `src/webview/composerExtras.ts`, with their decisions in `src/composerModel.ts`:
  - **File mentions.** `@` at the start of the box or after a space opens a list 8 px above the box at its width, rows of a codicon, the name and the folder dimmed and cut from its start: the page posts `files` with the text after the `@` (80 ms after the last key), and the extension lists files under the session's folder, or the window's first folder before the session has one, with `vscode.workspace.findFiles` and a glob that holds the query in either case, and files under a folder that holds it, leaving out the patterns that are on in `files.exclude` and `search.exclude`, their braces expanded, and the folders Claude Code's panel leaves out (`node_modules`, `.git`, `dist`, `build`, logs, `.env` files), at most 500, then adds the folders above them and keeps 50, a name that starts with the query first.
