The hub ends a console, delivering nothing more, when its page closes, its scope generation changes (another conversation or session, even one shown again), or the session runs under another launch (checked before each event and every second, `CONSOLE_CHECK_MS`), when its stream has more than 8 MiB queued (`CONSOLE_QUEUE_BYTES`), and on `hub.js web revoke`. `POST /v1/conversation/console-input {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId, launch, data, cols?, rows?}` takes only a page key and types `data` (at most 64 Ki characters) into that session with the checks of `prompt-key`: its scope and `scopeGen`, the launch the page showed (409 `SESSION_RELAUNCHED`), and the session's input reservation, checking the scope and launch again once it holds the reservation and after a resize, through holder `write` with `key`, so the keys count as keys typed in a terminal for the composer's 1.5 s pause; with `cols` and `rows` it first resizes the session to them through holder `size`, and `data` may then be empty. A repeated `opId` answers the first result, and one page's keys are typed in the order posted. Only this route resizes a session for the page, so a console that only watches never changes the size a terminal set. The page draws it on its **Console** tab, after Status and Artifacts, and from 1200 px as the third tab of the Status and Artifacts column beside the conversation (`packages/web/src/console.ts`): xterm.js and its styles are in their own files, `console.js` and `xterm.css`, which the page loads the first time a console shows, so the page itself never loads them. The console is mounted once per page key, scope generation, session and launch, and disposed, with its stream, when another tab shows or the session changes; a Codex session or one the page cannot talk to shows why it has no console, without loading it. It draws the session at the size the program draws (`size`), never re-wrapping: the font scales from 13 px down to 8 px to fit the columns, and past that the screen scrolls sideways. A bar above it shows the size and the state, "Watching. Click to type." or "Typing: keys go to the session. Click outside to stop.", with the focus border round the console while it types. Clicking the console focuses it and makes it type: it posts `console-input` with no data and its own size (the cells that fill its pane at 13 px), so the program redraws for it, then each key, Esc, Ctrl+C, arrows and Enter included, with that size, keys pressed while one is on its way going with the next, and its size again 200 ms after its pane resizes; blurring it makes it watch again. Ctrl+C with a selection copies it, and Ctrl+V pastes through the browser as one paste. Only data a key, a paste or an input method produced while it types is sent, so the terminal's answers to the program's queries never are, and the page's own shortcuts do not see its keys. Mouse and focus reporting stay off (mechanics M101): it drops the program's DECSET of modes 9, 1000 to 1006, 1015 and 1016 and any mouse report, so a pointer over it never types, and selecting text works. An exit, an `unavailable` or `closed` event, or a lost stream ends it with why and a Reconnect button, which opens the stream again and starts from a reset screen; it never reconnects by itself, which would replay the screen over itself. A paste longer than 64 Ki characters goes in order in pieces of that size; when the hub refuses a piece, the keys after it are dropped too and "Not typed: <why> <n> characters were not typed." shows under the console.
- **Row actions.** The page runs the dock's controls on its selected work item only (`pageActions` in `page.ts`): Start, Approve, Retry, Stop, Mark done, Snooze…, Unsnooze and Delete, those of the row's hub controls whose action is in `PAGE_ACTIONS` and that ask for no text, in the dock's menu order (the row's main control, its work, its state, then the controls that ask first), with the dock's labels and Delete's question; Stop asks "Stop <key>?" on the page too. `POST /v1/conversation/row-action {window, scopeGen, opId, workItemId, action, until?, comment?}` takes only a page key and the page's selected work item (403 otherwise, 409 for a stale `scopeGen`), looks up the work item's dock row as it is now, and posts the row's own control's action (`pageActionEvent`), Snooze with `until` and `comment` added, as the dock's `/action` does; 409 "The work item no longer offers this." when the row no longer offers it, and 409 with the flow's refusal. A repeated `opId` answers the first result (the last 100). `POST /v1/conversation/row-paste {window, scopeGen, opId, workItemId, label}` answers `{text}`, the drafted file Copy offers by that label as it is now, without a byte order mark or trailing whitespace, as the dock's Copy reads it (PRD R60); it only reads. The page draws the main control as a button in its header and every control in a menu under an ellipsis beside it, the work controls, the state controls, Copy and Delete separated; Stop and Delete open a sheet with the question, Snooze… a sheet with the dock's choices (`snoozeChoices`, computed when it opens), a date and a comment, and Copy writes the clipboard, or shows the text in a sheet when the browser refuses it. A refusal shows over the composer.
- **Files.** `GET /v1/conversation/files?window=<page key>&q=<text>` (cookie) answers `{files}`, the `@` picker's files and folders under the selected conversation's current folder whose path holds the text, at most 50 (`findFiles`): a name that starts with it first, never in `node_modules`, `.git`, `dist`, `build`, `bin`, `obj` or a dot folder, reading at most 20,000 entries; 403 for a page with no selection.
- **Attached files.** `POST /v1/conversation/upload?window=<page key>&scopeGen=<n>&name=<file name>` (cookie) takes one file's bytes as its body, from the page's own `Origin`, with a `Content-Length` (411 without one, so never chunked) of at most 10 MB (413, refused before the body is read), for a page key whose selected conversation's current session can take a message, at the `scopeGen` the page showed (403 or 409 otherwise, `pageUpload`). It is not a conversation command, so it is not in `PAGE_COMMANDS`, and the hub token reaches none. `writeUpload` (`packages/hub/src/uploads.ts`) writes the file to `<os temp>/switchboard-pasted/`, where the Conversation view writes pasted images: a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP picture, known by its first bytes rather than its name or type, as `pasted-<time>-<random>.<ext>`, as the view names one; any other file as `attached-<time>-<random>-<name>`, its name cut to its last path part in ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`, with no leading or trailing dot, at most 80 characters. It refuses an empty file, and one named `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif` or `.webp` that is not such a picture, since Claude reads a file with that extension as a picture (400); it never writes over a file, refuses the folder when it is a link or junction, and deletes only its own and the view's files there older than a week. It answers `{path, image}`. The composer's `add` button (Attach files…), left of `/`, opens the browser's file picker, which a phone fills from its camera and photos too; files pasted with no text beside them, as a copied screenshot, and files dragged onto the page from the computer are attached the same way, while a drop the composer cannot take is still kept from opening the file in the tab. Each file of at most 10 MB, at most 10 to a message, is posted at once and shows as a chip above the box with its name, spinning until the hub answers and then a file or picture icon, and a remove button that also ends its upload; a refusal drops the chip and shows why above the box. Send waits for every chip, and the message carries each file as a mention after the typed text, its path with forward slashes and quoted as the Conversation view writes it, so Claude reads a picture as one (mechanics M88); files alone make a message. Chips belong to their work item, kept in local storage (`switchboard.attachments`) over a reload, and leave once a message that names them is taken; a message refused before the hub answered keeps them, and gives back only its typed text.
- **Focus.** The page posts `page-focus {window, focused}` when it gains or loses the focus or is shown or hidden, `focused` being `document.hasFocus()` on a visible page; a focused page is the focused window the voice follows ([Voice](#voice)), so its selected work item's current session is a voice target as a window's is. A VS Code window's focus replaces it; the blur VS Code reports when the browser takes the focus does not clear it, nor does a page's own blur clear another window's. Closing the page ends its stream and its focus.
- **The page** (`packages/web/src`), phone first: under 768 px wide the list and the conversation are each a full-width view, the list until a work item is opened or after the Back button at the start of the conversation's header ("Back to work items", an arrow in the work items' toggle's place, which puts the focus on the selected row), every switch by the page's width alone, so a desktop window narrowed to a phone's width gets the phone's layout, the toggle and the sashes hidden, and every list control is at least 44 px tall with nothing shown only on hover; from 768 px the list is the side column, 260 to 340 px wide by default, and Back is gone; a sash on its right edge (`.side-sash`, a `separator`, `resizer` in `main.ts` as for the conversation's sash) resizes it from 200 to 600 px and at most half the page: dragged, or with Left and Right (16 px, 64 px with Shift, from the width drawn), Home and End while it has the focus, shown in the sash colour while hovered, dragged or focused; the width is kept in local storage (`switchboard.sideWidth`) and set before the list is first drawn, and a double-click returns to the default; from 1200 px (`WIDE_QUERY`) Status and Artifacts leave the tab strip for a column of their own right of the conversation, whose pane then keeps the conversation's width, 940 px by default, while the column, at least 320 px, takes the rest of the width, so a wider window widens only the column; a sash between them (`.chat-sash`) sets the conversation's width, 480 to 1600 px, kept in local storage (`switchboard.chatWidth`), and with the column hidden the timeline and composer keep that width, centred; topped by a Status and Artifacts switch drawn as the tabs, where the status, the artifact list and an open artifact show, an artifact row in the conversation opening there, while the conversation and its composer keep the middle pane and unread items in either pane are marked seen; narrower, the tabs stay; the header's first button (`layout-sidebar-left`, Ctrl+B) hides or shows the work items from 768 px, and its last (`layout-sidebar-right`, Ctrl+Alt+B) the Status and Artifacts column from 1200 px, which with that column hidden are tabs again, each choice kept in local storage (`switchboard.layout`); a phone keeps the list and the conversation as separate views; and with a fine pointer the group headers are 32 px tall as the dock's; a view options button in the header (`codicon-settings`) opens a menu with the text size (A−, the size and A+, and Reset) and Show message details (off by default, kept in local storage as `switchboard.messageDetails` and read before the first draw), and Ctrl+= and Ctrl+-, Ctrl+0 to reset, do the same in place of the browser's zoom: 85, 90, 100, 110, 125 or 150% of the theme's size for the conversation's text only (the timeline and the composer's box, `--chat-scale`), the list, header, menus and Status and Artifacts keeping theirs, kept in local storage (`switchboard.textScale`) and set before the first draw; the vertical scroll areas (the list, the panes, an open Markdown artifact, menus, the sheet and the composer's box, `OVERLAY_SCROLL` in `src/scrollbars.ts`) hide their own scrollbars, and one thin thumb, fixed over the right edge of the area being scrolled or hovered, shows the place without taking width, fades out 800 ms after the last scroll unless the pointer stays over the area, and can be dragged, as the dock's overlay scrollbars do. The list is drawn as the dock's: each group a header bar with a chevron, its codicon, its title and its row count, which opens or collapses it, Done collapsed at first, a collapsed group still showing the selected row; each row the dock's three lines: the priority dot, the title and the time at its end, muted; the comment icon, the playbook with its namespace muted, or a hosted conversation's host, the ticket key as a pill in its source's colour, as the Switchboard pane draws it, the link pills (a pull request purple with its icon and number, a link the ticket's text names grey) and Returned in the warning colour, each pill's name and URL in its tooltip, the pills shrinking to their icons as the dock's do (`fitPills` in `src/fit.ts`): first those wider than 56 px, longest first, while the playbook keeps its width, then, with the playbook down to 60 px, the rest longest first and Returned last, and, once the playbook has given way entirely, those that do not fit even as icons left out whole, the last first and Returned only when it alone does not fit, a shrunk ticket key showing GitHub's mark or a tag and every pill keeping its tooltip; the page fits every row in one pass a frame after a redraw of the list or a change of its width (`ResizeObserver`), reading every width before it changes a class; and, when the row has a mark or details, its mark (the codicon `MARK_ICONS` names for it, spinning for `SPINNING_MARKS`, a failed mark red) and the details, muted or in their warning or error colour, a muted `·` between them, those before the last keeping their width and the last cut with an ellipsis; the pills are text, not links, since the row is one button; the dock's stripe on its left edge, a dimmed row dimmed, and the selected row in the selection colour with a full-height stripe, as the dock's, the focus ring showing only on a keyboard focus; no unread counts; on the right the selected work item's header (the dropdown of its group, its button holding the state codicon, its provider's mark, its persona with the roster to pin from, a plain persona button for a session with none that can take a pin (`canPin`)), the Conversation, Status and Artifacts tabs, and the composer, drawn as the Conversation view is: messages as bubbles, replies whole as Markdown with raw HTML off, web links opening a new tab and paths shown as text, focused mode's Show more, rewrites, notes, times (on the first item after each day's divider, every item's with Show message details, its time otherwise the tooltip of its meta line or bubble; `metaLine` and the marks in `packages/conversation/src/messageMeta.ts`, shared with the Conversation view), the optional marks with Show message details only (a dictated message's mic mark, with its pieces and voice checks in the tooltip, from the message's `pieces`, `partial` and `interrupted`, and a reply's tone), the copy button and error marks always, the turn row ("Starting…", "Thinking…", or "Waiting for your answer" or "Waiting for permission" in the warning colour while the session waits on the operator in its terminal), unread dots, question cards that answer with an ordinary message such as "1A, 2B", Answered or Skipped and with their questions left out of the text over them as in the Conversation view, earlier messages, and the status with its New mark. The composer sends to the session it shows with its `scopeGen` and `launch`, keeps a draft per work item in local storage, lists files after `@` and the session's slash commands after `/`, walks earlier messages with Up and Down, and takes back the text of a message it sent that was not sent, into that work item's draft even when the page shows another, or into the box when the refusal comes before the send's answer; a card's refused answer unlocks its card. A Codex session's pending questions and approvals show under the timeline and are answered with `answer` (decisions as buttons, questions with their options and a typed or hidden field); one the page cannot answer says to answer it in the terminal. A refused select while no conversation is shown says why over the list, which a phone shows again, until the next select. Answers to a select, history page or picker that arrive after the page moved on are dropped, and the latest work item clicked is the one selected. The composer stays at the bottom on every tab, and a message sent from the Status or Artifacts tab leaves that tab shown with a "Sent" line for 4 seconds; an ended session's note, "The session has ended. Resume continues it.", shows just above the composer with **Resume**, as the Conversation view's, and the disabled composer says "Resume the session to send a message."; a session the page cannot talk to at all shows only its reason. Resume posts `POST /v1/conversation/resume {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId}`, which takes only a page key and its selected conversation's current session once it has ended (403 otherwise, 409 for a stale `scopeGen` or "The session has not ended."), and resumes it without a terminal: a Code Mode session through `Runtime.attach`, the attach terminal's own path, with its reservation, its wait for an end in progress, and its refusals for leftover processes and hooks off, the reservation lapsing as for an attach no terminal took; a Codex app-server session through `resumeThread`, as a message does; any other with 409. Its footer has the Conversation view's own voice controls, drawn by the one renderer both use (`renderVoiceControls` and `renderMicButton` in `packages/conversation/src/voiceControls.ts`), from the page's own `VoiceView` only: the mic button in each of its states (off, idle-off, starting, listening, hearing, paused while typing, paused by another app, paused by voice, held for a reply in half duplex, unavailable), Speak replies, Stop, and Talk here ("The voice is not on this page.") while the voice is elsewhere. They control this computer's one voice process as a window's do: the mic posts `mic`, and the box posts `composer {focused, draft}` on focus, blur and 300 ms after typing (`COMPOSER_DRAFT_MS`) for the focus rule, and gives the focus back after a send while the mic is on. The microphone and speaker are the computer's, not the browser's. When dictation goes to the page, or to a window on the same session, live words show dimmed right after its box's text, as in the Conversation view, and a `draft` for its session fills the box while the box is empty or still holds the hub's previous draft for that session, never over text typed after it; one that comes while a message is being sent fills the box once the send answered. Live words clear when the page shows another conversation. Unread replies and the status are marked seen once visible for a second on a focused page. The page draws each state by changing only the nodes that differ (its own `morph`), so the focus, scroll positions and open menus stay, and its conversation follows its end as the Conversation view does, until the operator scrolls away, and comes back where the operator left it after another tab. Open in terminal is not on the page.
- **Phones first.** The conversation pane works alone at a phone's width (375 px): under 600 px its scroller and composer keep 12 px of padding and bubbles take 92%; on a touch screen (`hover: none` or `pointer: coarse`) every button is at least 44 px and the copy buttons always show, a metadata line's copy button taking taps on a 44 px area round its glyph.

## Session reports

### Handled assignments (R85)

`report handled <sessionId> <ticket URL> [--release <assignment key>]` posts to `/handled`. It is available in every session contract when a skill directs it. The response contains `assignmentKey`; the skill retains it for an optional conditional release. Registration requires an open target assigned to the operator and the current session of an open work item. It cannot target the reporting work item's own ticket.

`tickets.handledAssignmentKey` is nullable text, the only added storage. Existing events and changes retain target-ticket registration and clearing evidence, with reporting session/work-item IDs in event detail. Deleting the reporting work item preserves this evidence. After every accepted event, `endUnownedHandling` clears each key whose registration (`Store.handledRegistrations`) names a reporting work item that is done or deleted, or names none, notes `handledEnded` (ticket IDs) on that event for History, and creates a work item for a still-assigned ticket. Core refuses ticket identity adoption of an active handled target; consolidation of an inactive target transfers its audit. Ended-key audit prevents a delayed registration retry from restoring handling.

`Source.assignmentAt(url)` returns the ticket, its opaque operator assignment key and fresh queue eligibility, or a read error. GitHub reads assignees and paginated assignment events directly by issue number; the key combines the issue node ID and the event that began the current continuous assignment across configured operator logins. Event IDs distinguish changes within one second. It also reads the configured project's current status to correct stale search eligibility. It never infers assignment changes from status history. Incomplete history, changed pagination snapshots and failed reads are errors.

The runtime serializes registration and polling per source. Every poll directly reads registered targets, including those absent from project search, and applies assignment reconciliation with the poll atomically. Core guards work creation, triage and session dispatch. Registration refuses a target whose open work already has a session, including an emitted start; otherwise it removes unstarted duplicates before acknowledgement. A new assignment clears the key and permits normal work even when consecutive polls both show actionable assignment. Explicit release compares the supplied key and uses freshly read eligibility before scheduling work.


### Report transport

Sessions report through the hub bundle, which posts to the hub's loopback HTTP server. The prompt carries the Switchboard session ID and the bundle's absolute path, because a background session has no Switchboard environment and `switchboard` is not on PATH. `node` below is `sessions.nodePath`; a session started before a change keeps the command its prompt holds. Bash, PowerShell and cmd run the command, and they agree only on a first word with no spaces, quotes or shell characters, so `parseSettings` refuses a `sessions.nodePath` with any of them or whose name is not `node` or `node.exe`, and turns its backslashes into forward slashes.

```
node "<home>/hub.js" report stage <sessionId> <stage>
node "<home>/hub.js" report worktree <sessionId> <path>
node "<home>/hub.js" report done <sessionId>
node "<home>/hub.js" report approval <sessionId> --prompt <prompt> [--summary <text>] [--report <path>]
node "<home>/hub.js" report link <sessionId> --label <text> --target <url>
node "<home>/hub.js" report playbook <sessionId> <name>
node "<home>/hub.js" report paste <sessionId> --label <text> --file <absolute path>
node "<home>/hub.js" report task <sessionId> --prompt <prompt> [--repo <repo root>] [--title <text>]
node "<home>/hub.js" report question <sessionId> --text <question>
node "<home>/hub.js" register --title <text>
```

- The contract asks for the approval summary; the CLI and the hub accept its omission, and `done` takes no text. The CLI resolves a relative `--report` against its working directory, and the hub refuses a report path that is not absolute.
- Git Bash rewrites an argument that starts with `/` to a path under its Windows root, so `/sb-ship 16416` arrives as `C:/Program Files/Git/sb-ship 16416`. When `MSYSTEM` is set, the CLI reads the root from `cygpath -m /` and restores an approval prompt that starts with it (mechanics M20).
- The CLI's usage text, printed for a malformed report, leaves out `task`, `register` and `ticket`, as the contract does (PRD R68, R81, R83).
- `report ticket <sessionId> <url> [--playbook <name>]` (PRD R83) posts `{sessionId, url, playbook, nativeSessionId}` to `/ticket`, outside `/report` and `REPORT_KINDS`, so no session's stored `reports` or contract text changes. `Runtime.ticket` refuses a retired copy before and after the lookup, as `report` does; asks each enabled source's `ticketAt` in turn, behind `REPORT_CHECK_MS`, and refuses when none answers for the URL, the lookup fails or is late, or the ticket is closed, because the move needs the ticket's title and state, and when the source was turned off or reconfigured during the lookup; then sends the `ticket` event. History shows a refused or repeated report, whose event notes no `adhocKey`, as a request. On success the CLI prints `Moved to <key>.`, the instruction to follow the contract that follows in place of any earlier one, a blank line and the session's contract, which `/contract` also restores after compaction; a retry prints them again. Its exit codes are a report's.
- `register` (PRD R81) registers the Claude session that runs it. It reads the native session ID from `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and exits 1 without it, posts `{nativeSessionId, title}` to `/register`, and waits up to 30 seconds, longer than the hub's 15-second roster read, and on success prints `Registered as <key>.`, or `Already registered as <key>.`, a blank line and the session's report contract. Its exit codes are a report's. `Runtime.register` answers the key and contract of a session whose current native ID it is, with `already: true`, so a retry after a lost reply recovers the contract; refuses a native ID in a Claude session's `retiredIds` or one the runner's `relation` finds `newer` or `older`, as a copy of that session; then asks the Claude runner's `live`, which reads `claude agents --json` and returns the entry with that full session ID and a pid: its short ID, else the session ID's first 8 characters, which `entryFor` matches as a prefix on later roster reads, and its `cwd`; no such entry refuses the registration. It then sends the `register` event and answers the work item's key and the contract, which `/contract` also restores after compaction, because the session's native ID is now a session's.
- The CLI's home is the directory that holds the `hub.js` it runs from, so a session started by a hub with `--home` reports to that home.
- The CLI finds the hub through `hub.json` and never starts one. Timeout: 10 seconds for the `/status` check and again for the request; 30 seconds each for `register`. Repeating a report is safe, because each report sets a value and a repeated `task` adds nothing.
- When no hub answers, the CLI exits 2 and prints that the hub is not running, or, when the process `hub.json` names still runs, that the hub with that pid did not answer. The hub stays up while sessions are active, so this is rare; the work item then ends `blocked`, and the operator marks it done.
- Exit codes: 0 recorded, 2 no hub, 1 usage, 3 refused (an unknown session, a report the session's report contract does not offer, a link its ticket's source refuses, or a path that `git worktree list` run in that path does not show as a worktree). A worktree can belong to a different repository than the source's repo root, as when a Support session in `D:\git\support` reports an widgets worktree.
- Before applying a task session's `done`, the hub polls the ticket's source and applies that poll. When the poll still lists the ticket as assigned, the hub sends the report with `assigned: true`: the flow sets the session's outcome to `done` with an `outcomeDetail` that starts with `UNCONFIRMED_DONE` and names the report's time, and leaves the work item's status. The hub then polls the source again 30 seconds later (`RECHECK_MS`), after any read in flight, because GitHub issue search can list an issue as assigned for seconds after its unassignment (mechanics M34), and sends `done-checked` with that `outcomeDetail` as its token: still assigned blocks the work item with "The session reported done, but the ticket is still assigned to you.", otherwise it is done; a failed read, one over 6 seconds or a source turned off accepts the report. `done-checked` changes nothing unless the session still has that token and the work item is `running` or `awaiting-approval` (`awaitsRecheck`), so a resume into `running` or `waiting`, which clears the outcome, an approval report after the done report, which clears the token, a later done report, which replaces it, and Mark done each make the check change nothing. A stopped hub skips the recheck, and `startup` schedules one for each work item whose latest session `awaitsRecheck`, at most one per token. When a poll finds the ticket outside the assigned state, the flow marks such a work item, or a blocked one whose task session reported done, done even while that session is still active, such as idle in a tab. The CLI still exits 0. A failed poll, a poll that takes over 6 seconds (`REPORT_CHECK_MS`, inside the CLI's 10-second timeout), a setup session, or an ad-hoc ticket skips the check (PRD R10). Reads of one source run one at a time, so a scheduled poll that started earlier cannot apply after the check's poll; a scheduled poll is skipped while a read is in flight, and a queued read does not start after the hub stops or its source is turned off.
- A report for a work item that is already done does not reopen it: `done` sets the session's outcome, `worktree` links the ticket's worktree, `link` adds the ticket's link, and `stage`, `approval`, `playbook` and `paste` are ignored.
- `playbook` names a task playbook in the work item's namespace; the hub refuses any other with "<name> is not a task playbook in the <namespace> namespace." It sets the work item's playbook only, so the session keeps the reports its prompt offered.
- `task` (PRD R68) creates an ad-hoc ticket and an `unstarted` work item whose `parentId` is the reporting session's work item, whether or not that work item is done. The title is `--title`, or the prompt's first line when it is left out or blank. The repo is `--repo`, which the CLI resolves against its working directory, or the reporting ticket's root (`ticketRoot`, [Repo roots](#repo-roots)). The core compares it with `repoRoots` (the list the ad-hoc prompt box offers) ignoring case, separators and a trailing separator, stores the configured spelling, and otherwise refuses with the list of repo roots. The CLI restores a prompt that Git Bash rewrote, as for `approval`. A report whose prompt equals that of an open work item from the same parent adds nothing, so a repeated report is safe.
- `link` takes an http(s) URL; the CLI and the hub refuse any other target with exit 1. Migration 8 deleted the local-file links of earlier sessions, after copying an open approval's newest one to its `approvalReport`. A link to a target the ticket already has replaces its label.
- `paste` (PRD R60) takes a non-empty label and a `--file` that names one file wherever it is read: on Windows a drive or UNC path, not `\x` or `D:x`, which depend on the reader's current drive or directory; elsewhere a POSIX absolute path. The CLI and the hub refuse any other file with exit 1 instead of resolving it, because the extension reads the file later, outside the session's working directory. Under Git Bash with path conversion off (`MSYS_NO_PATHCONV`), the CLI turns a drive path such as `/d/git/support/x.md` into `D:/git/support/x.md`; with conversion on, Git Bash has already done so (mechanics M20). The hub stores it on the session's work item when the session is the latest, replacing a paste with the same label.

## Friction debriefs

PRD R55, ported from Ground Control's debrief runner (GC M64). Off unless `sessions.debriefs` is true.

- **Files:** under the Claude root, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `~/.claude`: the prompt `skills/friction-review/prompt.md`, and `.wip/debrief/state.json` and `.wip/debrief/log/YYYY-MM.jsonl`. They are the files Ground Control wrote, in the same format, so Ground Control's record of what it asked still holds, and a reader of the log finds both runners' lines. Ground Control's `codex` entries that wait for a debrief stay as they are. A session's entry has one writer, the hub that started the session.
- **Candidates:** every minute, the started sessions in the store with a native session ID, state `idle`, `failed` or `ended`, and no end more than an hour ago. The session's transcript, `<Claude root>/projects/<slug of cwd>/<nativeSessionId>.jsonl`, gives its last turn by its write time. A session is due as soon as its work item is done, otherwise 45 minutes after its last turn, and is skipped from 60 minutes after that turn. It also needs at least 10 main-transcript tool calls after the last debriefed message, in a range whose debrief has not failed. The due session with the oldest transcript goes first, one fork at a time.
- **Codex sessions:** a Codex session's own thread is a candidate on the same terms, read from its rollout, `<Codex home>/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*-<thread>.jsonl`, whose write time gives its last turn (`findRollout` in `packages/codex/src/debrief.ts`). `codexDebriefRange` counts the `function_call`, `custom_tool_call` and `local_shell_call` records of the turns after the last debriefed turn, through the last completed one, and names a later range by its first user message that Codex did not add, such as its AGENTS.md instructions. The state keeps the turn ID as `throughMessageUuid`. Its live check is the session's state in the store, which its run decides. The fork is `codex exec fork --json --ephemeral -c approval_policy="never" -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <thread> -` in the session's `cwd` with the prompt on stdin, `CODEX_HOME` and `FRICTION_DEBRIEF=1`, and a 10-minute timeout; `{{subagents}}` is `none`. Its answer is the fork's last agent message, read as a Claude fork's is. The log line has `provider: "codex"`, `parentSessionId: null`, the turn IDs as `fromMessageUuid` and `throughMessageUuid`, the tool calls, and no skills or cache. Codex threads a Claude session delegated to through agent-delegate are asked as before.
- **Roster:** before each Claude fork the hub reads the state and `claude agents --json`, skips a session listed with a pid that is not idle, and checks the setting and the 60-minute limit again. An idle background session and a stopped one can be forked (mechanics M19).
- **Fork:** `claude -p --resume <nativeSessionId> --fork-session --session-id <new uuid> --no-session-persistence --output-format json` in the session's `cwd`, the prompt on stdin, `FRICTION_DEBRIEF=1`, a 10-minute timeout. No model, effort, tool or MCP flag, because each changes the prompt prefix and loses the cache. `{{scope}}` is the whole conversation, or the work after the first message after the last debriefed one, named by its first 200 characters. `{{subagents}}` lists the subagents written within 55 minutes whose type can be resumed (not `codex`, `Explore` or `Plan`), with new messages since their last debrief; their transcripts are copied to `<project>/<forkId>/subagents/` for the run, because a fork looks for them under its own ID, and removed after it.
- **Codex threads:** a session's fork, whether it succeeded or failed, marks `pending` in the state each thread that an `mcp__agent-delegate__*` result names in the range, in the main transcript or a subagent's transcript written after the range starts, and every pending thread is then asked once, at the next scan when the hub stopped or a write was held, through agent-delegate's command: `<node> <Claude root>/tools/agent-delegate/debrief.mjs <thread> --prompt <prompt> [--since <its last debrief>]`, with the hub's own executable, `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` and `FRICTION_DEBRIEF=1`, and a 10-minute timeout. Exit 0 with the friction JSON appends a `codex` log line with the session as `parentSessionId`; exit 3, a thread with no turn since, records nothing new; anything else is reported in the hub log and the state's `lastError`, and not retried. A missing command skips the threads.
- **Answer:** the last `result` event whose text, without a code fence, is `{"friction": [...]}` or a bare array, each item with non-empty `what`, `source`, `workaround`, `cost` and `evidence`. The last `result` event gives the cache use and cost.
- **Record:** a success appends a log line (`v`, `at`, `provider`, `sessionId`, `parentSessionId`, `cwd`, `fromMessageUuid`, `throughMessageUuid`, `skills`, `toolCalls`, `cache`, `friction`, `startedAt`, `subagents`, `delegated`), on a fresh line when the file ends inside one, then merges into the state as it is on disk, so another writer's entries are kept: the last message covered, and the fork's start as each shown subagent's last debrief. A failure, including a fork that throws, records the range and its error in the state and the hub log, and is not retried; the next debrief covers that range with any later work. A write that fails is held, and no fork starts until a later scan writes it. A hub that stops between the two writes asks about that range again.

## Settings

`settings.json` in the config folder, plain JSON, holding the settings and the [playbooks](#playbooks). The hub validates it on load and on change (PRD R42); an error outside `playbooks` keeps the last valid settings and playbooks.

```json
{
  "dock": { "openVSCode": "always", "openTerminal": "vscode" },
  "sessions": { "provider": "claude", "concurrency": 2, "permissionMode": "auto", "model": "", "claudePath": "claude", "codexPath": "codex", "nodePath": "node", "codex": { "permissionMode": "bypassPermissions", "model": "" }, "installHooks": true, "debriefs": false, "codeMode": false },
  "triage": { "model": "claude-sonnet-5", "concurrency": 2, "timeoutSeconds": 180 },
  "sources": {
    "github": {
      "pollIntervalSeconds": 300,
      "repoRoot": "D:/git/widgets",
      "triagePlaybook": "triage:github",
      "namespace": "dev",
      "setupPrompt": "/init-worktree {{ticket.sourceKey}}",
      "ghPath": "gh",
      "repo": "acme/widgets",
      "logins": ["work-login", "work-ai-login"],
      "requireAssignee": true,
      "waitForBlockers": false,
      "includeClosed": false,
      "project": { "owner": "acme", "number": 3, "statusField": "Status" },
      "assignedStatuses": ["🎁 Assigned", "⚒️ Dev", "🔍 Review"],
      "branchPattern": "^(\\d+)-",
      "maxPages": 5,
      "rolesFile": "~/.claude/skills/sb-handoff/roster.json"
    },
    "zoho": {
      "pollIntervalSeconds": 300,
      "repoRoot": "D:/git/support",
      "triagePlaybook": "triage:zoho",
      "namespace": "support",
      "tokenCommand": "bash ~/.claude/scripts/desk-token.sh",
      "automationAgentIds": ["8000000000000000002", "8000000000000000003"],
      "orgId": "700000001",
      "agentId": "8000000000000000001",
      "escalationField": "cf_queue",
      "escalationValue": "Support"
    },
    "forum": {
      "pollIntervalSeconds": 300,
      "repoRoot": "D:/git/support",
      "triagePlaybook": "triage:forum",
      "namespace": "support",
      "queryCommand": "bash ~/.claude/scripts/run-forum-sql.sh",
      "forumId": 1,
      "userId": "7"
    }
  },
  "schedules": {
    "friction-review": { "playbook": "ops:friction-review", "repoRoot": "C:/Users/operator/.claude", "every": "week", "day": "fri", "at": "12:00" },
    "cleanup-worktrees": { "playbook": "ops:cleanup-worktrees", "repoRoot": "D:/git/widgets", "every": "week", "day": "mon", "at": "08:00" },
    "self-review": { "playbook": "ops:performance-review", "repoRoot": "D:/git/research", "every": "month", "day": 1, "at": "08:00" }
  },
  "web": { "port": 47616 },
  "voice": { "enabled": true, "voice": "marin", "style": "none", "speed": 1, "expressive": false, "expandReplies": false, "awayMinutes": 2, "needsYouSound": true, "outputDevice": null, "micIdleMinutes": 10, "endPauseMs": 3000, "inputDevice": null, "personas": [{ "id": "nova", "name": "Nova", "voice": "cedar", "style": "pair", "personality": "Warm and direct, with dry humour." }], "playbookPersonas": { "dev:question": "nova" } }
}
```

- `dock.openVSCode` (PRD R71): `always` (default) or `review`; `dock.openTerminal` (PRD R28): `vscode` (default), `app` or `auto`. `parseSettings` refuses any other value and any other `dock` key.
- `sessions.codeMode` (PRD R93), experimental: `true` or `false` (default); `parseSettings` refuses any other value. `Flow.startSession` reads it at each Claude session start ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)); a change affects only later starts. The Settings window shows it under General → Sessions as **Code Mode (experimental)**, a checkbox whose info text reads "New Claude sessions run with Claude Code's Code Mode, held by Switchboard's terminal holder, and open in a terminal instead of the Claude view. They keep running between turns until you stop them or they sit idle for an hour. They skip the gateway in your Claude settings and use your Claude subscription sign-in. Sessions already started keep their mode. Needs Install hooks." With `sessions.installHooks` off a Code Mode start fails ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). The Code Mode idle limit is fixed at 1 hour, since opening an ended session resumes it at once; `hub.js --code-mode-idle-ms` shortens it for tests and live acceptance ([Hub lifecycle](#hub-lifecycle)).
- `voice` (PRD R105, [Voice](#voice)): `enabled` (default `false`), the central voice setting; `voice`, the realtime voice (`marin`, default, `cedar`, `alloy`, `ash`, `ballad`, `coral`, `echo`, `sage`, `shimmer` or `verse`); `style`, the delivery (`none`, default, `pair`, `upbeat`, `deadpan` or `expressive`); `speed`, 0.25 to 1.5 (default 1); `expressive` (default `false`), which types `[expressive]` with `[spoken]` and reads a reply's tone line in place of the style; `expandReplies` (default `false`), which starts a reply with a passage whole instead of in focused mode; `awayMinutes`, 1 to 1440 (default 2); `needsYouSound` (default `true`); `outputDevice`, part of a playback device's name, or null (default) for the system default; `micIdleMinutes`, 0 to 1440 (default 10, 0 never), after which silence stops capture; `endPauseMs`, 500 to 20000 (default 3000), the end-of-message pause; `inputDevice`, part of a capture device's name, or null (default) for the system default; `halfDuplexTailMs`, 0 to 5000 (default 800), how long after the voice stops playing listening resumes on capture with no echo cancellation; `duplex`, `{local, remote}`, each `auto` (default), `full` or `half`, whether dictation listens while the voice plays on this computer and over Remote Desktop ([Dictation](#dictation)); `matchThreshold`, 0 to 1 (default 0.6), the voice check's least voiceprint match, `pitchLowFactor`, 1 to 3 (default 1.22), the multiple of the enrolled voice's p90 pitch at or below which a voiced frame counts as low, and `pitchMinLowShare`, 0 to 1 (default 0.2), the least share of low frames a piece needs, `roomHold` (default `true`), the room hold, and `roomHoldSeconds`, 1 to 3600 (default 60), its length after the latest other voice ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)); `personas` (default `[]`), the persona roster ([Personas](#personas)), each `{id, name, voice?, style?, personality?}` with a unique `id` of lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, a `name` of 1 to 40 characters, `voice` and `style` from the lists above, defaulting to the section's, and a `personality` of at most 600 characters, default empty; `playbookPersonas` (default `{}`), a persona `id` of the roster per playbook name; and for [spoken alerts](#spoken-alerts) `spokenAlerts` (default `true`), `alertOn`, `{finished, question, permission}`, each default `true`, which events alert, `alertAnswerSeconds`, 3 to 60 (default 10), how long the voice listens for an answer, and `operatorName` (default empty), at most 40 characters, the name an alert calls the operator by. `parseSettings` refuses any other value and any other `voice` or persona key. The hub sends the voice process the section on each reload, and each window `{expandReplies}`. The dock's Settings window edits the section on its Voice and persona pages ([Settings window](#settings-window)).
- `web.port` (PRD R109, [Web page](#web-page)): the port the hub serves the web page on, a whole number from 0 to 65535 (default 47616), read at hub start; 0 serves the page only on the hub's own port, which changes at each start. `parseSettings` refuses any other value and any other `web` key. The Settings window does not show it.
- `sources` (PRD R3): each key names a source. `parseSettings` requires the name to match `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$` and not be `schedule`, and reads `kind`, defaulting to the name; `sources.adhoc` takes no `kind`. The hub builds each source with the plugin its kind names (`makeSource` in `app.ts`, `SOURCE_KINDS` and `sourceOptionsError` in `config.ts`) and passes the name, which the source reports as `Source.name` and uses in its error messages, such as `sources.personal.repo`. A kind with no plugin is reported as `Switchboard has no source kind <kind>.` and disables that source. `sourcesErrors` in `config.ts` rejects the file whole, at load and in `checkSettings`, for a source of a kind other than `github` named apart from its kind, a source named after another kind, such as `zoho` with kind `github`, and two GitHub sources whose `project` names the same owner and number, compared without case, including a source that is off. Polls, tickets (`tickets.source`), toggles and errors are keyed by the name. The snapshot's `sourceStates` carry each source's `kind`.
- `schedules` (PRD R82): `parseSettings` requires each name to match `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$`, `playbook` and `repoRoot` to be set, `every` to be `day`, `week` or `month` or absent, `day` to be a weekday (`mon` to `sun`) for `week` and a whole number from 1 to 31 for `month` and absent otherwise, and `at` to be `HH:MM` from `00:00` to `23:59` with `every` and absent without it; it stores `at` as minutes after midnight. `checkSettings` also refuses a schedule whose playbook the file does not hold as a task playbook, or that needs a worktree, because a run has no source `setupPrompt`.

- The values shown are the operator's, taken from their Ground Control configuration. Defaults are empty for `repo`, `logins`, `project` and `assignedStatuses`. The GitHub source refuses to poll until `repo`, `logins` and `project` are set; an empty `assignedStatuses` puts an issue on the board in any status, or none, in scope, and an issue the query reads with no item for the board stays out of scope. `requireAssignee` defaults to `true` and `includeClosed` to `false`; `parseGithubConfig` refuses a value of either that is not a boolean.
- `playbooks` holds the playbooks ([Playbooks](#playbooks)). `parseSettings` requires it to be an object and leaves its entries to `parsePlaybooks`.
- A source implements core's `Source`: `poll(current)` returns a complete read or an error, given the source's tickets the store lists as assigned, in the pool or in the queue, `context(ticket)` returns the triage context or an error, and `keyOfBranch(branch, repo)` reads a ticket key from a worktree branch in `repo`'s clone. The optional `ticketAt(url)` reads the ticket a ticket report names, or answers null for a URL that is not one of the source's tickets (PRD R83): the GitHub source reads an issue URL in any repository and spelling of its owner and name with `ISSUE_QUERY` (number, title, URL, state and up to 100 project boards), answers null for an issue not on its board, fails when the boards do not fit one read, and names a number that is not an issue of the repository, such as a pull request's. `assignmentAt(url, anyBoard)` answers null for an issue off the board unless `anyBoard`, which the poll's reread of a registered ticket passes (PRD R85). The hub asks the enabled sources in order, the one whose stored ticket has the URL (`Store.ticketByUrl`, ignoring case) first (`Runtime.byOwner`), and refuses with `<url> belongs to <source>, which is off.` while that source is configured but off. A handled-assignment report reads through the owning source with `anyBoard`, so a registered issue that left the board can still be released. A source with a pool marks each polled ticket `assigned`, `pool` or neither.
- Pick (PRD R48): a picked Support case starts its playbook's task session; Switchboard does not claim the case.
- GitHub keys (`packages/github/src/keys.ts`, PRD R44): `keyOf(home, repo, number)` is the bare number in the home repository (`repo`, lowercase) and `owner/name#n` in lowercase elsewhere, `refOf` is `#n` or `owner/name#n`, and `parseKey` reads either back; every read of a stored ticket (`confirmLeft`, `readContext`, `checkLink`, assignment reads) goes to the key's repository. A GitHub pull request link is read in its own repository and accepted when a closing reference matches the ticket's repository and number, or, in the ticket's repository, when `keyOfBranch(branch, repo)` reads the ticket's key from its branch. `labels` maps `owner/name` to a display label of lowercase letters, digits and hyphens.
- One source per issue (PRD R44): `Runtime.read`, after its last await so an overlapping poll cannot store the issue in between, drops a polled ticket whose URL already has a ticket in another source and keeps `<url> belongs to <source>.` in the snapshot's `notes`, which the dock lists with the errors. `sourcesErrors` also refuses a label that equals a source name, `gh`, `adhoc` or `schedule`, or labels two repositories, and a `repo` other than the home repository recorded for the source (`Store.homeRepos`: the `sourceHomes` table of migration 39, else the repository of a bare-key ticket), at load (`loadConfig`), `/config/check` and `/config/save`. On each valid load the hub records each GitHub source's `repo` as its home in `sourceHomes` once it has tickets, and on its bare-key tickets that record none (`Store.fillHomeRepo`), so the home repository is fixed from then on, even when every stored key is qualified.
- Start gate (PRD R44): a triaged work item whose ticket's `repo` is not its source's home repository is left `unstarted` with `<repo> is outside the home repository <home>, so it waits for Start.` as its error, whatever the playbook's `autoStart`.
- Display keys (PRD R27): the snapshot's `repoKeys` holds each source's home repository and a prefix per other repository (`repoKeysOf`: its label, else its name when no other labelled or stored repository has that name). `shownKey` and `issueKey` in `rows.ts` build the row's `ticket`, the `From` line, search text and linked issues' labels from it; `ticketKey`, the stored key, still names windows, workspace files, terminals, sessions and History.
- Worktree linking (PRD R25): after each poll the hub lists worktrees in the root (`ticketRoot`) of the home repository and of each repository with an assigned, pool or registered ticket in the source (`Store.openRepos`) and reads each branch with that repository.
- GitHub reads go through `gh api graphql`. The issue query is GC's, scoped by the board instead of a repository: `is:issue is:open assignee:<login>… project:<owner>/<number>` (mechanics M59), without `is:open` while `includeClosed` is on, so the search returns closed issues too (mechanics M60); `readAssigned` and `readAssignment` then count a closed issue as assigned, and `readAssignment` and `ticketAt` report it as open, so ticket reports and handled registration accept it, with each issue's `repository { nameWithOwner }`, then a filter on project status; an issue on another status is listed as not assigned (mechanics M2). With `requireAssignee` false (PRD R44) the query has no `assignee:` qualifiers, so it returns every open issue on the project and `maxPages` counts them all; only issues on a configured status, or on any status or none while `assignedStatuses` is empty, are listed, so a poll creates no ticket for an issue never in scope, and the flow reads an assigned ticket missing from the list as having left the assigned state. Their scope entry is null (PRD R53). The same query reads the Status field's `updatedAt`, the current assignees and the last 100 assignment and unassignment events. The scope entry ports GC's `assignedSince`: undo the events newest first, matching logins case-insensitively, until none of the operator's logins is assigned; that assignment's time is the entry, or null when no login is assigned or the events start mid-assignment. The sort time is the later of the last assignment and the Status field's `updatedAt`; status-change events have been missing from the issue timeline since about 2026-09-25, so the field's `updatedAt` stands in for them. A poll that needs more than `maxPages` pages of 100 fails. Each ticket the store holds as assigned that the search leaves out or lists outside scope is then read on its own with the handled-assignment query (`readAssigned` in `packages/github/src/assignment.ts`), because issue search can return a successful but incomplete result during a GitHub outage (mechanics M48). An issue that read finds in the assigned state is listed as assigned with its stored title and sort time; a missing issue is not assigned; a failed read fails the poll. Triage context ports GC's card context: the board status from the Status field, the issue body, the last 5 comments, the last 100 assignment and status events plus a `(no timeline event)` line at the field's `updatedAt` when that is more than a minute after the last status event or no status event exists, and a linked pull request's author, review requests, state, checks, last 5 comments, reviews and last 5 review threads, with each text clipped to 2,000 characters keeping the start and end. On somebody else's pull request, a line `Your submitted reviews and pull request comments:` gives `none`, or the count and latest UTC time of each, of the last 100 submitted reviews and last 100 pull request comments by the operator's logins, matched case-insensitively; pending reviews, bots and other people do not count. When neither list holds one and either list's `totalCount` exceeds 100, the line says `none in the last … of … reviews and last … of … comments; older ones were not read`. The count lets triage choose between `dev:review-pr` and `dev:review-pr-followup` without reading the lists (mechanics M58). A bot's GraphQL login has no `[bot]` suffix, so the query reads each author's and actor's `__typename`, and a bot shows as `<login> (bot)` in the history, the pull request's author and the reviews. Each comment heading is `### <login>, <UTC time> [<anchor id>]`, from each comment's `url`; when `rolesFile` names a JSON file of `{login: {roles: [...]}}`, read on each context read, a listed author's roles follow the login, as `### QaTester (tester), 2026-10-06 14:02 [c3]`, matching logins case-insensitively, after `bot` for a bot, as `### claude (bot, dev), …`. A roles file that cannot be read or parsed leaves the headings without roles, and the hub logs the context's warning; triage continues. The context read also returns that pull request as a link, which a successful triage adds to the ticket's links, keeping earlier ones (PRD R52).
- With `waitForBlockers` (default false; `parseGithubConfig` refuses a value that is not a boolean) the source keeps each issue's open blockers; with it off every listed issue gets `blockers: []`, which clears any stored ones (PRD R90). The issue query always reads each issue's `blockedBy(first:50)` with each blocker's number, title, URL, state and repository, and the source keeps the `OPEN` ones as `PolledTicket.blockers`, because GitHub also lists closed blockers (mechanics M56, PRD R90). An issue with more than 50 blockers loses the rest.
- GitHub errors: offline, timeout and rate limits are temporary. The poll changes nothing and runs again at the next interval. Missing `gh` or failed authentication shows in the dock. Before the issue search, each poll reads the configured project's `field(name: <statusField>)` once. A missing field, or one that is not `ProjectV2SingleSelectField`, fails the poll with a message naming the field, because `fieldValueByName` returns null for every issue and the poll would end their work items (mechanics M17). gh's own message is replaced only when it is the sole error.
- Zoho Desk and Forum port the intake of the operator's former Support board (`extensions/support-kanban/lib/zoho.js` and `forum.js` in the `D:\git\support` history before its deletion on 2026-10-06).
- Zoho reads go through the Desk REST API at `https://desk.zoho.com/api/v1`. They use the access token that `tokenCommand` prints, cached for 50 minutes and fetched again on a 401, plus the `orgId` header. Switchboard reads no secrets file; the operator's runner scripts do. Keep 19-digit IDs as strings.
  - Each poll reads the Open tickets with the escalation value through `GET /tickets/search?status=Open&customField1=<escalationField>:<escalationValue>&sortBy=createdTime`, paged by 100 up to `maxPages`, because only `/tickets/search` returns custom fields (mechanics M46). Creation order does not keep pages stable when a ticket leaves during a multi-page read. Each poll gets the source's current tickets from the store (assigned, in the pool or in the queue); one this search omits is read with `GET /tickets/{id}` and kept while its status is `Open`, its escalation field holds the value and it is not in the trash (`isTrashed` or `isDeleted`, mechanics M10); a 404 means it is gone, and any other failed read fails the poll. A ticket missing from a complete poll has left the assigned state.
  - Assignee classes follow the board's `classifyAssignee`, with the team map from `GET /teams` cached for 6 hours. The operator is `mine`; a missing assignee is `none`; an automation or a team member is `support`; anyone else is `engineer`. `mine` is the assigned state; `none` and `support` are the pool; `engineer` is neither. A poll whose team map cannot be read is incomplete and changes nothing.
  - Scope entry (PRD R53): `GET /tickets/{id}/history?from=<n>&limit=100` for each assigned or pool Support ticket, newest first and `from` counting from 1, takes the latest event that changes `Escalation` to the escalation value or `Status` to the ticket's current status. A history that ends without one gives `createdTime`; 10 full pages without one give null, so core keeps the time it has. The result is kept per ticket until the search's `modifiedTime` changes. A failed history read fails the poll.
  - Triage context: the subject, the custom fields `cf_queue`, `cf_user_id`, `cf_account_id` and `cf_page_url`, the thread bodies, the comments (internal notes), and the history of tier and assignee changes.
- Forum reads run read-only SQL on the production forum database, through `queryCommand`, the operator's runner script, which holds the connection. The SQL goes to its stdin and rows come back one JSON object per line (`--result-format=json/raw`). Runner exit code 7 means the database is unreachable, usually the VPN, and the poll fails without changing anything. A line that looks like a row but does not parse, or `maxRows` (500) rows, makes the poll incomplete.
  - A topic assigned to anyone else is retained for Support volume, without a work item or pool eligibility. The pool sorts by the last public post (`Thread.LastPublicEntryId`), else the topic's creation; DATETIME values are UTC.
  - Assigned state: `Thread.CommunityId = <forumId> AND DeletedUtc IS NULL AND IsLocked = 0 AND Status = 0` (`ThreadStatus.Open`), with `AssigneeId` equal to `userId` for the assigned state, or null for the pool.
  - Scope entry (PRD R53): a second query reads the change-history store, `auditDatabase` (default `audit`), on the same connection: the latest `AuditScope.CreatedUtc` of a `AuditEntity` row for the topic (`EntityType = 1`) whose `FieldNames` includes `Status`. The topic is Open now, so its latest status change is when it became Open. The insert row lists every field, so it covers a topic whose status never changed; without a row, `Thread.CreatedUtc`. The store keeps field names but not values on the database (mechanics M15). A failed read fails the poll.
  - Triage context: the topic title and posts in order. Deleted posts (`DeletedUtc` set), posts with `Status = 2` (private notes) and posts by `UserId = 2` (the automatic AI analysis) are left out. The source key is `Thread.Id`, and the URL is `https://forum.example.com/t/forum/<Thread.Key>`.
- `tokenCommand` and `queryCommand` run through `bash -c`. On Windows the hub uses Git Bash, from `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` or found beside `git.exe` on PATH, because `bash` on a VS Code-started hub's PATH is WSL's `System32\bash.exe`, which lacks the runner scripts' tools (`jq`, `mysqlsh`).
- A Forum topic and a Zoho ticket about the same case are separate tickets (PRD R26).
- Existing issues (PRD R61): the Zoho and Forum context reads pass the context's header lines and each unclipped HTML body to core's `githubLinks`, which returns each distinct `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/(issues|pull)/<n>` URL, in order of first appearance, as a link labelled `Issue #n` or `Pull request #n`. Reading the HTML before clipping finds an `href` whose link text is not the URL, and a URL in a clipped-out part, and avoids a clip that cuts the number short. A URL is cut to the issue or pull request, without a comment anchor or tab, with owner and repository lowercased. A successful triage adds the links, as it does the GitHub source's pull request; the triage context's text is unchanged.

The operator's playbooks live in their settings.json, not in this repository. Each runs one skill, such as `dev:develop` with `/sb-plan {{ticket.sourceKey}}`, or `support:defect` with `/support-process #<ticket number>`, `requiresWorktree: false` and `workdir: repoRoot`. Scheduled playbooks, such as `ops:friction-review`, are in the `ops` namespace, which no source triages into.

## Support volume (R84)

Core stores `Ticket.inQueue` and `Ticket.assigneeId` in the existing SQLite database. Zoho returns every fetched Open ticket with the configured escalation, and Forum every fetched eligible open topic; assigned/pool classification still decides execution and dock visibility. Other engineers' rows return `enteredAt: null` and skip the existing history/audit reads. A departure scan queries only currently queued, assigned or pooled tickets. Ticket field changes remain ordinary ticket-owned `events`/`changes`; they have no work-item deletion dependency.

Migration 29 adds the ticket fields, an index for current-queue lookups, and `sourceStates.volumeStartedAt`, `volumeLastAt`, `volumeInterval` and `volumeTimezone` (America/New_York). A successful Support poll updates these in the flow transaction. A gap over three previous polling intervals adds a source-level `volume-gap` event. Removed sources with volume state are disabled rather than deleted. A confirmed task-session start adds `volume-worked`, with the session ID in detail and no session/work-item foreign dependency. The bare-ticket consolidation path transfers history and current queue fields before deleting the redundant identity.

Migration 30 adds `ticketClassifications`, one latest successful playbook, immediacy, severity and observation time per Support ticket, seeded from surviving successful triages. Successful triage updates the classification in the same transaction. Work-item and triage deletion leave it intact; identity consolidation transfers it. The scheduler uses the existing ticket-only triage path after work items and pool tickets, including observed tickets that have since left the queue. It skips tickets with an existing classification, ticket-only attempt, open work item or disabled/removed source. Support ticket-only attempts survive pool departure, keeping running attempts in the concurrency count and failed attempts from automatic retries. A work item waits for any ticket-only triage already running on its ticket.

`packages/core/src/volume.ts` replays ticket changes into weekly distinct-ticket counts and detail lists. It calculates calendar boundaries in the stored timezone, including DST. It uses recorded observation times and never `scopeAt`; the initial baseline is excluded from arrivals. `/volume` is an authenticated POST handled in `packages/hub/src/volume.ts`: `{source?: 'all'|'zoho'|'forum'}` returns 12 weekly rows, timezone and source freshness; adding `{week, metric, offset?}` returns up to 100 detail rows and the total. Each detail includes `classification`, the latest successful ticket classification with its timestamp, or null; weekly counts are unchanged. Invalid filters and weeks outside the current 12-week range are refused. History is read on demand, not sent on each dock stream update.

The dock's Avalonia `VolumeWindow` opens from Ticket volume report in the More Actions menu, which lists it only while the dock view has a `zoho` or `forum` source, enabled or not. It requests aggregate counts, refreshes on demand or when the Source filter (All sources, Zoho, Forum) changes, and requests ticket pages on a count click. Its heading is Active queue by week; the week columns share the window's width, at least 125 pixels each, and a week with `unavailable` coverage shows a muted `—` in place of counts, which open nothing. Source links use the dock's existing URL action. The window explains observed coverage, last-observed boundaries, deferrals and the distinction between queue exits and resolution. No source write or agent session is involved.

The current ticket-report source lookup supports GitHub only; retaining volume evidence during identity consolidation does not add Support ticket lookup. Detail times use the reply's reporting timezone, or explicitly labeled UTC when the OS cannot resolve it. Boundary-state rows are labeled as state rather than a source observation timestamp.
